# BiblioViva — Implementation Plan

> **Status:** the plan in force; its history is in git.
> **Audience:** an LLM coding agent that will build the software, plus the project owner who reviews decisions.
> **Language:** this document and all code/docs are in English; the product UI is in Italian.
> **Open questions:** collected in §11, numbered `Q1…`; a question is ticked once the owner has answered it. New doubts get a new number there.

BiblioViva is a lean, self-hosted library management system for a **school library** (up to ~10 000 books, ~1 000 patrons). It is inspired by Koha's bibliographic-record/item split and circulation model, but radically simplified. It is a **staff-only** tool (no public catalogue, no patron accounts). It runs as a single container (linux/amd64 and linux/arm64), is reachable over Tailscale with HTTPS (needed for phone cameras), and is built with Bun, TypeScript, Svelte 5 (runes only), SvelteKit 3 (remote functions), Tailwind CSS 4, Skeleton v5, and SVAR DataGrid, on top of Bun's built-in SQLite. The UI is available in Italian and English.

---

## 0. How to use this plan (rules for the executing agent)

1. **Read §1–§4 before writing code.** §2 pins versions, §3 fixes the architecture, §4 fixes the data model. Do not deviate silently.
2. **Execute phases in order (§5)**, with these exceptions: Phase 11 (configuration from the admin UI) is required for the 0.9.0 release and is carried out **after Phase 8 and before Phase 9**, so the hardening, documentation and release of Phase 9 cover it (owner decision); Phase 12 (configurable barcodes and school structure) is also required for 0.9.0 and runs **after Phase 11 and before the remaining steps of Phase 9** (Q98); Phase 13 (photo census from class pictures) follows Phase 12 on the same terms (Q103). The numbers never change, even when a step runs out of its place: commits and `docs/STATUS.md` cite them (ADR-030). Inside a phase, steps are ordered by dependency. A step may start when everything in its *Depends on* list is done.
3. **A step is done only when** all its acceptance criteria hold, the tests required by §7 exist (unit tests for every service or pure module touched, a Playwright spec for every user-facing flow touched) and `bun run check`, `bun run lint`, `bun run test`, `bun run test:e2e` pass. Then tick the step's checkbox in this file and in `docs/TODO.md` (the short checklist of what is left, kept in step with this plan), append a line to `docs/STATUS.md`, and commit with `phase X.Y: <summary>` (one commit per step, smaller is fine).
4. **Never take product decisions on your own.** If a step touches an open question in §11 that is still unresolved, apply the question's *default*, and record it in `docs/DECISIONS.md` as an ADR (context / decision / consequences).
5. **Use only dependencies listed in §2.** Adding another one requires an ADR entry. The "avoid" list in §2.8 is binding. **Every dependency in `package.json` is pinned to an exact version** (no `^`, `~` or ranges), `bun.lock` is committed, and upgrades are deliberate, separate commits: the goal is full reproducibility.
6. **Remote functions are experimental** (SvelteKit 3 is stable, but remote functions are still marked experimental). When an API in this plan does not match the installed version, the official docs win (https://svelte.dev/docs/kit). Record every discrepancy in `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
7. **Languages:** code, comments, commit messages and docs in English. UI strings in Italian **and** English from day one through sveltekit-i18n JSON dictionaries (`src/lib/i18n/{it,en}/*.json`), both complete for every screen (§3.16). No hardcoded UI text in components.
8. **Privacy:** patrons are mostly minors. Never log names, photos or card numbers. Never commit real data. Seed data is synthetic.
9. **Lean by default:** server-side rendering first; client-side JS only where interaction needs it; heavy libraries (barcode scanner WASM, OpenCV) are lazy-loaded only on the pages that use them (§3.14 budgets).
10. **Version drift:** before starting each phase, check for newer *patch* releases of the pinned packages (especially `drizzle-orm@rc` and `drizzle-kit@rc`) and update the pins; never jump a major version without an ADR.
11. Items marked **[VERIFY]** are facts the plan could not confirm; check them when you reach that step and record the outcome.
12. **UX and visual design:** follow §3.18 on every screen. Light theme only (no dark mode anywhere), colourful and icon-rich, one design system; every new screen reuses the documented layout patterns and the icon map, and is checked against the living style guide (`/dev/ui`).
13. **`CLAUDE.md`** in the repository root summarises these rules for the coding agent; update it whenever a rule in this plan changes.
14. **Documentation for the school is written for non-technical readers**, in Italian first and English as a faithful mirror (setup guides of 8.7, admin and operator guides, user manual): numbered steps, one action per step, **screenshots generated from the app** with the demo data, never taken by hand (§3.23; the pages BiblioViva does not draw, such as the Tailscale admin console, are mocks drawn in the repository and taken the same way, ADR-046), links to the official pages of the tools (Tailscale, Docker, Raspberry Pi Imager) instead of paraphrasing them, plain words with a glossary. Technical documentation (`docs/deploy.md`, `config-reference.md`, authoring guides) stays in English.
15. **No dates or history in the plan, the documents and code comments.** They describe the current state, never when or in which order something was decided or changed; history lives in git, in the Date column of `docs/STATUS.md` and in the ADR titles of `docs/DECISIONS.md`. Dates that are data (example codes, holidays, sample calendars) are fine. A new step or phase takes the next free number, and rule 2 says when it runs.

---

## 1. Requirements summary and constraints

### 1.1 Functional scope (v1)

**Versions (owner decision).** The complete version described by this plan is released as **0.9.0**; **1.0.0** follows only after a few months of field testing at the school and the fixes it brings. "v1" in this plan names the scope of this plan (released as 0.9.0).

| Area | Requirements (from the owner) |
|---|---|
| Catalog | Bibliographic records (`biblio`) and physical copies (`item`) à la Koha, with few essential fields (§4). ISBN scanning with the phone camera, automatic metadata lookup via **Z39.50** (OPAC SBN pre-configured), cover photo with perspective straightening, item barcodes `YYYY-NNNNC` (Q7). School-library classification: fiction with a genre (favole, gialli, avventura, …) and, for primary schools only (config flag), an associated class; non-fiction with a very simple Dewey-like taxonomy of 1–2 levels; every genre and taxonomy node has an image (§3.17). |
| Labels | Template-based printing, like the cards, of internal barcode labels and spine bands ("fascette") for books, constrained by the size of the adhesive label sheets in use. The default reproduces the library's current printout: for each book a spine band and a barcode label stacked as one unit on a 70 × 48 mm label sheet (3 × 6, 9 books per A4), see §3.12 and `docs/reference/labels/`. |
| Print lists | Lists of patrons and lists of books defined easily from the UI (search, class, grid selection, scanning, quick filters), saved with a name and used as the source for printing cards and barcode/spine labels (§3.12, 2.8, 3.8). |
| UX | Simple, modern, easy to use yet powerful, pleasant to look at; strictly light theme with colours and icons (Lucide). Koha's dated and complicated interface is the anti-pattern (§3.18). |
| Tables and exports | Data tables with sorting, filtering, selection and virtual scrolling; every table exportable to CSV and ODS (§3.19). |
| Storage | Two container volumes: `/config` (configuration, reports, templates, classification images) and `/data` (database, photos and covers, imported files history, backups) (§3.20). Automatic daily database backup scheduled with a cron expression. |
| Platforms | Chromium is the browser of every everyday test run, and `bun run test:browsers` runs the suite on Firefox and WebKit too before 1.0 (owner decision, Q110); "mobile" means the same Chromium at a phone-sized viewport (412 × 915 CSS px, the size a recent phone reports regardless of its physical resolution), never a device emulator or a real device in CI (Q77). Container images for linux/amd64 and linux/arm64 only (32-bit excluded, §3.21, Q63). |
| Patrons | Students (class + section) and teachers. Card with barcode (counter + check digit, Q8). Photo (ID-style) taken from a phone in a dedicated census page. Card printing from an HTML template with database placeholders. Import of students (with class and section) and teachers from the CSV exported by the school register, with a start-of-year synchronisation mode (2.6). Admin-started class upgrade. Immediate anonymization of removed students (2.9, 7.1). |
| Circulation | Checkout by scanning the patron card (or typing a part of the name in the same field) and then the item barcodes (multiple items). Check-in. Renewals with configurable loan length and max renewals. Calendar with weekends and holidays. |
| Calendar | The days the library is closed (weekends, holidays, breaks), which drive the due dates, and **notable dates** that the staff write for everyone: a reading in a class, the deadlines of the bookmark contest, a meeting with an author (§3.8, step 9.25, Q125). |
| Reports | Predefined queries defined in JSON files (extensible). Statistics on loans (by class and section), patrons, books. |
| Notes | "Segnalazioni": short notes like post-its that any staff account can leave for the others and for the administrator; only the administrator archives them and puts some at the top of the dashboard (owner request, step 9.18, Q113). |
| Roles | `admin` sees/does everything; separate operator accounts for circulation, cataloging, membership/cards (Q10). Operator accounts are defined in `config.json` and are normally shared per role (Q20); students and teachers are the only patron types and live in the database (Q11). |
| Config | A folder of JSON files (§3.6), every setting editable from the admin UI (Phase 11). |
| Testing | Everything tested; Playwright is the owner's preferred framework (§7). |
| Localization | Italian and English supported from the start; one language for the whole installation (UI and printed material), set in `ui.locale` by the first-run wizard or the configuration, no per-user setting (Q52); simple, non-invasive library (sveltekit-i18n) (§3.16). |
| Audience | Staff only. No public or patron-facing interface: no OPAC, no self-service, no patron accounts (§3.15). |
| Cards | HTML templates with in-app preview; credit-card size; barcode, photo, texts and background image; front and back printed side by side, then cut and folded on the short edge, 4–5 per A4 sheet, or on the two sides of the sheet by a printer that prints both sides, 10 per A4, with a calibration of the backs (§3.12, step 9.27, Q127). |
| Platform | Web app, desktop + mobile. Container for x64 and arm64. Tailscale integration with HTTPS. |
| Size | ≤ 10 000 books, ≤ 1 000 patrons; must be lean and fast. |

### 1.2 Non-negotiable technical constraints

- Bun runtime with `bun:sqlite` (no external DB server, no native npm add-ons).
- TypeScript everywhere; Svelte 5 **runes only**; SvelteKit **3** with **remote functions**.
- Tailwind CSS 4 (required). Skeleton v5 (Tailwind 4 design system + Svelte components on Zag.js state machines) as the UI kit (Q1 resolved).
- SVAR DataGrid for tables.
- Latest stable release of every dependency (no beta), except Drizzle 1.0 which is explicitly accepted as RC (SvelteKit 3 was too until its stable 3.0.0).
- Single OCI image for `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64` (32-bit platforms: see §3.21).
- Exact version pins for every dependency; latest stable releases (Drizzle 1.0 RC is the accepted exception, Q4; SvelteKit 3 RC was until 3.0.0, Q3).
- Target browser: Chromium, desktop and phone-sized viewport (Q77). Other browsers best effort.

### 1.3 Repository state at the start

- `PLAN.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md` + `README.it.md`, `LICENSE` (**GPL-3.0-or-later**, see Q24), `docs/reference/` (current printouts and Koha classification list), and a `.gitignore` for .NET projects (to be replaced). No code yet.
- Development host: Fedora, `podman 5.8.4`, `bun 1.4.4`, `node 25.8.0` available (Node is used only by the Playwright runner, Q37).

Legend: ✅ verified from the npm registry or the official docs · ⚠️ decision pending (see §11).

### 2.1 Runtime and framework

| Package | Version | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `bun` | **1.4.4** ✅ | First Rust-based release line (1.4.0). Ships `Bun.Image`, `Bun.cron`, `Bun.password`, `node:sqlite` backup API, Temporal. Required ≥ 1.4 by adapter-bun. |
| `svelte` | **5.57.2** ✅ | `await` in components still behind `compilerOptions.experimental.async` (required by remote functions). No Svelte 6 published. |
| `@sveltejs/kit` | **3.1.1** ✅ | Docs: https://svelte.dev/docs/kit. Peers: vite ^8.0.12, svelte ^5.56.4, **typescript ^6**, vite-plugin-svelte ^7. |
| `@sveltejs/adapter-bun` | **1.0.0** ✅ | Official adapter (SvelteKit 3 only, Bun ≥ 1.4). Build `bun run --bun build`, run `bun ./build`. Supports `compile` for a single executable. It builds the server with Vite and leaves the packages under `dependencies` external (`sveltekit-i18n`'s `.svelte.js` core would run uncompiled, `$state is not defined`); a build-only plugin in `vite.config.ts` bundles every package, so the image needs no node_modules (ADR-020). |

| `svelte-check` | **4.7.7** ✅ | |
| `sveltekit-i18n` | **3.4.1** ✅ (runes core since 3.0.0) | i18n built for SvelteKit: JSON dictionaries, Curly parser with modifiers, per-route lazy loaders, documented per-request server instance for SSR; peer `svelte >=5`; bundles `@sveltekit-i18n/base` 3.3.2 (linear merge, ADR-019) and `parser-curly` 3.2.2 (§3.16, Q19). |
| `valibot` | **1.5.0** ✅ | Standard Schema validation for remote functions, config, reports. |

Types from `@techstark/opencv-js` 5.0.0-release.1 (dev dependency only). Lazy-loaded. |

### 2.7 External services

| Service | Parameters | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **OPAC SBN Z39.50** ✅ | host `opac.sbn.it`, ports `2100` (or `3950`), database `nopac`, syntaxes UNIMARC / MARC21 / SUTRS, charset UTF-8, anonymous, Bib-1 (ISBN `1=7`, title `1=4`, author `1=1003`, any `1=1016`). Records are returned **one at a time** (`show 1+1`, `show 2+1`, …). Avoid diacritics in queries. Contact `ic-cu.opacsbn@cultura.gov.it`. | No SRU exists. Undocumented JSON gateway `https://opac.sbn.it/opacmobilegw/search.json?isbn=…` exists (Q26). |
| Open Library (optional) | `https://openlibrary.org/isbn/{isbn}.json`, covers `https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/isbn/{isbn}-L.jpg?default=false` | 3 req/s with identifying `User-Agent`; covers by ISBN limited to 100 req / 5 min / IP (Q26, Q27). |
| Google Books (optional metadata; **default cover provider**) | `https://www.googleapis.com/books/v1/volumes?q=isbn:<isbn13>&country=IT` → `items[0].volumeInfo.imageLinks.thumbnail` (~128 px, `zoom=1`); larger image with `https://books.google.com/books/content?id=<volumeId>&printsec=frontcover&img=1&zoom=2` (the size is undocumented and is checked on download); unauthenticated calls are answered HTTP 429 with a daily quota of 0, so an API key is needed in practice — the `GOOGLE_BOOKS_API_KEY` environment variable, a secret that never goes in the configuration (the provider reports `rate_limited` next to the other results when the key is missing). | Q26, Q27. |
| Amazon cover images (optional, **second cover provider**) | undocumented URL pattern `https://images-na.ssl-images-amazon.com/images/P/<ISBN10>.01.LZZZZZZZ.jpg` (a 1 × 1 GIF means "no cover", handled as a miss); no API, no published terms for this use, may break at any time | Q27: enabled by default at the owner's request, easy to disable. |

### 2.8 Do not use (binding)

`lucia` (deprecated), `@oslojs/crypto` (deprecated), `html5-qrcode` (maintenance mode), `node-zoom2` / any node-gyp add-on (breaks Bun and multi-arch), `apexcharts` (non-MIT license since 5.2), `exceljs` (stalled) and `xlsx` from npm (frozen at 0.18.5), `sharp` (native; `Bun.Image` replaces it), `jsmarc` (GPL-3, license mismatch, see Q24), `quagga` (dead), `@catdadcode/svelte-adapter-bun` (dead), `svelte.config.js` (removed in SvelteKit 3), `@inlang/paraglide-js` (rejected by the owner as too invasive), `flowbite-svelte`/`flowbite` (superseded by Skeleton, Q1), `svelte-i18n` (superseded by sveltekit-i18n, Q19), `daisyui` (CSS-only, less complete than Skeleton for interactive components; never mix two Tailwind design systems), `@iconify/*` and `unplugin-icons` (icons come directly from `@lucide/svelte`), `xlsx`/`exceljs` (exports are CSV and ODS, §3.19), any dark-mode code path (light theme only, §3.18).

## 3. Architecture

### 3.1 Overview

```
 phones / PCs on the tailnet
        │ https://biblioviva.<tailnet>.ts.net  (Let's Encrypt cert issued by tailscaled)
        ▼
 ┌────────────────────────── compose stack ──────────────────────────┐
 │  tailscale sidecar  ──serve──►  127.0.0.1:3000  app container      │
 │  (tailscale/tailscale)          shared netns   (oven/bun:1.4.4-    │
 │                                                 distroless         │
 │  TS_SERVE_CONFIG=serve.json                     + yaz-client)      │
 │                                                    │               │
 │                                    SvelteKit 3 SSR + remote fns    │
 │                                    bun:sqlite (WAL) ── /data/db    │
 │                                    uploads, imports ── /data/…      │
 │                                    backups ─────────── /backups     │
 │                                    config.json ─────── /config      │
 │                                    reports, templates, pictograms ── /config/…
 │                                    yaz-client ──TCP──► opac.sbn.it:2100
 └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

One process, one SQLite file, three volumes (`/config`, `/data` and `/backups`, §3.20). No queue, no cache server, no external auth provider. An optional third container (`offsite`, rclone) copies the backups off-site (1.9).

### 3.2 Repository layout

```
biblioviva/
├── PLAN.md                     # this file
├── README.md
├── LICENSE
├── package.json  bun.lock  vite.config.ts  tsconfig.json  drizzle.config.ts
├── eslint.config.js  .prettierrc  .editorconfig  .dockerignore  .gitignore
├── src/
│   ├── app.html  app.css  app.d.ts  hooks.server.ts  hooks.client.ts  env.ts  params.ts
│   ├── lib/                    # imported as #lib/...  (SvelteKit 3 convention)
│   │   ├── server/             # server-only (never imported by client code)
│   │   │   ├── config/         # loader + Valibot schema + defaults
│   │   │   ├── db/             # schema.ts, client.ts, migrate.ts, fts.ts, seed.ts
│   │   │   ├── auth/           # sessions, passwords, rbac
│   │   │   ├── services/       # patrons.ts, catalog.ts, classification.ts, circulation.ts, calendar.ts, reports.ts, cards.ts, labels.ts, imports.ts, media.ts, backup.ts, audit.ts, anonymize.ts
│   │   │   ├── z3950/          # yaz-client driver, query builders, target config
│   │   │   ├── marc/           # ISO2709 parsing + UNIMARC → BiblioDraft mapping
│   │   │   ├── metadata/       # provider chain (sbn, openlibrary…), cache
│   │   │   └── barcodes/       # Luhn, YYYY-NNNN sequence, SVG rendering
│   │   ├── remote/             # *.remote.ts  (remote functions; NOT under server/)
│   │   ├── components/         # DataGrid.svelte, BarcodeInput.svelte, CameraCapture.svelte, …
│   │   ├── ui/                 # icons.ts (icon map), patterns: PageHeader, ListToolbar, FormLayout, DetailLayout, EmptyState, StatusBadge, Loading
│   │   ├── scanner/            # lazy-loaded barcode-detector wrapper
│   │   ├── vision/             # lazy-loaded OpenCV.js loader + cover dewarp
│   │   ├── shared/             # isomorphic pure code: isbn.ts, calendar-core.ts, formats.ts
│   │   └── i18n/               # index.ts (sveltekit-i18n config + client instance), server.ts (per-request helpers), it/*.json, en/*.json (one file per namespace)
│   └── routes/
│       ├── (auth)/login
│       ├── (app)/              # authenticated area with app shell layout
│       │   ├── dashboard, circulation/{checkout,checkin,overdues}, patrons/..., census, print-lists/[id],
│       │   ├── catalog/..., cataloging/new, reports/..., admin/{users,audit,backups,rollover,anonymize}
│       ├── (print)/print/...   # print layouts (cards, labels, slips) without app chrome
│       ├── media/[...]/+server.ts   # protected file serving (photos, covers)
│       └── healthz/+server.ts
├── static/                     # favicon, manifest (OpenCV.js is vendored in src/lib/vision/vendor/, lazy)
├── config/                     # the shipped configuration, copied into CONFIG_DIR on first run (config.json itself is written by the first-run wizard, 1.11):
│   ├── config.example.json, classification.json, classification/images/ (generated pictograms), school-presets/
│   ├── reports/                # built-in report definitions (*.json)
│   └── {cards,labels,slips}/templates/<name>/  # template.json, the faces (front.html+back.html | top.html+bottom.html | label.html | slip.html), style.css, assets/; labels/stocks/*.json
├── local/                      # dev CONFIG_DIR and DATA_DIR (gitignored, §3.20)
├── docker/                     # Dockerfile, compose.yml, tailscale/serve.json, .env.example
├── scripts/                    # seed.ts, backup.ts, hash-password.ts, import-images.ts, z3950-test.ts
├── setup.sh                    # guided installer for non-technical administrators (8.7)
├── site/                       # documentation site (§3.23, 9.7): assets/ (the mock's stylesheet, logo), .shots/ (generated screenshots, gitignored), dist/ (the built site, gitignored), CNAME; built by scripts/site-build.ts
├── tests/                      # e2e/ (Playwright specs + page objects), fixtures/ (.mrc, CSVs, images, fake-camera video), snapshots/ (visual baselines)
└── docs/                       # setup/{setup.it.md,setup.md,img/} (non-technical guides, 8.7), reference/{labels,cards,overdues,classification}/ (current printouts, layout descriptions, Koha CCODE list and pictogram sheet), manual/{it,en}/ (user manual, one Markdown file per chapter, 9.5), STATUS.md, DECISIONS.md, admin-guide.it.md, operator-guide.it.md, config-reference.md, reports-authoring.md, templates-authoring.md, deploy.md
```

### 3.3 SvelteKit 3 conventions cheat-sheet (differences from SvelteKit 2)

Source: https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/migrating-to-sveltekit-3.

- **No `svelte.config.js`.** Everything goes in `vite.config.ts`:
  ```ts
  import { sveltekit } from '@sveltejs/kit/vite';
  import adapter from '@sveltejs/adapter-bun';
  import tailwindcss from '@tailwindcss/vite';
  import { defineConfig } from 'vite';

  export default defineConfig({
    plugins: [
      tailwindcss(),
      sveltekit({
        adapter: adapter({ precompress: true }),
        compilerOptions: { experimental: { async: true } },
        experimental: { remoteFunctions: true },
        csrf: { trustedOrigins: [] } // filled from env in Phase 8
      })
    ]
  });
  ```
  Force runes mode on project files only (third-party components may still be legacy) through `compilerOptions.runes` as a function of the file name (verified in 0.2: `runes: ({ filename }) => node_modules ? undefined : true` in `vite.config.ts`).
- **`$lib` → `#lib`** (Node subpath imports in `package.json`: `"imports": { "#lib": "./src/lib/index.js", "#lib/*": "./src/lib/*" }`). `tsconfig.json` extends `$app/tsconfig`.
- `$app/environment` → `$app/env`; `$app/stores` removed → `$app/state`; `$env/*` → explicit env vars declared in `src/env.ts` (typed, validated).
- Route param matchers live in a single `src/params.ts` (`defineParams`, accepts Standard Schema).
- `invalidateAll()` → `refreshAll()`; `pushState/replaceState` → `goto(url, { shallow: true })`; `$app/paths` → `asset()` / `resolve()`.
- Internal links and `goto()` are typed against the routes (ESLint `svelte/no-navigation-without-resolve`): a raw `<a href>` or `goto()` takes `resolve("patrons/7?tab=card")` (a path without the leading slash, query allowed); the `href` of the UI components (`Button`, `Link`, `MenuItem`, `NavCard`…) is a `ResolvedPathname` — a route path like `"/patrons"` is accepted and checked — or, where offered, an outside address (`#lib/ui/href.ts`), opened with `rel="external"`. `paths.relative` is off, so `resolve()` gives absolute paths in server-rendered pages too.
- `error(status, message)` second argument is always a string; all errors go through `handleError` (validation errors carry `kind: 'validation'`).
- Cookies default to `path: '/'`. External redirects need `{ external: true }`. `csrf.checkOrigin` → `csrf.trustedOrigins`.
- Migration tool if ever needed: `npx sv@next migrate sveltekit-3 --tasks all --confirm`.

### 3.4 Remote functions conventions

Docs: https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/remote-functions (experimental).

- Files: `src/lib/remote/<domain>.remote.ts` (the filename must contain `remote`; must **not** be inside a `server/` directory). They import server-only services from `#lib/server/...`.
- Kinds: `query` for reads (pass every input as an argument; do not rely on `event.url`/`params` inside queries), `form` for HTML forms (progressive enhancement, file uploads via `v.file()` and `field.as('file')`), `command` for imperative mutations triggered by scans/buttons, `prerender` for static data. `query.batch` for lists that fan out.
- Validation: the first argument is a Valibot schema. Never accept unvalidated input.
- Authorization: first line of every handler is `const user = requireUser('loans')` (a **permission** name, §3.5; helper built on `getRequestEvent()`), which throws `error(401/403, …)`. There are **no** unauthenticated remote functions except `login`.
- Mutations refresh affected queries server-side (single-flight): `void getPatron(id).refresh()` inside the handler, or `command.updates(...)` on the client.
- Business logic lives in `#lib/server/services/*` as plain functions taking `(db, input, actor)`, or `(ctx, input, actor)` with a `ServerContext` (`#lib/server/context.ts`, a `Pick` of it when only part is needed) when they also need the configuration or the volumes. Remote functions are thin: validate → authorize → call service → return DTO. Services are unit-tested without HTTP.
- DTOs are plain JSON-serializable objects; dates are ISO strings; money is not used (no fines, Q15).

### 3.5 Authentication, sessions and roles

- **Staff accounts are defined in `config.json`** (`security.users[]`, Q20 resolved): `username`, `passwordHash` (argon2id, produced by `bun run auth:hash-password`; a plain `password` is accepted too but `config:check` and the admin dashboard warn about it), `roles`, `active`, optional `displayName`. Accounts are normally **shared per role** (`admin`, `prestiti`, `catalogo`, `tessere`), so the audit trail records the account, not the person (Q69 resolved: no operator-name tracking, not even optional). On startup and on every config reload the accounts are mirrored into `staff_user` (`source = 'config'`, upsert by username; accounts removed from the file are deactivated), so sessions, audit and permission checks work identically in every mode. The general case with personal accounts managed from the admin UI (`security.auth.mode = 'database'`, `source = 'database'`, password change, must-change flag) is Phase 10.1; who did what beyond the account name is deliberately not tracked. Staff accounts are separate from patrons; patrons never log in (staff-only product). Password hashing: `Bun.password.hash(pw, { algorithm: 'argon2id' })` (defaults m=64 MiB, t=2, p=1). Verification `Bun.password.verify`.
- Sessions: random 32-byte token (`crypto.getRandomValues`), stored **hashed** (SHA-256) in `session`, cookie `bv_session` (`HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax; Path=/`), sliding expiration (`security.sessionDays`, default 14). `hooks.server.ts` resolves the cookie to `event.locals.user` on every request.
- Login rate limiting: in-memory token bucket per IP and per username (5 attempts / 15 min), plus constant-time failure path.
- **Permissions are configurable (Q10 resolved).** The code knows a fixed list of *permissions*; *roles* are named bundles of permissions defined in `config.json` (`security.roles`, Appendix A) and a staff user holds one or more roles. Every check in the code uses a permission name, never a role name, so the owner can reshape roles without code changes.

| Permission | Grants |
|---|---|
| `loans` | checkout, check-in, renew, overdues and slips, read patrons (name, class, photo, active loans) |
| `patrons` | create/edit patrons, photos, census, cards, print lists of patrons |
| `patrons.import` | student and teacher import and synchronisation (removing the missing students also needs `anonymize`) |
| `catalog` | create/edit biblio and items, Z39.50 lookups, covers, classification, labels, print lists of items |
| `catalog.import` | catalogue import (CSV, UNIMARC) |
| `reports` | run reports and statistics, export tables |
| `anonymize` | removing (= anonymizing) patrons: manual removal, removal by the synchronisation, class upgrade leavers |
| `admin` | staff accounts page, configuration reload, backups, audit log, class upgrade (7.1), open-loans import, everything else |

Default roles shipped in `config.example.json`: `admin` (`*`), `circulation` (`loans`), `cataloging` (`catalog`), `membership` (`patrons`), `reporting` (`reports`); the import permissions belong to `admin` only by default, since the imports live in Administration → Importazioni (Q86), and a school may still grant them to another role. Reading the catalogue is implied by every role. `anonymize` is deliberately only in `admin` by default: a `membership` operator running the student synchronisation sees the students missing from the file but cannot remove them; an admin (or a role that includes `anonymize`) confirms the removal.

- Tailscale identity headers are **not** used for login (Q20 resolved): the app relies on its own password sessions only, whatever the network path.

### 3.6 Configuration (JSON)

- Location: `CONFIG_DIR` (default `./local/config` in dev, the dedicated `/config` volume in the container, §3.20). **The configuration is a folder, not one file** (Q78): `config.json` holds the short core sections; the bulky or copy-and-paste sections live in their own files and folders, merged at load time (step 2.4a):

  | Path in `CONFIG_DIR` | Contents | Merge rule |
  |---|---|---|
  | `config.json` | core sections (`library`, `catalog`, `barcodes`, `circulation`, `calendar`, `z3950`, `metadata`, `performance`, `photos`, `covers`, `privacy`, `backup`, `security`, `ui`, and optionally `cards`/`labels` defaults) | base document |
  | `<section>.json` (e.g. `classification.json`, `circulation.json`) | any top-level section kept in its own file | deep-merged over the same section of `config.json`; the fragment wins |
  | `calendar.<year>.json` | yearly holidays and closures | merged into `calendar` (existing rule) |
  | `labels/stocks/<id>.json` | one adhesive stock per file (`id` defaults to the file stem) | appended to `labels.stocks`, replacing the entry with the same `id` |
  | `cards/templates/<name>/`, `labels/templates/<name>/`, `slips/templates/<name>/` | one template per folder: `template.json` + Mustache HTML + `style.css` + `assets/` (§3.12) | discovered by folder, never merged into JSON; `cards.defaultTemplate`, `labels.templateByKind` etc. reference folder names |
  | `classification/images/` | pictograms of the taxonomy | referenced by `classification.json` |
  | `reports/<name>.json` | report definitions (§3.11) | discovered by file |

  Making a new template or stock means copying a folder or a file and editing it. `config:check`, the reload and the admin UI report every issue with the file it comes from, and Phase 11 writes each section back to the file it was read from. The shipped defaults mirror the same layout (`config/` in the repository: `config.example.json` + `classification.json` + `labels/stocks/` + `*/templates/`), and the first-run wizard writes only `config.json`.
- Loaded once at startup, validated with a Valibot schema (`#lib/server/config/schema.ts`); a **missing** `config.json` puts the app in setup mode (first-run wizard, 1.11); an **invalid** one makes the process exit with a readable error listing every problem. `bun run config:check` validates without starting the server.
- Hot reload: on `SIGHUP` or when the admin presses "Ricarica configurazione" (admin page) — no restart needed (Q14).
- **One archive format** (step 9.31): every archive the program writes — "Esporta configurazione", "Esporta tutto", the archive or backup of chosen parts — is one ZIP with `manifest.json` (app, version, date, library, the parts it holds), `db/biblioviva.db` (a snapshot cut to the chosen data), `uploads/` (photos, class pictures, covers) and `config/` (the files of the configuration folder, never `history/`), each present only for the parts asked for (11.12); "Esporta configurazione" is the archive of the configuration parts (`biblioviva-configurazione-YYYYMMDD-HHmm.zip`). One reader (`unpackArchive`, over the safe reader of `#lib/server/archive/read.ts`) takes every archive: safe paths, no links, only the entries the format knows, the size limits, BiblioViva's manifest. "Importa configurazione" takes any archive and applies its configuration parts (the database and the uploads are not even unpacked; an archive without configuration parts is refused with a readable message); "Ripristina" takes any archive and offers the parts it holds. Both stage the archive, check the folder the live one would become with the same checks (`checkFolder`, what is in use) and write it as one change of the history through the same path (`config-archive.ts`); the command-line tools (`config:export`, `config:import`, `backup:restore`) follow the same rules.
- The full example with every key documented is in **Appendix A** (shown as one document for readability; on disk it is split as described above). Top-level sections: `library`, `catalog`, `barcodes`, `circulation`, `calendar`, `z3950`, `metadata`, `performance`, `classification`, `cards`, `labels`, `photos`, `covers`, `privacy`, `backup`, `security` (including `users` and `roles`), `ui`. Everything in it will later be editable from the admin UI (Phase 11); the merged configuration folder stays the single source of truth.
- Secrets are **not** in config.json; they come from env vars declared in `src/env.ts` (`DATABASE_PATH`, `DATA_DIR`, `PROTOCOL_HEADER`, `HOST_HEADER`, …). Staff password hashes live in `config.json` (`security.users`, §3.5) because the config volume is admin-only; plain passwords there are tolerated with a warning.

### 3.7 Identifiers and barcodes

The formats are configuration (`barcodes.*`, Phase 12, Q90–Q93), edited in Administration → Codici a barre and chosen in the first-run wizard.

| Entity | New format (generated by the app) | Older formats (recognised, never generated) | Symbology | Generation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Patron card | `barcodes.patron.format`, default **`CNNNNNN`**: a mod-23 check letter and a 6-digit counter (`U012345`, Q91–Q92) | `retired` (formats the app generated before, e.g. the 10-digit Luhn cards of the first version) and `legacy` (shapes of the previous system: this library's 10-digit zero-padded cards `NNNNNNNNNN`, e.g. `0000001240`) | **Code 128** for new cards (`barcodes.patron.symbology`, Q65); the old cards are Code 39 and keep working | `sequence` row `card:<pattern>`; assigned at patron creation and on import of new names; the counter starts above every stored card of the same shape and every number of the older formats (or at `barcodes.patron.counterStart`), and numbers are never reused, not even after anonymization |
| Item | `barcodes.item.format`, default **`YYYY-CNNNN`**: 4-digit calendar year of cataloguing (current date in `library.timezone`; not the school year) + `-` + mod-23 check letter + 4-digit per-year counter (`2026-E0267`) | `retired` (e.g. `YYYY-NNNNC` with Luhn, the format of the first version) and `legacy` (`YYYY-NNNN` without check, the recent Koha labels) | **Code 128** for new labels (`barcodes.item.symbology`, Q65); the old labels are Code 39 and keep working | `sequence` row `item:<pattern>:<YYYY>` (one per year when the pattern has a year); above every stored code of the same shape and every number of the older formats of that year (`2026-E0043` after the old `2026-0042`); generated only for new items created in the app; imported items keep their barcode |
| ISBN (scanned) | EAN-13 starting 978/979 → ISBN-13; ISBN-10 converted; check digits validated | — | EAN-13 | — |

**Formats** are patterns of fixed-width tokens (`#lib/shared/barcodes/pattern.ts`): `YYYY`/`YY` year, one run of `N` for the counter, `C` the check character anywhere (computed over the payload digits in order), `{lib}` the library code (`barcodes.libraryCode`, unused here), literal digits and `-`, quoted capital letters (`'T'`) without `I O Q`. Check characters (`check.ts`): `luhn`, `damm` (digits) and `mod23` (a letter: the payload's value mod 23 on the 23 letters without `I O Q`; every single-digit error, adjacent and jump transposition and twin error changes it). Ready pairs (`presets.ts`): **standard** `YYYY-CNNNN` + `CNNNNNN` (default), **classic** `YYYY-NNNNC` + `NNNNNNNNNC` (the formats of the first version), **prefix** `'L'-YYNNNNC` + `'T'NNNNNNC`, each with any check; any other pair can be written under "Avanzate".

**Never confused (owner rule, Q90; `compare.ts`):** a configuration is refused (startup, `config:check`, the editor) when a copy format and a card format could give the same code — as written, without their dashes (a scanner losing the separator), or against the other kind's legacy shapes, the plain numbers of older systems (`barcodes.plainMaxLength` digits) and the ISBN-13/ISSN shapes; the decision never relies on the check character's value. The current copy and card formats must also look different: a length difference of at least 2 characters, a letter in a different position, or a different dash pattern. Shared legacy shapes of the two kinds and ISBN-10-shaped formats are warnings. Besides, a card number may never equal a stored copy barcode and vice versa (`#lib/server/barcodes/guard.ts`: patron form, imports).

**Recognition** (`#lib/shared/scan.ts`, driven by the compiled format table of the configuration, which the browser receives with the layout data): a code matching a generated format (current or retired) with the right check character is a copy or a card; with a wrong check character it is rejected with a clear message (`item_check_digit`, `card_check_digit`); a legacy shape gives `legacy_item` or a card (a legacy card shape equal to the generated one — the formats of the first version — follows `barcodes.patron.counterStart`: from that counter on, a wrong check digit means a mistyped code or an ISBN-10); `^97[89]\d{10}$` with a valid EAN digit → ISBN; any other all-digit string → **ambiguous legacy code** (old card or old item), resolved by the screen context first (the patron step of the checkout desk expects a card, the item step an item) and then by a database lookup on both tables. Uniqueness is enforced per table, plus the cross-table guard above; the import wizards reject a code that reads as the other kind.

Scanning is symbology-agnostic: the handheld scanner decodes both Code 39 (old labels and cards) and Code 128 (new ones) and sends plain text, and the camera scanner enables both formats; the setup guide reminds to keep both symbologies enabled on the scanner. Scanner input handling (`BarcodeInput.svelte`): the code is taken on Enter — the scanner sends it by itself with its Enter suffix (the setup guide says to keep it on), and there is no automatic submit after a burst of keys (Q87: whoever types must be able to check the code first) —, **normalizes layout mishaps** with the repair table of `barcodes.keyboardLayout` (`it`, `es`, `de`, `fr`, `uk`, `us`; `layouts.ts`): with an Italian keyboard a scanner configured for a US layout types `'` instead of `-`, a German one `ß` and swaps `Y`/`Z`, a French one `)` and the symbols of the digit row; lower case and a lost dash are repaired too, so `2026'E0267`, `2026 e0267` and `2026E0267` all become `2026-E0267` (Q9 resolved: the dash stays, input is normalised). A repaired reading is used only when it is a code the table knows.

### 3.8 Calendar and due dates

- Config: `calendar.weeklyClosedDays` (0 = Sunday … 6 = Saturday; default `[0, 6]`), `calendar.autoItalianHolidays` (1 Jan, 6 Jan, Easter Monday via computus, 25 Apr, 1 May, 2 Jun, 15 Aug, 1 Nov, 8 Dec, 25–26 Dec), `calendar.holidays` (explicit dates, e.g. local patron saint), `calendar.closedRanges` (e.g. Christmas break, summer closure with `reason`).
- **Notable dates** (step 9.25, Q125) are data, not configuration: the table `calendar_event` (§4.7) holds what the staff write for everyone (a title, an optional note, one day or a period from one day to another); no types and no link to classes. They never affect due dates. Every account reads them (the page `/calendar` and the dashboard section "Prossime date"); accounts with `loans` write them.
- Due date computation (`circulation.dueDateMode`, Koha-like): `datedue` (default: add loan days, then push to the next open day), `calendar` (count open days only), `days` (ignore calendar). `circulation.hardDueDate` (e.g. end of school year) caps every due date. Renewal extends from `today` or from the current due date (`circulation.renewFrom`).
- School year: starts on `library.schoolYearStart` (`MM-DD`, default `09-01`). It is **only a label** (`2026/2027` stored on every loan for statistics, default date range of reports): no action is ever triggered automatically by the date (Q47); the class upgrade is started by the admin (7.1).
- **The time zone is configuration:** `library.timezone` in `config.json` (IANA name, default `Europe/Rome`) is the single source of truth. The server never relies on the container clock's zone: "today", due dates, school-year boundaries and report date ranges are computed with `date-fns` + `@date-fns/tz` (`TZDate`) in the configured zone; the container also sets `TZ` to the same value so logs match. Clients receive ISO timestamps plus the configured zone from the server and format with `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, so a phone set to another zone still shows the library's dates. Calendar dates are stored as `YYYY-MM-DD`; timestamps as ISO-8601 UTC.

### 3.9 Z39.50 / metadata pipeline

No usable pure-JS Z39.50 client exists (verified) and native bindings do not work under Bun. Decision (Q25): wrap the **YAZ `yaz-client` CLI** installed from the Debian package in the container image.

```
ISBN/title query ──► z3950/yaz-driver.ts ──Bun.spawn──► yaz-client -f cmd.txt -m out.mrc < /dev/null
                                                        (timeout 15 s, stdin ignored, stderr captured)
                     out.mrc (ISO 2709, UTF-8) ──► marc/iso2709.ts (marcjs) ──► marc/unimarc-map.ts ──► BiblioDraft[]
```

- Command file per request (Appendix D): `open tcp:opac.sbn.it:2100/nopac`, `charset - utf-8 utf-8`, `format unimarc`, `elements F`, `find @attr 1=7 <isbn>`, `show 1+1` … `show N+1` (SBN returns one record per show; N = `z3950.maxRecords`, default 5 for ISBN and 20 for title/author searches), `quit`.
- Queries strip diacritics (NFD + remove combining marks) and quote phrases. Attribute helpers: isbn `1=7`, title `1=4`, author `1=1003`, any `1=1016`, right truncation `5=1`.
- Concurrency limited to 2 yaz processes; results cached in `metadata_cache` (key = provider + query, TTL 30 days). Errors are mapped to Italian user messages (no hits / target unreachable / timeout / parse error).
- Provider chain (`#lib/server/metadata`): `metadata.providers`, **default `["sbn"]` only** (Q26 resolved); `openlibrary`, `googlebooks` and `sbn-json` (the undocumented mobile-app gateway, not the public Z39.50 service) exist as opt-in providers. All providers return the same `BiblioDraft` shape plus `source`, `sourceId`, `raw` (for debugging/audit).
- **Cover chain** (`#lib/server/covers`, Q27 resolved): when no cover photo is taken, "Scarica copertina" tries the enabled entries of `covers.providers` in order (each `{ id, enabled }`), default Amazon → Open Library with Google Books present but **disabled** (owner decision: it needs an API key), `covers.country = "IT"`; the first image wider than `covers.minWidth` (300 px) wins, is resized to `covers.width` (600 px) and stored like a photographed cover, with `biblio.cover_source` recording the provider. `covers.autoFetch` (default `false`) fetches right after a successful metadata lookup instead of on demand. Amazon has no API: the provider uses the undocumented image URL by ISBN-10 and treats the 1 × 1 placeholder as "not found"; it is on by default because the owner asked for it, but it can disappear without notice, so the chain must degrade silently to the next provider.
- The Z39.50 target list is config-driven (`z3950.targets[]`), so other UNIMARC/MARC21 targets can be added later (Phase 10.8).

### 3.10 Files and uploads

- `DATA_DIR/uploads/patrons/<id>.webp` (480×640) + `<id>.thumb.webp` (96×128); `DATA_DIR/uploads/covers/<biblioId>.webp` (600 px wide) + thumb (150 px).
- Client does capture/crop/resize/encode (canvas → WebP q0.85) so uploads are small; server **re-encodes** with `Bun.Image` (`new Bun.Image(bytes).resize(480, 640, { fit }).webp({ quality: 80 }).bytes()`, verified in 2.3), which also strips EXIF/GPS metadata. Reject non-image or > `photos.maxUploadBytes`.
- Files are served only through `routes/media/...+server.ts` with authorization (patron photos are personal data), `Cache-Control: private, max-age=86400`, ETag. There is no public interface (Q18), so covers are protected as well: a staff session is required for every media route.
- `BODY_SIZE_LIMIT=32M` (adapter env) to allow CSV/MARC imports; photos are ~50 KB each after client-side processing.
- Class pictures of the census (Phase 13): `DATA_DIR/uploads/class-sheets/<sheet>/picture-<version>.jpg` (drawn upright and scaled by the browser, re-encoded by the server to at most `photos.sheet.maxPixels` on the longest side, no EXIF) and the straightened photo of each frame next to it (`frame-<frame>-<version>.<ext>`, exactly the photo size). The browser finds the frames (OpenCV.js) and sends their corners; the server straightens them (`#lib/server/images/warp.ts`). Served by `/media/class-sheets/<id>` and `/media/class-sheets/frames/<id>` to accounts with `patrons`.

### 3.11 Reports engine

- Definitions: `reports/*.json` built in (read from the application's `config/reports/`, never copied, so updates reach every installation, ADR-014); the school's files in `CONFIG_DIR/reports/` override built-ins by `id`. Texts (`title`, `description`, labels) are a string or one string per language (`{ "it": …, "en": … }`). Schema in **Appendix B** and `docs/reports-authoring.md`: `id`, `title`, `description`, `category`, `restricted`, `params[]` (`date`, `dateRange`, `int`, `text`, `select`, `level`, `stage`, `group`, `patronType`, `itemType`), `sql` (single `SELECT`/`WITH` with `:named` params) or `query` (the guided form), `columns[]` (`hidden` for an order column), optional `pivot`, optional `chart` (`bar` | `line` | `doughnut`, `x`, `y`), `defaultSort`, `rowLimit`.
- Safety: reports run on a **separate read-only connection** (`new Database(path, { readonly: true })`, `PRAGMA query_only = 1`), statement must start with `SELECT`/`WITH` after comment stripping, `;` and `ATTACH`/`PRAGMA` are rejected, parameters bound by name only, row cap (default 5 000), execution time logged.
- Output: SVAR grid, Chart.js chart, CSV and ODS export through the shared exporters (1.10), print view.
- **Pivot tables** (`pivot`: rows, one column dimension whose values become the columns, a value, totals) and **guided definitions** (`query`: source, filters, rows, columns, measure; the SQL is generated on the server and checked like any other), edited in the application with a guided view and an SQL view (9.20, Q116).
- Built-in report list is in Phase 6.3, reviewed in 9.20.

### 3.12 Card, label and slip templates

Cards are **credit-card size (CR80, 85.60 × 53.98 mm)** and are printed **front and back side by side** on the same face of the sheet: each printed *unit* is 171.20 × 53.98 mm (front on the left, back on the right, sharing a short edge). The unit is cut out and folded along that shared short edge, so the back needs no rotation or mirroring. An A4 portrait sheet holds **4 or 5 units** stacked vertically (`cards.sheet.rows`, default 4 like the current cards; 5 also fits: 5 × 53.98 mm = 269.9 mm plus gaps and margins).

Cards can also be printed **on both sides of the sheet** by a printer that prints both sides (step 9.27, Q127): the fronts on one page (as many cards as fit, 2 × 5 on A4), the backs on the next one at the mirrored places (the column mirrored when the sheet turns on its long edge, the row when it turns on its short edge, `cards.duplex.flip`), moved by the calibrated offset of the backs (`cards.duplex.backOffsetMm`); the faces themselves are never mirrored or turned. The operator chooses side by side or both sides at every print (default `cards.layout`). Every card is placed in mm from the corner of the paper (`@page { margin: 0 }`), and in both-sides mode the backgrounds run `bleedMm` past the edge, so a small shift leaves no white border. The calibration prints a 150 × 100 mm sample card on both sides: side 1 has the outline to cut along, side 2 two rulers in mm across where the cut edges should fall; the operator types the two values read on the rulers.

- **A template is a folder** `cards/templates/<name>/` (built-ins in the repo, user templates in `CONFIG_DIR/cards/templates/`, same name overrides):
  - `template.json` — `name`, `description`, `patronTypes` (which patron types it applies to), optional `cardMm` override, `bleedMm`, `barcode` (symbology and size, defaults from `barcodes.patron`).
  - `front.html`, `back.html` — Mustache templates with the inner markup of each face (Appendix C).
  - `style.css` — CSS in physical units (mm); the renderer scopes it to this template's faces; `background-image` allowed.
  - `assets/` — background images, logos, fonts (`.png`, `.jpg`, `.webp`, `.svg`, `.woff2`) referenced with the Mustache lambda `{{#asset}}sfondo.png{{/asset}}`, which the renderer replaces with a data URL so preview and printing never depend on network access or cookies.
- **Fields** are an allowlist documented in `docs/templates-authoring.md`: `{{patron.first_name}}`, `{{patron.last_name}}`, `{{patron.class_label}}`, `{{patron.card_number}}`, `{{{barcode_svg}}}`, `{{photo_url}}` (data URL; a neutral silhouette when the patron has no photo), `{{library.name}}`, `{{issued_on}}`, `{{valid_until}}`, `{{school_year}}`, sections `{{#patron.is_student}}…{{/patron.is_student}}`, `{{#patron.is_teacher}}…`, `{{#patron.has_photo}}…`. Every value is HTML-escaped except `barcode_svg`.
- **Built-in `default` template** reproduces the library's current cards (layout described in `docs/reference/cards/README.md`; the sample printout is not stored because it shows minors): **front** = school logo top-left, library name in italics across the top, ID photo (3:4) on the right, first name in grey above the surname in bold, a group line (`Sezione B` for students, `Docente` for teachers), barcode (Code 128 on new cards, Q65; the existing cards are Code 39) with the card number underneath, all on white; **back** = a full-bleed illustration supplied by the school (`assets/retro.jpg`; the repo ships a neutral placeholder because the original artwork is commissioned) and no text. No school year or validity date is printed, so a card stays valid for the whole school career (the section does not change from one year to the next). Units are separated by thin red cut lines (`cutLines`), no fold marks, four units per A4, as today. The same template serves every patron type through Mustache sections; a school-specific variant starts as a copy of it in `CONFIG_DIR/cards/templates/`.
- **Rendering** is server-side (`services/cards.ts`): per patron → `{ front, back }` HTML → assembled by the sheet wrapper (not part of the template) into `(print)/print/cards` with `@page { size: A4; margin: … }`, `print-color-adjust: exact`, one unit per row, optional dashed fold line and crop marks. `(print)/print/cards/[id]` renders a single unit (reprints, card printers). Photos, barcodes and assets are inlined as data URLs.
- **Preview page** `/admin/cards/templates` (permission `patrons` for cards, `catalog` for labels; `admin` sees all): template list with validation status; live front/back preview at real size (zoom 100 % and 200 %) for a sample patron or any real patron picked with the quick search; whole-sheet preview; "Stampa pagina di prova" (test sheet with fold/cut marks and a 10 cm ruler to check printer scaling); rendering inside a sandboxed `<iframe srcdoc>` so template CSS cannot affect the app; errors (missing file, unknown field, bad asset) shown inline; "Ricarica template" re-reads the folders without restart. Templates are managed from the admin UI (11.6) and drawn in the in-app designer (11.10); editing the files by hand keeps working.
- **Book labels** use the same folder convention (`labels/templates/<name>/` with `template.json`, one HTML file per cell of the unit, `style.css`, `assets/`) and the same preview page, but their geometry comes from the **adhesive label stock**, not from the template: `labels.stocks[]` in config describes each sheet product (label width and height in mm, columns, rows, top/left margins, horizontal/vertical gaps, corner radius, paper size; the bottom margin is what is left, because the sheets sold give the size of the labels exactly and only the top margin changes between makers, Q128) and every label template declares the stock ids it fits and the shape of its **unit** (`unit.cells`: how many consecutive cells one book occupies; `unit.stack`: `vertical`). Three label kinds ship in v1; the **pair** is the default because it reproduces what the library prints today with a Koha plugin (the reference printout, its HTML and the measured geometry are kept in `docs/reference/labels/`):
  - *Pair* (`labels.pair`, template `pair-default`, Appendix G): one unit = two labels stacked vertically for the same book on the 70 × 48 mm stock (3 columns × 6 rows, so 9 books per A4 in a 3 × 3 grid of units). The **top label is the spine band** ("fascetta", 70 mm wide so it wraps around the spine onto both covers): item barcode as small text, short title, the classification pictogram (20 × 20 mm, centred so it sits on the spine) and the call number split in two large lines: for fiction the head (genre letter and, when `classification.fictionByClass` is on, the associated class, e.g. `B 4`) and the rest (author cutter, e.g. `DEL`); for non-fiction the class code (e.g. `590`) and the class label in capitals (e.g. `ANIMALI`). The **bottom label is the internal barcode label**: call number, short title in italics, the barcode (50 × 15 mm; Code 128 for new labels, Q65; the template option `barcode.symbology` still accepts `code39`) and the barcode text in large type. All text is bold, centred, sans-serif (the printout uses `Arial, Helvetica`; Liberation Sans in the container for snapshots); the title is truncated to `labels.titleMaxChars` (30) characters like the original. **The template adapts to the stock** (Q56): `pair-default` declares two variants chosen by the cell height, `full` (≥ 40 mm, the layout above) and `compact` (< 40 mm, e.g. the half-height 70 × 24 mm sheet: 12 mm pictogram beside the call number instead of above it, no title line on the band, 40 × 8 mm barcode), and a **black-and-white printer** must never lose information: colour is only ever a redundant cue, pictograms are printed as they are, and `labels.monochrome: true` replaces the colour band of the compact spine sticker with a hatched pattern.
  - *Internal barcode label* alone (`labels.barcode`, template `barcode-default`): the bottom half of the pair on any single-label stock; for reprints and books that only need a new barcode.
  - *Spine band* alone (`labels.spine`, template `spine-default`): the top half of the pair, or the compact variant with a colour band for small spine stickers; for reprints after a reclassification.
  - Print routes `(print)/print/labels/{pair|barcode|spine}` with `?ids=…|list=<id>|pending=1|from=&to=`, plus `stock=` and a **start position** (counted in units, so in pairs for the pair kind) to skip already-used labels on a partially used sheet; a calibration test page per stock (label outlines, unit outlines and a 10 cm ruler); `item.barcode_label_printed_at` and `item.spine_label_printed_at` track what is still pending, and printing a pair sets both.
  - Unit placement: a unit with `cells = 2, stack = vertical` occupies cells *(column, row)* and *(column, row + 1)*; the renderer walks a grid of `columns × floor(rows / cells)` units, refuses stocks whose rows are not a multiple of `cells` (config validation, 1.1) and renders each cell with its own template file (`top.html`, `bottom.html`; single-cell kinds use `label.html`). CSS is scoped per template and sized by the renderer to the stock's `labelMm`; the sheet wrapper applies `@page { size: A4; margin: 0 }`, the stock's margins and gaps, `print-color-adjust: exact`, and draws no outlines except on the calibration page.
  - Field allowlist: `item.barcode`, `item.call_number`, `call.head` (the classification part at the start of the call number: genre + class for fiction, class code for non-fiction), `call.rest` (the remainder, normally the author cutter), `biblio.title_short` (first `labels.titleMaxChars` characters), `biblio.author_short`, `biblio.publication_year`, `classification.kind`, `classification.is_fiction`, `classification.is_nonfiction`, `classification.code`, `classification.label`, `classification.image_url` (data URL; a child node without an image inherits the parent's, §3.17), `classification.color`, `classification.reading_level`, `library.name`, `{{{barcode_svg}}}` (rendered with the template's `barcode` options), `{{#asset}}…{{/asset}}`.
- **Slips** (`slips/templates/<name>/`) use the same convention with their own field allowlist. The built-in **`overdue-by-class`** slip reproduces the library's current overdue notice ("solleciti", described in `docs/reference/overdues/README.md`): one block per group in class order, each with the heading `<group> - Solleciti biblioteca del <dd/MM/yyyy>` (group = class and section such as `1 D`, or `Docente` for teachers) and a bordered table with the columns *Nome*, *Titolo*, *Giorni ritardo*, one row per overdue loan (a patron with two overdue books has two rows), rows sorted by surname. Blocks follow each other on the page as today; `pageBreakPerGroup` (route parameter, default from `circulation.overdueNotice.pageBreakPerGroup`) puts each group on its own sheet so it can be handed to the class teacher. The date is the print date in `library.timezone`; days overdue are calendar days after the due date; a loan appears only when overdue by at least `circulation.overdueNotice.minDays` (default 5, configurable, 0 = immediately). Field allowlist: `group.label`, `group.kind` (`class` | `teacher`), `date`, `library.name`, `rows[]` with `patron.full_name`, `patron.first_name`, `patron.last_name`, `biblio.title`, `biblio.author_short`, `item.barcode`, `due_date`, `days_overdue`, plus `{{#asset}}…{{/asset}}`. A second built-in slip, `overdue-patron`, lists one patron's overdue loans for the desk (same fields, one block).
- **Print lists** are the normal way to choose *what* to print. A list has a kind (`patrons` or `items`), a name and an ordered set of entries. Staff build lists from anywhere in the UI: "Aggiungi alla lista" on patron and item rows and detail pages, grid selection → "Aggiungi selezionati", quick filters on the list page (a whole class or section, patrons without a printed card or without photo, items with pending labels, acquisitions in a date range, a genre or non-fiction class, an import batch) and **scan mode** (scan patron cards or item barcodes with a handheld scanner or the phone camera to add entries one by one, e.g. walking along a shelf). A basket indicator in the app bar shows the number of lists still to print (with entries, never marked as printed) and opens a menu with those lists and their counts; the list page allows removing, reordering, clearing, renaming, duplicating and saving; lists are shared among staff (Q61). Every print route accepts `list=<id>` in addition to `ids=`; after printing, "Segna come stampate" updates the printed flags and can empty the list.
- Playwright keeps screenshot baselines of the built-in templates (front, back, sheet), so rendering regressions are caught (§7).

### 3.13 Privacy and retention

- Data minimisation: no birth date, no fiscal code, no address, no phone (Q11, Q122); the only contact is the school's own email address of the student or teacher. Photos are optional and only visible to logged-in staff.
- Loans keep a snapshot of the patron type, the class (level, its position and stage, the group's id and name, the class label as printed) and the school year at issue time (Q95): a photo never rewritten, so history and statistics survive class upgrades, renamed classes and anonymization.
- Anonymization (step 2.9) happens **immediately when a patron is removed** (class upgrade, student synchronisation, manual removal), never on a timer: it irreversibly erases every personal datum (names, photo, email, notes, card number, external id) and keeps only what statistics need: class group, level, patron type and the loan rows linked to the patron id (Q32, Q33, Q71).
- Class pictures of the census (Phase 13, Q106) hold the faces of a whole class: kept only until the class upgrade, which deletes them all; deleted with their patron list; and removing a patron covers at once their frames in the pictures (painted over, their photo files deleted), so a removed pupil's face survives nowhere while the picture stays with the class. They are only visible to accounts with `patrons`.
- Audit log stores ids and actions, never names or photos.
- Backups contain personal data: stored in `BACKUP_DIR` (`/backups`, a volume of their own, §3.20), download restricted to admin, retention configurable.

### 3.14 Performance and size budgets

- Client JS on core pages (dashboard, circulation, patrons, catalog lists): ≤ 250 KB gzipped total; SVAR grid loaded only on list pages.
- Lazy chunks: `barcode-detector` + WASM (~1 MB) only on scan-enabled pages after the user taps "Scansiona"; OpenCV.js (~16.2 MB, ~3.3 MB brotli, `Cache-Control: immutable`) only on the cover-capture page (Q6).
- DB: WAL, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `busy_timeout=5000`, indices in §4, FTS5 for catalog search; every list query paginated (server-side) or virtualised (grid).
- Long jobs give the turn: the server answers every request on one JavaScript thread, so the sheets of labels and cards are rendered with breaks (`#lib/server/runtime/breaks.ts`: after `BREAK_AFTER_MS`, 20 ms, of work the job awaits the next turn of the event loop and the requests waiting are served). A sheet of every copy takes as long as before, but the desk and the other pages keep answering meanwhile.
- Targets on the minimum host (Raspberry Pi 4, 2 GB, SD card, 64-bit OS): p95 < 250 ms for circulation actions, catalog search over 10k records < 100 ms, cold start < 5 s; on an x86 PC with SSD: p95 < 100 ms, cold start < 2 s.
- **Hardware profile (Q22 resolved, step 9.28):** the settings that depend on the host are collected in `performance` (Appendix A) with `profile: "micro" | "small" | "medium" | "large"` choosing the defaults and every key overridable (values micro | small | medium | large, `#lib/shared/performance.ts`, every one read through `resolvePerformance`): `sqliteCacheMb` (8 | 16 | 64 | 256), `sqliteMmapMb` (0 | 0 | 0 | 512), `walAutocheckpointPages` (500 | 1000 | 2000 | 4000), `imageConcurrency` (1 | 1 | 2 | 4: photos, covers, class pictures and the logo processed with `Bun.Image` at once, the others wait their turn), `yazConcurrency` (1 | 1 | 2 | 2: `yaz-client` processes at once), `heavyConcurrency` (1 | 1 | 2 | 4: print sheets — cards, labels, notices, receipts, the print view of a report — and exports — CSV, ODS, archives, backups — at once, the others wait their turn in the order they came; the slot is held until the answer has been sent, and a job that holds one never asks for another, 9.32), `argon2MemoryMb` (16 on every profile, per password hash: the system is never exposed, 9.29), `smol` (true | true | false | false: the server started with `bun --smol` by the launcher `scripts/start.ts`, the container's entry point, which reads the profile from `CONFIG_DIR` and replaces itself with the server; verified in 9.28, no measurable effect in the load test of 9.29). The launcher also sets `BUN_JSC_forceRAMSize` to three quarters of the container's memory limit (cgroup), so the engine sizes its heap after the container rather than the host's whole RAM and leaves the rest to SQLite, the images and the binary (9.29). The server and the basic local-network front close a connection only after 60 s without traffic (`CONNECTION_IDLE_TIMEOUT`, at most 255 s: the image's default, set by the launcher when missing, overridable), so a slow print sheet is not cut (9.32). Backups and archives are always compressed. Profiles: **`micro`** = one slow core and 512 MB (a Raspberry Pi Zero 2 W, the smallest VPS); **`small`** = Raspberry Pi 4 with 2 GB and an SD card (the only profile that assumes an SD card); **`medium`** = an old PC with a spinning hard disk and a few GB of RAM (no mmap, moderate cache, checkpoints spaced out because seeks are slow); **`large`** = a PC with plenty of RAM and an SSD. `small` is the default because it is safe everywhere; the public demo runs `small`. Things that do **not** depend on the host: OpenCV and barcode WASM run in the browser; Chromium is never needed on the server. SD-card care under `small`: WAL with `synchronous=NORMAL`, logs to stdout only, backups written with `VACUUM INTO` to `/backups` and gzipped, no periodic `VACUUM`; the admin dashboard shows database size, free disk space and last backup. The compose file sets the container's `mem_limit` for each profile: micro 256m, small 512m, medium 1g, large 2g (Appendix E, measured in 9.29).

### 3.15 Security checklist (enforced in Phase 9.1)

CSRF (SvelteKit built-in + `csrf.trustedOrigins`), RBAC on every remote function (tested), secure cookies, rate-limited login, upload validation/re-encoding, read-only report connection, CSP + `Referrer-Policy` + `X-Content-Type-Options` headers set in `hooks.server.ts`, no secrets in config or repo, `bun audit` in CI, container runs as non-root with read-only root FS, app port never published (only via Tailscale), Funnel disabled, no unauthenticated routes except `/login`, `/healthz` and `/setup` (the latter only while no account exists, 1.11) (staff-only product, Q18).

### 3.16 Localization

- Library: `sveltekit-i18n` 3.0.0 (Q19 resolved): Svelte 5 runes core, Curly parser (`{{name}}` placeholders; the dictionaries use plain placeholders only, no modifiers so far), loaders with per-route lazy loading. **One language per installation (Q52 resolved):** `ui.locale` is chosen in the first-run wizard (1.11) — English first, always available — or changed later in Administration → Appearance and language (owner request), applies to the UI, printed material and exports alike, and there is no per-user or per-browser preference. The one exception is the public demo (Q120, ADR-047): there `resolveLocale` takes the visitor's language (the cookie `bv_lang` shared on `biblioviva.org`, then `Accept-Language`, then `ui.locale`), the top bar and the login page show the switch, and the messages, exports and sheets built on the server follow the request (`requestLocale()`); the data keep the language they were written in. The list of languages is not hardcoded: it is built from the dictionary folders present in `src/lib/i18n/` (`bun run i18n:locales` generates `locales.ts`; the parity test fails when it is stale), so adding a language is adding a folder.
- **Italian and English are both complete from the first screen.** Dictionaries live in `src/lib/i18n/{it,en}/<namespace>.json` (namespaces `common`, `nav`, `auth`, `patrons`, `catalog`, `circulation`, `reports`, `admin`, `errors`, `print`), one loader per locale and namespace with `routes` filters so a page loads only what it needs. A unit test enforces identical key sets across the two locales.
- Locale resolution: `event.locals.locale = resolveLocale(event)` in `hooks.server.ts` (`ui.locale`; the visitor's language in the public demo), `<html lang>` set through `transformPageChunk`. The server keeps **one** `I18n` instance initialised with that locale and rebuilt on configuration reload (no per-request instance is needed with a single locale, but the server code still reads the locale from `locals`, never from a module constant, so a reload takes effect on the next request); `+layout.server.ts` awaits `loadTranslations(locale, url.pathname)` and returns `{ locale, translations: i18n.snapshot() }`; the client hydrates from that snapshot and lazy-loads further namespaces on navigation.
- Usage: `i18n.t('patrons.title', params)` in components (reactive); `i18n.l(locale, key, params)` for a fixed locale; server code (error messages raised by services, exports, slips) uses `tServer(locals, key, params)` from `#lib/i18n/server.ts`, which reads the locale from `event.locals` and the shared server instance (§3.16 above).
- Dates, numbers and currency are formatted with `Intl` for the active locale (`it-IT` / `en-GB`) in the configured time zone (§3.8). SVAR grid locale packs follow the app locale.
- Printed material (cards, labels, slips) and exports use the same `ui.locale`; template texts written by the school (e.g. `Sezione`, `Solleciti biblioteca`) are data and are not translated.
- Rules: no hardcoded UI text in components; report titles, template texts and config-driven labels (item types, sections) are data written by the admin, not i18n keys.
- **Language packs (step 1.14, Q81):** besides the built-in languages, a folder `CONFIG_DIR/i18n/<code>/` with `pack.json` (`name`, `nativeName`, `appVersion`) and the same namespace files as `src/lib/i18n/en/` adds a language. Packs are validated against English at startup and on reload (`#lib/server/i18n/packs.ts`: missing files, bad JSON, non-string values and placeholder mismatches reject the pack; missing or extra keys are only warned, English fills the gaps through the fallback locale); the built-in `en` and `it` are written there on first run as examples to clone and are ignored as packs; `bun run i18n:check <folder>` validates offline; `ui.locale` accepts any available code and falls back to English (warned once) when the code is unknown.

### 3.17 Book classification (school-library scheme)

Two kinds of books with different rules, both configured in `config.json` (`classification` section, Appendix A) and stored on `biblio` (§4.1):

- **Fiction (`kind = fiction`)**: a **genre** (`genre_code` from `classification.genres`; the library uses single-letter codes `A`…`R` from its Koha CCODE list, e.g. `A` Romanzi e Racconti, `D` Avventura, `M` Fumetti, each with its pictogram, see `docs/reference/classification/` (Q66 resolved); one genre per book, Q55) and, only when `classification.fictionByClass` is `true`, a **reading level** (`reading_level`, a code of `classification.readingLevels`, the library's own ordered scale of `{ code, label }`; default one level per primary class, `1`…`5`; Q53, Q96, Phase 12.6). Middle and high schools set the flag to `false` and the field disappears from forms, labels, filters and reports.
- **Non-fiction (`kind = nonfiction`)**: a **local class code** (`class_code`) from a very simple Dewey-like taxonomy with **one or two levels** (`classification.nonfiction[]`, nodes `{ code, label, image, color?, children? }`; depth validated to ≤ 2). The Dewey number delivered by SBN (UNIMARC 676) is kept in `biblio.dewey` for reference and only *suggests* a local class through `classification.deweyMap` (longest prefix wins).
- **Images**: every genre and every top-level taxonomy node has an image (`image` file name in `CONFIG_DIR/classification/images/`, PNG/SVG/WebP/GIF; placeholder pictograms ship in the repo and are copied on first run); a child node without `image` (or `color`) inherits its parent's, which is how the current labels work (one pictogram per Dewey hundred, e.g. `500` for `590 Animali`). Any node, parent or child, can be assigned to a book. They appear in the classification picker, as badges in catalog lists and detail pages, in search filters and on spine bands (inlined as data URLs at print time). The UI loads them through `/media/classification/[file]` (staff session).
- **Call numbers** are suggested from the patterns in `classification.callNumber` (`fiction`, `fictionWithClass`, `nonfiction`) with placeholders `{{class}}`, `{{genre}}`, `{{code}}`, `{{author3}}`, `{{title1}}`; the suggestion is editable on each item (Q30). The defaults follow the scheme already on the shelves (Q30 resolved): the *segnatura* proper is the first three letters of the author's surname (`{{author3}}`, used to keep books in alphabetical order); the label adds the classification in front: fiction with reading class `{{genre}} {{class}} {{author3}}` → `B 4 DEL`, fiction without class `{{genre}} {{author3}}` → `B DEL`, non-fiction always `{{code}} {{author3}}` → `590 ROS`. This mirrors how the shelves are organised in this library (with classes: a shelf per class, books grouped by genre on the shelf and alphabetical within the genre; without classes: by genre, or by subject for non-fiction), but the patterns are configuration, not code, so another library can shelve differently. The label renderer splits a call number into `call.head` (the part produced by the classification placeholders) and `call.rest` (§3.12).
- **Reading level per copy (Q72)**: normally the reading level belongs to the record. This library keeps copies of the same title in different class shelves, which is logically wrong but practical, so `classification.classPerItem` (default `false`) moves the field to the item: when it is on, `item.reading_level` is used everywhere (labels, filters, reports, forms) and `biblio.reading_level` stays NULL; when it is off the opposite holds. The catalogue import detects copies of one title with different classes during validation and, when the flag is off, asks the operator whether to enable it (the answer is written to `config.json`, audited) before continuing; refusing turns those rows into errors.
- **Validation**: codes unique per list; images must exist (startup warning, not fatal); a startup check and the report `biblio-unknown-classification` list records whose codes no longer exist in the configuration. The taxonomy is edited in Administration (taxonomy editor, 11.1, Q59) or in its JSON file.
- **Everywhere else**: catalog filters by kind, genre, class (including children) and reading level (5.1); CSV import/export columns `kind`, `genre_code`, `class_code`, `reading_level` (3.9, 7.3); reports by genre and classification (6.3); batch reclassification (3.10).

### 3.18 UX and visual design principles

- **Colour sets (owner reviews; six more in 11.13, fifteen in all; one style, colours only, owner decision).** Nine palettes (`ocean` default, `forest`, `grape`, `sunset`, `cherry`, `lagoon`, `silver` light grey, `graphite` dark grey accents on light surfaces, `apricot` light orange) generated from hue/chroma definitions; switching one changes colours only, never shape or type. Status colours are the same everywhere. Style: warm and friendly in the spirit of Flowbite/daisyUI (soft cards, shadows, coloured tiles) implemented on Skeleton. Placeholders visibly lighter than typed text. Main navigation icons are large (40 px). The BiblioViva logo (`docs/reference/branding/`) appears on the login page and top-left of every page, and it is the link to the dashboard: the navigation has no separate dashboard item (owner review). The Italian UI says "Dashboard", never "Cruscotto". Tabs are a segmented control: the selected tab is a filled, bordered pill (never an underline alone). Every usable element gives feedback: buttons, links, selectable cards and swatches, menu and navigation items have a visible hover state and a pointer cursor (owner review).

The owner's brief: Koha's interface is dated and complicated; BiblioViva must be simple, modern, easy to use yet powerful, and pleasant to look at, in a **strictly light theme** with colours and icons. These rules are binding for every screen.

- **Light only.** No dark mode, no `prefers-color-scheme` handling, `color-scheme: light` on `:root`. Colour comes from the Skeleton theme (primary, secondary, tertiary and semantic colours), from the classification colours and from coloured icon tiles; surfaces stay white or very light with clear elevation; text contrast ≥ 4.5:1 (WCAG 2.2 AA).
- **Icons everywhere, consistently.** Lucide through its official Svelte package `@lucide/svelte`, one icon per concept (icon map `#lib/ui/icons.ts`, step 0.9); icons accompany labels and never replace them on primary actions; icon-only buttons carry `aria-label` and a tooltip.
- **Task-first navigation.** At most seven areas in the sidebar (Prestiti, Catalogo, Utenti, Stampe, Segnalazioni, Report, Amministrazione; Segnalazioni added by step 9.18, owner), a dashboard with the day's numbers and quick actions, the circulation desk as one screen, a help icon in the top bar that opens the manual of the area (9.5b); each area searches in its own page (no search box in the top bar, owner). No nested menus with dozens of entries, no MARC editing screens, no settings jungle.
- **Scan-first and keyboard-first at the desk.** One focused input, big colour + icon + text feedback (no sounds at the desk, Q40; the only sound of the application is the short beep of a camera read, Q137), undo instead of confirmation for reversible actions, shortcuts (F2 focuses the scan input, Enter confirms, Esc cancels), visible focus rings.
- **Progressive disclosure and good defaults.** Essential fields first, advanced ones in collapsible sections; defaults everywhere (today, default item type, suggested call number, last used label stock); inline validation on blur with plain-language messages that say what to do; a sticky save bar on long forms.
- **One pattern per job.** Every page is assembled from the documented patterns (`PageHeader`, `ListToolbar` + grid, `FormLayout`, `DetailLayout`, `EmptyState`, `StatusBadge`, `Toast`, `Dialog`); the living style guide `/dev/ui` is the reference. Lists: SVAR grid with search, filter chips, column presets and selection actions. Details: header card with photo or cover and key facts, then tabs.
