- **Status is colour + icon + text.** Item status, loan state (in corso, in scadenza, in ritardo, restituito), patron status and pending-print flags always use the same triple; overdue red, due soon amber, available green, reference or not-for-loan blue; never colour alone.
- **Mobile is a first-class client.** Designed for a 412 × 915 CSS px viewport (what recent phones report; physical resolution does not matter), bottom navigation, 44 px touch targets, thumb-reachable primary actions, camera flows full screen, grids that degrade to horizontal scroll or cards (Q44).
- **Speed you can feel.** Optimistic updates through single-flight mutations, one wait for anything over 200 ms (`Loading`: the large turning icon with "Caricamento…" over the page's part of the window, the menu and the bars left in sight), no full-page reloads, prefetch on hover or touchstart.
- **Microcopy.** Italian first (English mirrored), short, action verbs on buttons ("Registra prestito", not "OK"), operator vocabulary instead of library-science jargon ("copia" not "item", "segnatura" not "call number" — never "collocazione", owner), the technical term kept in tooltips for staff who know Koha; error messages say what happened and what to do.
- **Pleasant, not noisy.** Generous whitespace, 8-pt spacing scale, at most two font weights per screen, subtle motion (150–200 ms, honours `prefers-reduced-motion`), illustrations only in empty states, no gradients or shadows for their own sake.
- **Review gates.** Nielsen's ten heuristics checklist and a five-task timed walkthrough (checkout, check-in, add a book by ISBN, enrol a student with photo, print a class's cards) in step 9.3; axe in every e2e run.

### 3.19 Data tables and exports

- **Why SVAR.** Skeleton ships only CSS table styles, no data grid. Alternatives reviewed: `@vincjo/datatables` 2.8.1 (headless toolkit for Svelte 5, MIT, needs every column, virtual scroll and export to be built by hand), TanStack Table (headless, Svelte 5 adapter still unstable), Tabulator (vanilla JS, rich exports, heavy, not Tailwind-friendly), AG Grid (no official Svelte package, enterprise features paid). SVAR `@svar-ui/svelte-grid` 2.7.3 remains the best fit: Svelte-5-native, MIT, virtual scrolling, inline editing, sorting and filtering, frozen columns, responsive and touch mode, keyboard navigation, built-in CSV export and print, localisable. It is wrapped once (`DataGrid.svelte`, step 0.4) so it could be replaced without touching pages.
- **Every table exports.** The `ListToolbar` of every grid has an "Esporta" menu with **CSV** and **ODS** (LibreOffice/OpenDocument; opens in Excel too). Exports are produced **server-side** by `#lib/server/export/{csv.ts,ods.ts}` from the same query the grid is showing (current filters, sort and visible columns are sent along), so they are complete even when the grid is virtualised or paginated. CSV: UTF-8 with BOM, `;` separator, dates as `YYYY-MM-DD`; a text that a spreadsheet would run as a formula (`=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab, carriage return first) gets a leading `'` (numbers stay numbers), which the imports drop again. ODS: `content.xml` + `manifest.xml` + `mimetype` zipped with `fflate` (typed cells: numbers, dates, strings; a header row; column widths from the grid). Reports (Phase 6) use the same exporters. XLSX is not needed (Q34 resolved).

### 3.20 Storage layout: three volumes

| Volume | Env | Contents | Backup |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/config` | `CONFIG_DIR` | `config.json`, `<section>.json` fragments (`classification.json`, …), `calendar.*.json`, `labels/stocks/*.json`, `{cards,labels,slips}/templates/<name>/`, `classification/images/`, `reports/*.json` (§3.6); `serve.json` for Tailscale is **not** here (it belongs to the sidecar) | small, text: version it or copy it with any file backup |
| `/data` | `DATA_DIR` | `db/biblioviva.db` (+ WAL files), `uploads/patrons/`, `uploads/covers/`, `uploads/class-sheets/` (Phase 13), `imports/<batch-id>/` (original imported files with a `meta.json`), `tmp/`; a **named volume** in the compose files (SQLite's locking needs a file system the container owns, 9.34) | database copies made by the app into `/backups` (`backup.cron`, default `0 3 * * *`, retention `backup.keepDays`) |
| `/backups` | `BACKUP_DIR` | the database copies and the archives of chosen parts (`biblioviva-YYYYMMDD-HHmm.db`, `.zip`), `uploads/` (the copy of the pictures, 1.9b), `offsite.json` (the state of the off-site copy); the folder **`./backups` beside the compose file**, a plain folder the operator can see, copy, or mount from a second disk or a USB drive (step 9.35) | it is the backup: copied off-site by the `offsite` service or by hand |

The backups have a volume of their own so that a data volume removed or damaged does not take them with it; `backupsDir()` (`#lib/server/services/backup.ts`) is the one place that knows the folder. The public demo sets `BACKUP_DIR=/data/backups`: its only backup (the class upgrade's) goes with its data at every start. The three directories are created on first run (`/config` populated with the defaults copied from the image when missing). A copy of a built-in print template whose every file is a version the application shipped was never edited: at every start the current built-in replaces it (`#lib/server/templates/upgrade.ts`, record of shipped versions written by `bun run templates:shipped`, ADR-031); edited copies are left alone. In development they live under `./local/` (`local/config`, `local/data`, `local/backups`; gitignored).

### 3.21 Target platforms (Q63)

- **Supported by the plan:** `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64` (64-bit Raspberry Pi OS, Debian, Fedora, Ubuntu…). Both are covered by the official `oven/bun` images and by the Debian `yaz` package.
- **Windows (Q130, ADR-038):** Windows 10 and 11 64-bit run the same `linux/amd64` image in Docker Desktop with the WSL 2 back end (step 9.34), with named volumes only; the app has no Windows-specific code.
- **32-bit (`linux/386`, `linux/arm/v7`) cannot run Bun:** Bun ships no 32-bit builds at all, so neither the app nor `bun:sqlite` can start there. Node.js has no official 32-bit Linux builds either (only community "unofficial builds" for `armv7l` and `x86`). Supporting 32-bit would therefore mean a second runtime path on Node with reduced features: `node:sqlite` instead of `bun:sqlite` (Drizzle has a driver for both), `adapter-node` instead of `adapter-bun`, `node:crypto` scrypt instead of `Bun.password` argon2id, no server-side image re-encoding (`Bun.Image`; photos would be processed client-side only), a `setInterval` scheduler instead of `Bun.cron`, `child_process` instead of `Bun.spawn`. Estimated cost: a runtime facade plus a second CI matrix, and a permanently slower, less tested variant.
- **Decision (Q63): 64-bit only.** Rationale kept for the record: a Raspberry Pi 3 or newer runs the 64-bit OS, and i386 machines are older than 2010. To keep the door open cheaply, every Bun-specific API is used through a tiny facade `#lib/server/runtime/` (password, image, scheduler, spawn, file) from Phase 1, so a Node runtime variant, should it ever be wanted, would touch only that folder.

---

### 3.22 Imports: validate, confirm, commit (Q74)

Every import (students 2.6, catalogue and Koha migration 3.9, open loans 3.9; the pictogram CLI of 3.8 follows the same validate-then-copy logic) follows the same three phases, implemented once: on the server the shapes, the mapping of the cells and the `import_batch` shell of every commit in `#lib/server/imports/pipeline.ts`, the upload, validate and commit steps in `handlers.ts` over one descriptor per import (`kinds.ts`: columns, size limit, detection, validation and commit), the remote functions only declaring their schemas and permission; on the client one wizard (`#lib/components/imports/import-wizard.svelte.ts` and `ImportShell.svelte`), each import adding its own questions:

1. **Validate** the whole file against the current data and configuration without writing anything: parse, map columns, normalise values, resolve references (existing barcodes, card numbers, classification codes, item types), detect duplicates and conflicts, and classify every row as `new`, `update`, `unchanged` or `error` with a plain-language reason. Some findings are **questions** rather than errors, e.g. "copies of the same title have different classes: enable the class-per-copy behaviour?" (§3.17), "students missing from the file: remove them?" (2.6); the wizard asks them here.
2. **Confirm**: the preview shows counts, the error grid and the questions. The "Importa" button is enabled **only when there are zero errors** (there is no "skip errors" option: the operator fixes the file or the data and validates again). Warnings are allowed and listed.
3. **Commit** in one transaction, with an `import_batch` row, the original file kept, and a summary; any failure rolls everything back and reports it.

Cells are read without control characters, and every text column has the length cap of the field in its form (read from the form's Valibot schema, `#lib/shared/text-limits.ts`; a longer cell is an error `tooLong` with the column and the cap). Columns have English names only (`#lib/shared/import-columns.ts`), grouped as required, keys and optional; the template is generated with the columns the operator chooses, and a missing key is announced with what replaces it (9.19, Q115).

Validation is a pure function over the parsed rows and a read-only snapshot, so it is unit-tested with fixtures for every kind of error and question.

### 3.23 Documentation site and generated screenshots (Q114)

The user manual, the guides and the presentation site show BiblioViva's screens as **screenshots generated from the running app**, never taken by hand.

- **Generated.** `bun run docs:shots` is a Playwright run (Chromium) on the test setup: a fresh database with the deterministic demo data, the fixed font configuration of the e2e tests (`tests/fixtures/fontconfig/`), the default colour set and the demo library's name. A shot list, `docs/shots.ts`, names each picture (`circulation/desk-ok`), the page it opens, the windows it is taken in (Q132: **computer** 1280 × 800 at scale 1, **tablet** 834 × 1194 at scale 2 — an upright 11-inch tablet —, **phone** 412 × 915 at scale 2; computer and phone are the windows of the e2e projects; a screen is taken in all three unless the shot names fewer, a detail usually in one), the steps that bring the screen to its state (a scan, a dialog opened, a menu unfolded) and, for a detail, the element to crop to (a desk message, a dialog, a badge). Each picture is taken in every language of the site, the interface switched with the tests' `?lang=` override. A step that fails or an element that is missing fails the run, so a change of the app that breaks a picture is seen at once; a picture that merely looks different is simply taken again.
- **Format: lossless WebP.** Exact pixels (text and thin lines stay sharp, no compression artefacts), about 60 % smaller than PNG, read by every current browser, encoded by `Bun.Image` (no new package). Tablet and phone screens are taken at scale 2, so they stay sharp on high-density screens; computer screens are shown scaled down to the text column. A figure with more than one window has a switch Computer / Tablet / Telefono (CSS only, no script) that starts on the window closest to the reader's screen (a media query) until the reader picks another.
- **Not committed.** The pictures are written to `site/.shots/<lang>/<name>.webp` (gitignored) by the site build and frozen with each release in the archive branch (below); the repository keeps the shot list, not the pictures. Only synthetic demo data appear, never a school's installation.
- **The prose stays Markdown.** The manual (`docs/{it,en}/manual/`) and the guides stay Markdown, readable on GitHub; a picture is placed by a marker line `<!-- figure: circulation/desk-ok -->`, invisible on GitHub. The site converts the Markdown with `Bun.markdown.html` (built into Bun, no new package), and replaces each marker with a numbered `<figure>`: the picture of the page's language, at its width, with its caption as the text alternative. The names of buttons, tabs and pages are written in bold in the prose, and the site check fails when a bold label is not a text of that locale's dictionaries (a short exception list covers bold used for emphasis), so the manual never names a button the app has renamed. `bun run site:check` fails on a marker missing from the shot list, a bold label missing from the dictionaries, a heading anchor missing in one language and a step-number mismatch between the languages. The conversion: `Bun.markdown` gives tables, task lists, code blocks, links and heading ids, and a thin layer of the site build adds the rest — notes and warnings are GitHub alerts (`> [!NOTE]`, `> [!WARNING]`, shown by GitHub too) turned into the site's boxes; the anchors of a page are the same in every language, taken from the English heading at the same place (the parity of the headings is checked), so a help link works in both; a link with an anchor is written with the anchor GitHub makes from the target heading in the page's own language, so it works on GitHub too, and the site turns it into the shared anchor; links between the Markdown files are relative `.md` links, which work on GitHub and become site paths; the variant blocks of the installation steps (8.7) are kept or dropped by path; no footnotes and no raw HTML apart from the markers.
- **Publishing after a change (owner rule, ADR-050).** The site is published by hand only; so every change that alters what the documentation describes or shows (screens, quoted labels and messages, flows, settings, commands, installation, compose files) updates the pages of `docs/` and the shot list in the same commit, and the coding agent tells the owner that the site must be published again (and the home page built and uploaded again when it changed).
- **When the pictures are taken.** `bun run ci` takes every picture of the list in Italian (a broken step fails it) and builds the site with them, an English page showing the Italian picture; the site build of the Pages workflow and of a release takes them in every language. The manual's PDF (9.5) is printed from the built site.
- **Two sites (Q131, ADR-039).** The **home page** of `biblioviva.org` — the presentation, not versioned, Italian at `/` and English at `/en/` — is `home/`: two hand-written pages with the same markup in each language (`it.html`, `en.html`, a test keeps them mirrors), built by `bun run home:build` (`scripts/home-build.ts`: Lucide icons inlined as one sprite, the drawn screens, the addresses of the sites) into `home/dist/` and uploaded by hand to **Cloudflare Pages** (a direct-upload project: `bun run home:build` also writes `home/biblioviva-home.zip`, which the owner uploads; Cloudflare never builds from the repository); its one short script drives the scroll effects, and the page is complete and still without it or with reduced motion; a few inline lines in the head of the Italian page send a browser that prefers English, or neither language, to `/en/`, unless the visitor chose a language with a switch of `biblioviva.org` (the cookie `bv_lang` shared by the home page, the documentation and the public demo, ADR-047; the same switch in all three: a globe, then IT · EN); the links to the documentation and to the repository appear only once those sites are online (`ONLINE` in the build script: 9.7, 9.36). The **documentation** on `docs.biblioviva.org` is built by a Bun script, `scripts/site-build.ts` (`bun run site:build`; owner decision, ADR-043): the Markdown of `docs/` converted with `Bun.markdown` and a thin layer of its own (figures, notes and warnings, variant blocks, the pages of each installation path, links, the side menus read from the sections' READMEs), wrapped in the layout of the approved mock, written as plain files to `site/dist/` (gitignored) with the assets of `site/assets/` and the pictures of `site/.shots/`, and published by **GitHub Pages** (`site/CNAME`); `bun run site:serve` shows it locally (port 5175). No framework and no new dependency; the only scripts of the pages are the search and the few lines of the language switch, and the root `/` opens the language of the cookie `bv_lang`, else the browser's. Neither site is **served by BiblioViva**: the app only links to them (9.5b); it keeps adapter-bun and server-side rendering, because it needs a server (sessions, database, remote functions).
- **Sections of the documentation (owner).** Five, named as nouns in the top bar: **Manuale** (for whoever works at the desk, catalogues or makes the cards: step-by-step guides of the tasks — "Catalogare un libro nuovo", as many as the real situations of a school library need (the guides are the part readers use most) — and one reference page per screen, the target of the help icons), **Amministrazione** (the administrator's account: the first steps in order, one guide for each of its tasks, the reference of the administration pages), **Installazione** (for whoever installs, without experience: the choice of computer and network, the material, then a path of numbered steps in the manner of GOV.UK's step by step — every step visible at the side, the ones done ticked —, each ending with a check before going on; then updating, moving, starting again after a failure, the local-network mode), **Aiuto** (common problems, the glossary — copia, segnatura, tessera, tailnet… —, how to ask for help) and, last, **Tecnica** (for whoever knows: deployment, the configuration reference, commands, templates, reports, languages, security — the Markdown of `docs/` as it is, with the contents on the right). Four kinds of page, never mixed (Diátaxis): a guide (one task from start to end: time, permission, what is needed, numbered steps with pictures, the result, possible problems, related pages), a reference page (one screen: what it is for, permission, where it opens from, the picture with numbered callouts and their legend, its parts and messages with stable anchors), an installation step and a technical page. Paths are `/<lang>/<section>/<page>/#<anchor>` with English names in every language (`/it/guide/catalog-a-new-book/`, `/it/manual/checkout/#…`, `/it/install/raspberry-tailscale/3-network/`); the Markdown folders map onto them: each language has its folder `docs/<lang>/`, the sections are folders inside it, so a language is added by adding one folder (`docs/<lang>/manual/guides/<page>.md` → `/<lang>/guide/<page>/`, `docs/<lang>/manual/screens/<page>.md` → `/<lang>/manual/<page>/`, `docs/<lang>/admin/` → `/<lang>/admin/`, `docs/<lang>/setup/` → `/<lang>/install/`, `docs/<lang>/help/` → `/<lang>/help/`, the English documents of `docs/` → `/<lang>/tech/`). The documentation's home lists the sections with their main pages, frequent questions, what is new in the release and the PDFs. **Search** (Q134): Pagefind, a static index built with the site, one per language, its script loaded only when the search opens; without JavaScript the button leads to the A–Z index (a dependency, with its ADR when 9.7 adds it).
- **Tone (owner).** The documentation is serious and plain, neither chatty nor telegraphic: in Italian the instructions are in the infinitive as Italian manuals write them ("Premere **Salva**", "Leggere l'ISBN con il lettore"), the headings are nouns ("Procedura", "Occorrente", "Risultato", "Possibili problemi", "Verifica prima di proseguire"), every page opens with complete sentences saying what it is for, never a joke or a colloquial aside; English mirrors it with the imperative ("Press **Save**"). The home page addresses the visitor as *tu*, in a slightly serious tone. Everywhere the USB barcode scanner is the normal reader of cards, books and ISBNs; the phone is an optional extra, never presented as required.
- **One documentation, without versions (owner decision, ADR-042).** The site describes the current release only: it is built and published by hand with the manual Pages workflow, on the release's tag after each release (on `main` when only the text needs a correction; a push never publishes it, ADR-050), under `/<lang>/…` (e.g. `/it/manual/checkout/`: the same path in every language, so the language switch keeps the page), with the PDFs of the manual beside it (`/manual.it.pdf`, also attached to each release). There is no version selector, no path per release and no archive of old releases: old versions are not supported and an update is always painless (`setup.sh` again, or `docker compose pull`; the migrations run at start). The home page (`biblioviva.org`, Italian at `/`, English at `/en/`) always describes the current release too.
- **Look (owner).** The site has the layout and look of the mock the owner approved (the header with the five sections, the search and the language switch; the side menu of the section; the breadcrumbs; the step boxes of the guides and of the installation paths; the figure switch; the notes and warnings), whose stylesheet is the starting point of `site/`; it reuses the app's colour sets and type, light only; boxes have no accent stripe on the left — the side menu, the notes and warnings and every other box use a full light border or a tinted background.
- **Mocks of other programs (owner, ADR-046).** The documentation is self-contained: nothing in it is a screenshot taken by hand. Pages that BiblioViva does not draw (the Tailscale admin console, Raspberry Pi Imager, the phone's install prompt) are **mocks**: plain HTML pages in `docs/mocks/<area>/<name>.html` that draw the parts the step needs (the fields, buttons and labels named on the official page, no logos), taken by `docs:shots` in every language like the app's screens; as few as possible, the official pages being linked instead whenever they explain the step. A screen of BiblioViva that the test setup cannot bring to its state may be a mock too, until the shot list reaches it. Print outputs (cards, labels, slips) are pictures of the app's print pages, taken by `docs:shots` like any screen.

## 4. Data model (few essential fields; see Q7, Q8, Q11, Q30, Q42, Q53–Q55, Q58, Q72–Q75)

SQLite via Drizzle. Conventions: `id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`, timestamps `TEXT` ISO-8601 UTC (`created_at`, `updated_at` on every table), dates `TEXT` `YYYY-MM-DD`, booleans `INTEGER 0/1`, enumerations as `TEXT` with `CHECK`. Soft deletes are **not** used (except patron anonymization).

### 4.1 `biblio` — bibliographic record

Aligned with the fields the library actually fills in Koha's fast cataloguing framework (Q73): nothing more.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | int PK | |
| `title` | text NOT NULL | UNIMARC 200$a |
| `subtitle` | text | 200$e |
| `original_title` | text (nullable) | the title of the original work, only when the book is a translation (Q123): UNIMARC 500$a when the original language (101$c) differs from the language of the text (101$a), else typed by hand; searched like the title (9.24) |
| `responsibility` | text | 200$f (+$g) statement as printed (display and search) |
| `author` | text NOT NULL | main author "Surname, Name" (700); **special values** from `catalog.specialAuthors` (`Anonimo`, `AA. VV.`, …) offered as quick picks when there is no personal author (Q73) |
| `publisher` | text NOT NULL | 210/214$c; special value `catalog.unknownPublisher` (`s.n.`) when unknown |
| `publication_place` | text | 210/214$a; special value `catalog.unknownPlace` (`s.l.`) |
| `publication_year` | int | 210/214$d |
| `isbn` | text UNIQUE (nullable) | normalised ISBN-13 |
| `issn` | text (nullable) | periodicals (011$a), Q75 |
| `ean` | text (nullable) | EAN-13 of non-book material (DVDs) when the scanned code is not an ISBN; no external lookup in v1 |
| `series` | text (nullable) | the narrative series, e.g. `Harry Potter` (Q84: typed by hand with suggestions; MARC 21 490/800/830 when a provider has it; never the UNIMARC 225/410 *collana*) |
| `series_number` | text (nullable) | number in the series as printed: `3`, `3.5`, `II`; sorted numerically when numeric |
| `physical_description` | text | the whole 215$a string, e.g. `120 p. : ill. ; 24 cm` (pages, illustrations and size together, Q73) |
| `dewey` | text | numeric part of 676$a only, reference (suggests `class_code` through `classification.deweyMap`) |
| `kind` | text CHECK | `fiction` or `nonfiction` (§3.17, Q58); NULL while the record waits to be classified (9.21, Q117) |
| `genre_code` | text | fiction genre code from `classification.genres` |
| `reading_level` | text | fiction reading level, a code of `classification.readingLevels` (Phase 12.6), only when `classification.fictionByClass`; **NULL when `classification.classPerItem` is on** (the class then lives on each item, Q72) |
| `class_code` | text | non-fiction local class code (1–2 levels) from `classification.nonfiction` |
| `subjects` | text | JSON array (606); free keywords, searchable |
| `notes` | text | general note (500$a / internal) |
| `cover_path` | text | relative to uploads |
| `cover_source` | text (nullable) | `photo`, `googlebooks`, `amazon`, `openlibrary`, `upload` (§3.9) |
| `source` | text | `manual` / `z3950:sbn` / `openlibrary` / `import` |
| `source_id` | text | e.g. SBN BID `IT\ICCU\RMB\0803390` |
| `created_by`, `updated_by` | int FK staff_user | |

Dropped on purpose (Q73): contributors, edition, language, summary, separate page count (the series is kept, Q84; the original title of a translation is kept, Q123). Indices: `isbn`, `author`, `publication_year`, `kind`, `genre_code`, `class_code`, `reading_level`. FTS5 virtual table `biblio_fts(title, original_title, subtitle, author, responsibility, publisher, subjects, isbn, series)` with `tokenize = 'unicode61 remove_diacritics 2'`, kept in sync by triggers.

### 4.2 `item` — physical copy

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | int PK | |
| `biblio_id` | int FK NOT NULL | ON DELETE RESTRICT |
| `barcode` | text UNIQUE NOT NULL | the configured copy format for items created in the app (default `YYYY-CNNNN`); older formats, legacy plain counters and `YYYY-NNNN` accepted as-is (§3.7) |
| `call_number` | text | shelf mark ("segnatura" in the UI), e.g. `B 4 DEL` (Q30) |
| `item_type` | text | code from `library.itemTypes` (drives loan rules / not-for-loan) |
| `reading_level` | text | **only when `classification.classPerItem` is on** (Q72): the reading level this copy is shelved for; NULL otherwise (the biblio carries it) |
| `enumeration` | text | issue designation for periodicals, e.g. `2026, n. 3` (Koha 952$h), Q75 |
| `status` | text CHECK | `available` (default), `lost`, `damaged`, `in_repair`, `withdrawn` — "in repair" is a status, not a flag (Q73); "on loan" is derived from open loans, not stored (Q42) |
| `not_for_loan` | int 0/1 | reference copies |
| `acquisition_date` | date | |
| `notes` | text | one note per copy |
| `barcode_label_printed_at`, `spine_label_printed_at` | text | pending-label tracking per label kind (§3.12) |

Dropped on purpose (Q73): physical location (single library, no shelving locations beyond the classification), price, separate public/private notes. Indices: `biblio_id`, `status`, `reading_level`. Unique partial index on `loan(item_id) WHERE returned_at IS NULL` guarantees one open loan per item.

### 4.3 `patron`

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | int PK | |
| `card_number` | text UNIQUE (nullable) | the configured card format (default `CNNNNNN` with a mod-23 check letter); older formats and legacy counters accepted as-is (§3.7) |
| `external_id` | text UNIQUE (nullable) | stable id from the school register (matricola), used to match imports |
| `first_name`, `last_name` | text NOT NULL | |
| `search_name` | text | lower-case, diacritics stripped "lastname firstname" for prefix search |
| `patron_type` | text CHECK | `student`, `teacher` (Q11 resolved: library operators are not patrons, they are staff accounts of §3.5) |
| `class_group_id` | int FK `class_group` | the class of a student, the teachers group of a teacher (Phase 12, Q94, Q102); NULL when the class upgrade left a student without one |
| `level_code` | text | the student's year, a code of `school.levels` hosted by the group (a pluriclasse hosts several); NULL for teachers |
| `email` | text | optional: the school's own address of the student or teacher (internal mail, no access from outside), shown on the patron's page and used by the notices of 10.2 (Q11, Q122); no phone |
| `photo_path` | text | |
| `card_printed_at` | text | last card print (used by the "senza tessera stampata" quick filter) |
| `status` | text CHECK | `active`, `anonymized` (a removed student is anonymized at once, Q32). There is no manual block state (Q79). |
| `circulation_note` | text | shown as an alert at checkout (Koha "message") |
| `notes` | text | |
| `anonymized_at` | text | |

Indices: `search_name`, `class_group_id`, `level_code`, `status`, `patron_type`.

### 4.3a `class_group` and `class_group_level` (Phase 12)

A class is an object of its own (Q94): `class_group` (`id`, `kind` `students` | `teachers`, `name` — the section or the name, `B`, `Coccinelle`, `Docenti` —, `stage` for a teachers group, `notes`, `archived_at`) and `class_group_level` (`group_id`, `level_code`: the years a students group hosts, several for a pluriclasse). Groups are persistent cohorts (`3 B` becomes `4 B` at the class upgrade by changing its level, Q100), archived when empty, never deleted. The years themselves are configuration (`school.levels`, mirrored into `school_level` with `school_setting` for the class label template), and the view `patron_class` gives every patron's class label (`3 B`, a teachers group's name) for the screens and the reports.

### 4.4 `loan`

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | int PK | |
| `item_id` | int FK NOT NULL | |
| `patron_id` | int FK NOT NULL | |
| `issued_at` | text NOT NULL | timestamp |
| `due_date` | date NOT NULL | |
| `returned_at` | text | NULL = open |
| `renewals` | int default 0 | |
| `last_renewed_at` | text | |
| `issued_by`, `returned_by` | int FK staff_user | |
| `patron_type_snapshot` | text | for statistics (§3.13) |
| `level_snapshot`, `level_position_snapshot`, `stage_snapshot`, `group_id_snapshot`, `group_snapshot`, `class_label_snapshot` | text/int/text/int/text/text | the class at issue time, never rewritten (Q95) |
| `school_year` | text | `2026/2027` |
| `notes` | text | |

Indices: `(item_id) WHERE returned_at IS NULL` UNIQUE, `(patron_id, returned_at)`, `due_date`, `school_year`.

### 4.5 `loan_event` (audit trail of circulation)

`id`, `loan_id` FK, `type` (`issue`, `renew`, `return`, `lost`, `found`, `override`, `notice_printed`), `at`, `by_user_id`, `details` (JSON: old/new due date, reason, notice group).

### 4.6 `staff_user`, `session`

`staff_user`: `id`, `username` UNIQUE, `password_hash`, `display_name`, `roles` (JSON array), `active`, `must_change_password`, `source` (`config` for accounts mirrored from `security.users`, `database` for accounts created from the admin UI in Phase 10.1), `last_login_at`.
`session`: `id` (token hash) PK, `user_id` FK, `created_at`, `expires_at`, `last_seen_at`, `ip`, `user_agent`.

### 4.7 Supporting tables

- `sequence` (`name` PK, `value`): atomic counters, one per format: `card:<pattern>` and `item:<pattern>:<YYYY>` (§3.7).
- `metadata_cache` (`key` PK, `provider`, `payload` JSON, `fetched_at`).
- `audit_log` (`id`, `at`, `user_id`, `action`, `entity`, `entity_id`, `details` JSON).
- `import_batch` (`id`, `kind` patrons|catalog|loans, `filename`, `stored_path` under `DATA_DIR/imports/<id>/`, `mode`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `summary` JSON, `by_user_id`) — traceability of imports; the original file is kept.
- `print_list` (`id`, `kind` patrons|items, `name`, `created_by`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `last_printed_at`) and `print_list_entry` (`list_id`, `entity_id`, `position`, `added_at`, UNIQUE(`list_id`, `entity_id`)) — the lists used to print cards and labels (§3.12).
- `class_photo_sheet` (`id`, `class_group_id` or `print_list_id` — exactly one —, `path`, `width`, `height`, `detected_at`, `uploaded_by`, timestamps) and `class_photo_frame` (`id`, `sheet_id`, `position`, `corners` JSON, `crop_path`, `state` pending|ignored|saved, `patron_id`, `assigned_at`, `saved_at`, timestamps) — the pictures with many photos of the census and their frames (Phase 13); one frame per patron among the pictures of a class or list, checked by the service. Details in `docs/database.md`.
- `note` (`id`, `body`, `color` yellow|pink|green|blue|orange, `pinned`, `created_by`, `created_at`, `archived_at`, `archived_by`) — the staff notes ("Segnalazioni", step 9.18, Q113): written once, never edited; archived, pinned to the dashboard, restored and deleted by the administrator only.
- `calendar_event` (`id`, `starts_on`, `ends_on` nullable, `title`, `notes`, `created_by`, `updated_by`, timestamps) — the notable dates of the calendar (step 9.25, Q125): free text, one day or a period, no types and no classes.
- No `hold` table: reservations are not planned (Q16 resolved).

Everything else (loan rules, holidays, item types, sections) is configuration, not data (§3.6).

---

## 5. Phases and steps

Each step lists **Goal**, **Tasks**, **Deliverables**, **Acceptance criteria** and **Depends on**. Tick the checkbox when done.

### Phase 0 — Bootstrap (repository and toolchain)

- [x] **0.1 Dev environment and repository hygiene**
  - Tasks: verify `bun --version` ≥ 1.4.4 (`bun upgrade` otherwise); verify `podman` (or docker) can run `oven/bun:1.4.4-slim`; install `yaz` on the development host (`sudo dnf install yaz`) and verify `yaz-client -V`; replace the .NET `.gitignore` with one for Bun/SvelteKit (`node_modules`, `.svelte-kit`, `build`, `local/`, `.env*` except `.env.example`, `test-results`, `playwright-report`, `*.db*`); `LICENSE` already holds the GPL-3.0 text (Q24 resolved): set `"license": "GPL-3.0-or-later"` in `package.json` and add `// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later` headers to source files (ESLint header rule or a CI grep).
  - Deliverables: updated `.gitignore`, `.editorconfig`, `docs/STATUS.md` (empty checklist mirrored from §5), `docs/DECISIONS.md` (ADR-000 "Plan accepted").
  - Acceptance: `bun --version` ≥ 1.4.4; `yaz-client -V` prints a version; `git status` clean after commit.
  - Depends on: —

- [x] **0.2 Scaffold SvelteKit 3 with Bun**
  - Tasks: run `bunx sv@next create . --template minimal --types ts --install bun --no-add-ons --no-dir-check` (the directory is not empty; done in 0.1); then `bunx sv@next add tailwindcss eslint prettier vitest playwright` answering: no Tailwind plugins, Vitest unit + component, Playwright yes (no Paraglide: localization is set up in 0.8). Replace the adapter with `@sveltejs/adapter-bun@next` and configure `vite.config.ts` as in §3.3 (async + remote functions enabled). Pin **exact** versions from §2 in `package.json` (no `^`, `~` or ranges; run `bun pm untrusted`-free installs with `bun install --exact` or edit the file), run `bun install`, commit `bun.lock`; add `"license": "GPL-3.0-or-later"`.
  - Add `package.json` scripts: `dev`, `build` (`bun run --bun vite build`), `preview`, `start` (`bun ./build`), `check` (`svelte-check`), `lint` (eslint + prettier check), `format`, `test` (`vitest run`), `test:e2e` (`playwright test`), `db:generate`, `db:migrate`, `db:seed`, `config:check`, `auth:hash-password`, `classification:import-images`, `z3950:test`, `docker:build`.
  - Deliverables: running skeleton; `README.md` with quick-start commands.
  - Acceptance: `bun run dev` serves a page; `bun run build && bun ./build` serves the same page on :3000; `bun run check` and `bun run lint` pass; `bun run test` runs the sample test; `bunx playwright test` passes the sample test (if Playwright's runner fails under Bun, run it with Node and document it, Q37).
  - Depends on: 0.1

- [x] **0.3 Tailwind CSS 4 and Skeleton v5**
  - Tasks: install `@skeletonlabs/skeleton` and `@skeletonlabs/skeleton-svelte` (5.0.1). `src/app.css`: `@import 'tailwindcss'; @import '@skeletonlabs/skeleton'; @import '@skeletonlabs/skeleton-svelte'; @import '@skeletonlabs/skeleton/themes/cerberus';` (theme per Q51; a custom theme generated with Skeleton's theme generator goes to `src/lib/theme.css`) [verified in 0.3 against https://www.skeleton.dev/docs/get-started/installation/sveltekit: Skeleton 5 works with the SvelteKit 3 RC]; `<html data-theme="…">` in `app.html`; **light theme only**: `color-scheme: light` on `:root`, no `data-mode="dark"`, no `prefers-color-scheme: dark` handling anywhere (§3.18); the type of §2.2 (Inter and JetBrains Mono served by the app, works offline); `/dev/ui` route (dev-only) showing buttons, inputs, select, `Combobox`, `Dialog`, `Toast`, `Tabs`, badges, cards, `Pagination` at 412 px and 1280 px.
  - Deliverables: `app.css`, `/dev/ui`.
  - Acceptance: Tailwind utilities and Skeleton presets apply; the Zag-based `Dialog`, `Toast`, `Combobox` and `Tabs` work with keyboard and screen reader (axe clean); no Flowbite, daisyUI or ApexCharts package in `package.json`.

Check Tailwind preflight vs SVAR CSS (tables, borders) and fix with a scoped `@layer` if needed.

  - Tasks: install `sveltekit-i18n`; `src/lib/i18n/index.ts` with `config` (loaders for `it` and `en`, one per namespace, `routes` filters) and the client instance; `hooks.server.ts` sets `locals.locale` from `ui.locale` (§3.16), `<html lang>` through `transformPageChunk`; `+layout.server.ts` returns the snapshot; root layout hydration from the snapshot; the language is chosen once in the first-run wizard (1.11) and changed only in `config.json`; a `?lang=` override is allowed only in development and in tests; `#lib/i18n/server.ts` with `tServer()`; both dictionaries filled for every screen built from now on; unit test for key parity between `it/*.json` and `en/*.json`; CI heuristic (warning only) for hardcoded UI strings in `.svelte` files.

  - Tasks: choose and configure the Skeleton theme (Q51) and document the tokens (primary, secondary, tertiary, semantic success/warning/error/info, surface scale, radius, spacing) in `docs/design-system.md`; install `@lucide/svelte` (Q62 resolved: Lucide directly, no Iconify); icon conventions (per-icon imports `@lucide/svelte/icons/<name>` so only used icons are bundled, sizes 16/20/24, `aria-hidden` on decorative icons, `aria-label` on icon-only buttons) and the icon map `#lib/ui/icons.ts` (patron, teacher, book, copy, barcode, scan, camera, print, label, spine, loan, return, renew, overdue, report, settings, …) so one concept always has one icon; `StatusBadge.svelte` (colour + icon + text for item status, loan state, patron status, pending prints); layout pattern components `PageHeader`, `ListToolbar`, `FormLayout` (sections, sticky save bar on mobile, inline validation), `DetailLayout` (header card + tabs), `EmptyState`, `Loading`; `/dev/ui` becomes the living style guide showing every pattern and every status triple.

### Phase 1 — Core platform (config, database, auth, app shell)

- [x] **1.1 Configuration loader**
  - Tasks: `#lib/server/config/{schema.ts,load.ts,defaults.ts}`; Valibot schema for Appendix A (including the `classification` taxonomy: unique codes, non-fiction depth ≤ 2, `deweyMap` prefixes, image files existing → warning only; `labels.stocks` geometry must fit the page, and the rows of every stock referenced by a multi-cell label template must be a multiple of its `unit.cells`; `security.users`: unique usernames, roles that exist in `security.roles`, `passwordHash` or `password` present, at least one active account with `admin`); merge `config.json` + `calendar.*.json`; typed `getConfig()`; `bun run config:check`; `config/config.example.json` fully commented via `_comment` keys (JSON has no comments) and mirrored in `docs/config-reference.md`; first-run copy of the default `reports/`, `templates/` and `classification/images/` into `CONFIG_DIR` when missing (`config.json` itself is created by the wizard, 1.11); SIGHUP/admin reload hook.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for valid/invalid configs (missing keys, bad dates, unknown item type in rules) with readable error paths; server refuses to start on invalid config.
  - Depends on: 0.6

- [x] **1.2 Database layer, migrations, seed**
  - Tasks: `drizzle.config.ts` (dialect sqlite, driver bun-sqlite, `out: ./drizzle`); `#lib/server/db/schema.ts` with every table of §4; `client.ts` opening `DATABASE_PATH` with `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL; foreign_keys=ON; busy_timeout=5000; synchronous=NORMAL` plus `cache_size`, `mmap_size` and `wal_autocheckpoint` from the `performance` profile (§3.14); `migrate.ts` applying `drizzle/` migrations at startup (in `hooks.server.ts` `init`) and a custom SQL migration for FTS5 tables + triggers (Drizzle cannot model virtual tables); startup check that `fts5` is available (`SELECT fts5(?)` or creating a temp virtual table) with a clear error; `sequence` helpers (`nextValue(name)` inside a transaction); `seed.ts` with faker `it` locale (fixed seed): 300 patrons (classes 1–5 × sections A–D + 30 teachers) with a mix of legacy card numbers and new 10-digit ones, 2 000 biblios, 2 500 items with a mix of legacy barcodes (plain counters, `YYYY-NNNN`) and new `YYYY-NNNNC`, 3 000 loans across two school years, some overdue by more than 5 days (for the notices), copies of one title in two different classes (for the `classPerItem` question). (Revised by 9.12: 4 000 biblios, 5 000 items and ~4 000 loans, every card and copy in the current format.) (Patron photos, owner request: about half of the patrons get a photo, one of the 30 synthetic cartoon portraits of `demo/photos/` — cut from `docs/reference/demo/portraits-sheet.jpg`, listed with gender and age in `photos.json` — stored like a real photo with the configured format and size; faces follow the gender of the first name and the patron type and are shared by several patrons; class 2 C stays without photos for the photo census demonstration of Phase 13. `seed-photos.ts`, used by `db:seed` and by the wizard's demo data; the container ships `demo/`.) (Print lists, owner request: three lists of patrons — cards to reprint, a reading group, the teachers' cards — and two of copies — labels of new arrivals, the copies of one series in order — so lists, the basket and printing from a list show at once in the demo.)
  - Note: bun:sqlite may raise `SQLITE_LOCKED` on DDL touching FTS5 tables while cached statements exist → call `db.clearQueryCache()` before running migrations.
  - Acceptance: fresh DB migrates in < 1 s; seed runs in < 10 s; `biblio_fts` returns results for a prefix query; tests for sequences under concurrent transactions.
  - Depends on: 1.1

Creating and editing accounts from the UI is Phase 10.1.

- [x] **1.15 UI factoring (repeated Tailwind class sets → Svelte components) and explicit return types** (owner request)
  - Rule (owner): even though Tailwind's authors recommend repeating utility classes on every element, repetition goes against factoring. A control whose set of **at least 3** Tailwind classes is repeated **2 or more times** across the app must become a Svelte component in `#lib/ui/`; when the repeated set has only **2** classes the threshold is **4** occurrences (owner decision). Structural repetitions count as well (label + `label-text` + input, icon + label inside a button), since they are the real controls. Measured at the time with a script over `src/**/*.svelte` (1 284 static `class` attributes, 407 distinct sets): 61 sets meet the 3-classes rule and 15 sets the 2-classes rule; the biggest structural ones are the form field (81 occurrences), the icon + label button (46), the hint line `text-sm opacity-70` (39), `<select class="select">` (33), the page `<main>` wrapper (21), icon-only buttons (16), alert paragraphs (10).
  - Tasks: `scripts/ui-audit.ts` (`bun run ui:audit`) that prints the repeated sets with counts and fails above the thresholds (added to `bun run ci`); new components in `#lib/ui/` — expected: `Button` (variants filled/tonal/outlined/error, sizes, optional icon, `href` renders an `<a>`), `IconButton`, `Field` (label + control slot + hint + error, wrapping `input`/`select`/`textarea`), `Select`, `Hint`, `Alert` (error/warning/info paragraph), `Page` (the `<main>` wrapper with its width), `Card`/`Section`, `Badge`, `FilterSelect` (the `select w-auto` of the toolbars), `Toolbar` rows (`flex flex-wrap gap-2`), `DataGrid` footer already exists; every replaced occurrence goes through the component, `/dev/ui` shows each one, no visual change (Playwright screenshots of the main pages before and after compared by eye, e2e unchanged).
  - TypeScript: every declared function, method and exported arrow states its return type, `: void` included — ESLint `@typescript-eslint/explicit-function-return-type` with `allowExpressions: true`, `allowTypedFunctionExpressions: true`, `allowHigherOrderFunctions: true` (inline callbacks passed as arguments stay untyped: their type comes from the parameter) plus `@typescript-eslint/explicit-module-boundary-types`; measured impact: 246 signatures to annotate with these options (395 with every arrow included, 11 at module boundaries alone). `.svelte` scripts follow the same rule.
  - Acceptance: `bun run ui:audit` reports no set above the thresholds; `bun run lint` green with the two rules; `bun run ci` green; the component list documented in `docs/design-system.md`.
  - Depends on: 1.14

  - Context: lists of copies (print lists of kind `items`, 3.8) are already built by scanning barcodes, quick filters and the catalogue selection, and used to print labels. The owner wants the same lists to drive **bulk changes**, typically withdrawing ("dismettere") a batch of books: scan the barcodes into a list, then change the status of every copy in it. The record-level selection of the catalogue grid already adds every copy to a list and prints their labels.
  - Tasks: `services/batch-items.ts`, on the copies of the records selected in the catalogue grid (or given one by one; the lists of Stampe only print, owner): `planBatch` (validate first, §3.22: per copy the current and the new value, and the copies that cannot change with the reason — on loan for withdrawn/lost, already in that state, not found) and `applyBatch` (one transaction, one `item.status` / `item.update` audit row per copy with the reason typed by the operator plus one summary row); operations: **status** (available, damaged, in repair, lost, withdrawn — withdrawal is never applied to a copy on loan, it is listed instead), item type, not-for-loan flag, the copy's class when `classPerItem` is on, acquisition date; for selected records: reclassify (kind, genre, class, associated class) with the call numbers optionally re-suggested. UI: "Modifica le copie dei selezionati" on the catalogue grid's selection → dialog: operation, new value, reason (required for withdrawn and lost), preview with counts ("32 copie diventano ritirate, 2 in prestito restano come sono") and the list of exclusions, confirm. Permission `catalog` (the same as the single status change of 3.1; the confirmation shows the count before anything changes). Docs: operator guide (withdrawing books in bulk: select them in the catalogue, then change the status), admin guide §3.2.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the plan (every exclusion reason, idempotency, per-copy class only with the flag) and the apply (one transaction, audit rows, reasons stored); e2e on both projects: scan three barcodes into a list, one of them on loan, withdraw the list → two withdrawn with the reason, one listed as on loan and unchanged, audit rows present, the list emptied on request; e2e batch status change from the catalogue selection.
  - Depends on: 3.1, 3.8

- [x] **3.11 Image format and cropping from the configuration** (owner request)
  - Context: patron photos and covers are re-encoded by the server with `Bun.Image`, so far always as WebP (`photos.width/height/quality`, `covers.width/quality`). The owner wants the saved format defined in the configuration — resolution and JPEG quality — for every cover and patron photo, and asked where cropping is needed. Cropping: the browser already crops interactively (camera and file upload inside the 3:4 frame of `PhotoCapture`, perspective-corrected covers of `CoverCapture`); the server receives uncropped images only on the paths without a browser — the patron photos from Koha (3.9b, CLI and ZIP), a file the browser cannot decode (HEIC on some phones, uploaded as it is) and the photos of a scanned ID sheet (Phase 13). There the photo is kept whole (`Bun.Image` has no crop) and only displayed cropped (`object-fit: cover`), so the stored file does not have the configured shape. Covers keep their own proportions by nature and need no crop.
  - Tasks: configuration `photos.format` and `covers.format` (`jpeg` | `webp`, default **`webp`**, owner decision; WebP is about a quarter smaller at the same quality, and JPEG stays available for photos that must leave BiblioViva, e.g. for Koha), with `photos.width`/`height` (exact size, 480 × 640 by default: the photo is always **cropped centred** to that shape when the browser did not crop it), `photos.quality`, `covers.width` (maximum width) plus a new optional `covers.maxHeight`, `covers.quality`; thumbnails follow the same format. A server-side **centre crop without new dependencies**: scale with `Bun.Image` to the smallest size covering the target, encode as uncompressed PNG, decode it with `node:zlib` (filters of the five PNG types, colour types 0/2/4/6 at 8 bits), cut the centre, write a 24-bit BMP and let `Bun.Image` encode the configured format (`#lib/server/images/crop.ts`, unit-tested on odd sizes, greyscale and alpha inputs). File names carry the extension of the format actually written (`…-main-<version>.jpg` or `.webp`), the media routes, the card and preview data URLs and the export archive read the type from the extension, so photos saved before the change keep working; a photo or cover changed later is written in the new format. The browser keeps sending its cropped image (WebP or JPEG, whatever it encodes best); the server always re-encodes. Config reference and admin guide (images: format, size, quality, what the crop does). No bulk re-encoding of the existing files (not needed; a CLI can come later if the owner asks).
  - Acceptance: unit tests: the crop helper (landscape, portrait, square, 1-pixel edge cases, greyscale PNG, RGBA), `encodePhoto` produces exactly `width × height` in the configured format for inputs of any shape, covers respect `width` and `maxHeight` without cropping, JPEG and WebP outputs recognised by `Bun.Image.metadata`, the media routes answer with the right content type for old `.webp` and new `.jpg` files; the Koha photo import stores 480 × 640 JPEGs; e2e photo upload and cover capture still pass.
  - Depends on: 2.3, 3.7, 3.9b

### Phase 8 — Container image and Tailscale deployment

**Owner decision:** the code and the documentation of 8.2–8.7 are written first; the checks that need real resources (a Tailscale account and auth key, an arm64 host or a Raspberry Pi, the repository's GHCR settings, a person who has never used a terminal) are listed as *pending owner checks* in `docs/STATUS.md`, and a step is ticked only once they are done. Phase 11 starts without waiting for them.

- [x] **8.1 Dockerfile (multi-stage, non-root, healthcheck)**
  - Tasks: `docker/Dockerfile` (Appendix E): `FROM --platform=$BUILDPLATFORM oven/bun:<version>-slim AS build` (install with `--frozen-lockfile`, `bun run --bun build`), runtime on `oven/bun:<version>-distroless` (target platform, ADR-020) with `yaz-client` and its libraries copied from a `debian:trixie-slim` stage, copy `build/` (no `node_modules`: the server bundle is self-contained), `USER bun`, `VOLUME ["/config", "/data", "/backups"]`, `HEALTHCHECK` via `bun -e fetch('http://127.0.0.1:3000/healthz')`, `ENTRYPOINT ["bun", "./build"]`, env defaults (`PORT=3000`, `HOST=0.0.0.0`, `BODY_SIZE_LIMIT=32M`, `PROTOCOL_HEADER=x-forwarded-proto`, `HOST_HEADER=x-forwarded-host`, `ADDRESS_HEADER=x-forwarded-for`, `XFF_DEPTH=1`, `DATA_DIR=/data`, `CONFIG_DIR=/config`, `BACKUP_DIR=/backups`, `DATABASE_PATH=/data/db/biblioviva.db`); `.dockerignore`; three volumes `/config`, `/data` and `/backups` (§3.20) created on first run; migrations run at startup.
  - Acceptance: `podman build -f docker/Dockerfile -t biblioviva:dev .` then `podman run -v bv-config:/config -v bv-data:/data -p 3000:3000 biblioviva:dev` → `/healthz` OK, login works, `yaz-client -V` works inside the container, container runs as uid 1000, image size < 350 MB.
  - Depends on: all previous phases (functional freeze for v1)

- [x] **8.2 Multi-arch build and registry publishing**
  - Tasks: local: `podman build --platform linux/amd64,linux/arm64 --manifest biblioviva:multi .` (uses QEMU only for the runtime stage's `apt-get`, never for Bun, because the build stage runs on `$BUILDPLATFORM`); CI: `.github/workflows/publish.yml` on tags `v*` and `main`: prefer native runners per platform (`ubuntu-24.04` + `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, free for public repos) via `docker/github-builder` reusable workflow or a two-job matrix + `buildx imagetools create`; fallback QEMU job documented; push to GHCR with `docker/metadata-action@v6` tags (`semver`, `sha`, `latest`); provenance attestation optional. Known issue: Bun crashes under QEMU (SIGILL) — never run `bun install`/`bun run build` emulated.
  - Acceptance: manifest list contains both platforms (`podman manifest inspect`); the arm64 image boots on an arm64 host (or under QEMU for a smoke `bun --version` only) — Q22.
  - Depends on: 8.1

  - Tasks: `docker/compose.yml` (Appendix E): `tailscale` service (`tailscale/tailscale:v1.102.5`, `hostname: biblioviva`, `TS_AUTHKEY` from `.env`, `TS_EXTRA_ARGS=--advertise-tags=tag:biblioviva`, `TS_STATE_DIR=/var/lib/tailscale`, `TS_SERVE_CONFIG=/config/serve.json`, `TS_ENABLE_HEALTH_CHECK=true`, userspace networking default) + `app` service with `network_mode: service:tailscale`, `/data` volume, `read_only: true` + `tmpfs /tmp`, `cap_drop ALL`, `stop_grace_period: 35s`; `docker/tailscale/serve.json` (HTTPS 443 → `http://127.0.0.1:3000`, plain HTTP 80 → 301 to the HTTPS address, Funnel off); the optional `offsite` service (rclone, `profiles: [offsite]`, 1.9); `.env.example`; the same `compose.yml` must work with `docker compose` and with `podman compose` / `podman-compose` (verify `network_mode: service:` support; fallback = one podman pod for both containers or Quadlet units); volumes `./config:/config`, the named volume `app-data:/data` and `./backups:/backups` (§3.20, 9.35; the `offsite` service mounts `./backups` too).
  - Acceptance: on a test host: `compose up -d` → node appears in the tailnet, `https://biblioviva.<tailnet>.ts.net/healthz` returns 200 with a valid certificate, phone camera works on the census page.

- [x] **8.4 App configuration behind the proxy** *(checked against the deployed URL on a test tailnet)*
  - Tasks: the public origin is derived at runtime from `PROTOCOL_HEADER=x-forwarded-proto` and `HOST_HEADER=x-forwarded-host` set by tailscaled (adapter-bun has no runtime `ORIGIN`, ADR-007), `csrf.trustedOrigins` stays empty (same-origin), cookies `Secure`, `getClientAddress()` correct (`x-forwarded-for` from tailscaled), HSTS header. No plain-HTTP LAN mode: every client is on the tailnet (Q21 resolved).

The non-technical guide is 8.7.
  - Acceptance: fresh host walkthrough executed once following only the doc (recorded in STATUS.md).
  - Depends on: 8.3

- [x] ~~**8.6 Tailscale identity login**~~ — dropped (Q20 resolved: password login only; Tailscale is transport, never identity).
- [ ] **8.7 Guided setup for non-technical administrators (Italian and English)** — **postponed** (owner decision): Phase 11 comes first; a round of adjustments requested by the owner precedes the user manual and the site (9.5, 9.7), and this step is taken up together with them.
  - Goal: a teacher or school secretary with no IT background installs BiblioViva and reaches it from the desk computer, with the USB barcode scanner, in under an hour, following one document and answering a handful of questions. Everything that can be automated is automated; everything else is one numbered step with a screenshot and a link to the official page.
  - Tasks:
    - `setup.sh` (bash, POSIX-safe, idempotent, `curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/<owner>/biblioviva/main/setup.sh | bash` documented with the checksum next to it; written before the guide, which quotes its questions and messages): checks the OS (64-bit Linux, Raspberry Pi OS or Debian/Ubuntu), installs Docker Engine + compose plugin if missing (official convenience script, with a pause explaining what it does), asks in plain language **how the devices reach BiblioViva** — with Tailscale (the full option: HTTPS, camera, phones, from outside the school; a free account) or on the local network only (the basic option of 8.8: plain HTTP, no camera, no account) —, then the hardware profile (`small`/`medium`/`large`, with a one-line description each) and, with Tailscale only, the auth key (pasted); creates `~/biblioviva/` with the compose file of the chosen mode (`compose.yml` with the Tailscale sidecar and `tailscale/serve.json`, or `compose.lan.yml`), `.env`, `config/` and `backups/`; on the local network installs `avahi-daemon` where it is missing (so the computer answers to `<hostname>.local`), starts the stack, waits for the health check (with Tailscale, checks that the tailnet gives the node a certificate name and says to enable HTTPS certificates when it does not; the certificate itself is issued at the first opening) and prints the address to open: `https://biblioviva.<tailnet>.ts.net`, or `http://<hostname>.local:<port>` and the computer's network address on the local network, where the browser first-run wizard (1.11) asks for language, theme colour, library name, admin password and optional demo data; re-running it updates the image instead, and `--network tailscale|lan` moves an installation from one mode to the other keeping its volumes. Every question has its option (`--network`, `--profile`, `--key`, `--yes`), so it runs without a terminal too; whichever way is chosen, the stack's file is `~/biblioviva/compose.yml`, so the commands of the guides are the same for both. Italian and English messages (`LANG` detection, `--lang it|en`). Tested by `scripts/setup-sh.test.ts` (bash with stand-ins for docker, sudo, curl and the computer's commands on `PATH`: no new tool); end-to-end run recorded in STATUS.md on a Raspberry Pi and on a PC.
    - **The installation section** of the documentation (§3.23 Installazione), Markdown in `docs/it/setup/` and `docs/en/setup/` (same files, same headings and step numbers, Italian written first and English mirroring it; the mocks of other programs in `docs/mocks/`, ADR-046), written by the method of 9.5 (`docs/writing-guide.md`). Tailscale is never required: the guide presents the two ways side by side and the reader chooses.
      - **Entry pages**: `README.md` (the section's first page, shown by GitHub for the folder: what the section is for, the two choices, the paths in a table with the time each takes), `choose-computer.md` (a Raspberry Pi 4/5 with 2 GB or more and a 32 GB card, an old PC with Linux, the desk PC with Windows (9.34); the hardware profiles in plain words; a dedicated computer or the desk computer itself), `choose-network.md` (Tailscale and the local network compared: what works — HTTPS, the camera, the phone, the app installed on the phone, use from outside the school —, what each needs — a free account and internet, or nothing —, the account model of Q70; the mode can be changed later), `material.md` (the computer, the USB barcode scanner, the printer, label stock and card sheets; a phone, optional; links to buy or download).
      - **Paths**: one per computer and network — `raspberry-tailscale`, `raspberry-lan`, `linux-tailscale`, `linux-lan`, and with 9.34 `windows-tailscale`, `windows-lan` —, each a sequence of numbered steps in the manner of GOV.UK's step by step, every step ending with "Verifica prima di proseguire": prepare the computer (Raspberry Pi OS 64-bit Lite with Raspberry Pi Imager, hostname, user, Wi-Fi and SSH set in the imager; or Debian/Ubuntu on a PC), reach its terminal (SSH from another computer, or the computer's own terminal), with Tailscale prepare the tailnet (account, MagicDNS and HTTPS certificates, `tag:biblioviva` in the access controls with the exact JSON to paste, a reusable tagged auth key) or on the local network give the computer an address that does not change (a reservation on the router, asked of whoever manages the school's network, or the `.local` name), run `setup.sh` and answer its questions, the first-run wizard, connect the devices (Tailscale on each device with the school's account, or the address on the school network; the shared passwords handed to the colleagues), test the scanner and the printer (keyboard layout, "actual size / no scaling", the test pages, the checklist page `/admin/setup`), the backups (what is saved automatically, a copy on a USB stick or a NAS). Each step is one file in `docs/<lang>/setup/steps/`, shared by the paths that take it; where a step differs between paths, the file holds one block per variant between invisible markers (`<!-- variant: tailscale -->` … `<!-- /variant -->`, variants `raspberry`, `linux`, `windows`, `tailscale`, `lan`), each block opening with a visible label so that the file reads well on GitHub too, and the site keeps only the blocks of the path. Each path has a short page of its own, `docs/<lang>/setup/paths/<computer>-<network>.md`, a numbered list of links to its steps: GitHub readers follow it, and the site reads from it the order of the steps (the variants come from the path's name).
      - **After the installation**: `update.md` (re-run `setup.sh`, or `docker compose pull && docker compose up -d`), `move.md` (to another computer, with a backup), `restore.md` (start again after a failure, from a backup), `lan-to-tailscale.md` (from the basic to the full mode, `setup.sh --network tailscale`), `uninstall.md`; the common problems ("the address does not open", "the padlock is missing / the camera does not start", "the scanner types strange characters", "the printout is too small", "I forgot the admin password") and the glossary (tailnet, container, volume, backup) belong to the Aiuto section, linked from the steps.
      - Screenshots are taken only of pages BiblioViva does not draw (the Tailscale admin console, Raspberry Pi Imager), as few as possible because the official pages are linked; BiblioViva's own screens (the first-run wizard, the checklist page) are generated figures (§3.23). The Windows paths are written with 9.34.
    - `/admin/setup` first-run checklist page (permission `admin`, also linked from the dashboard while any item is red): configuration file valid; HTTPS and hostname detected from the request; hardware profile in use with a hint when the host looks stronger than `small`; accounts still using a plain password or the example password; backup schedule and last backup; printer test pages (cards, labels, calibration) with "print" buttons; camera test (opens the census page); scanner test (an input that shows what the scanner sends and detects the `'`/`-` layout problem); "Everything green" state. No configuration editing here (Phase 11), only status and links.
    - Q70 (Tailscale account model) answered in the guide: one school account used on every device (simplest, within the free plan) versus personal accounts invited to the tailnet.
  - Acceptance: a person who has never used a terminal completes the Italian guide on a fresh Raspberry Pi and reaches the login page from the desk computer (recorded in STATUS.md with the time taken); the English guide is a faithful mirror (headings and step numbers identical, checked by a script); `setup.sh` re-run is harmless; the checklist page turns green on a correctly installed system and each red item links to the right guide section; links validated by a CI job (`lychee` or equivalent) [VERIFY].
  - Depends on: 8.5

- [x] **8.8 Basic local-network mode without Tailscale** (owner request, Q89)
  - Tasks: a second, simpler way to run BiblioViva for a school that does not want Tailscale: `docker/compose.lan.yml` with the app container alone, published on a port of the host (`LAN_PORT`, default 8080); plain HTTP on the local network, so what browsers keep for secure pages does not work — no camera (barcode scanning, patron photos and covers by camera; files can still be uploaded), no installation as an app, no offline cache — while everything else works with a handheld scanner. adapter-bun assumes https when `x-forwarded-proto` is missing (the CSRF check would refuse every form from an http page), so with `LAN_PORT` set the app opens a small front on that port (`#lib/server/runtime/lan-front.ts`, started from `init`) that forwards each request to the app on the loopback with the headers the sidecar would add (scheme http, the requested host, the client's address), replacing whatever the client sent in them. The session cookie is marked `Secure` only when the request arrives over HTTPS, so the login works over plain HTTP; HSTS stays HTTPS-only. The clipboard copies (password hash, report link) fall back to the browser's copy command where the Clipboard API is missing, and the camera message names the basic mode. `docs/deploy.md` describes the mode and its limits; the non-technical guide (8.7) will present it as the basic option, Tailscale as the full one.
  - Acceptance: unit tests of the cookie options by scheme, of the front (forwarded headers replacing the client's, bodies, redirects and compressed answers untouched, 502 while the app is down) and of the clipboard fallback; the e2e server runs the front on port 3001 and `lan.spec.ts` logs in there over plain HTTP, with a spoofed `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`, getting a non-`Secure` cookie and no HSTS; `compose.lan.yml` validated with `docker compose config` and run once with a local image (login from Firefox over the host's network address); `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 8.4

### Phase 9 — Hardening, QA, documentation, release

**Owner decision:** the documentation starts with the installation section (8.7), which depends little on the screens; the manual and the administration guide (9.5, 9.5b) wait until the owner has closed the reviews of the UI (9.13), and the documentation site (9.7) goes online once the repository is public (9.36); every other step of this phase goes ahead.

- [x] **9.0 Wider desktop layout** (owner request) — the pages use more of a large window: the maximum widths of `Page` grow and adapt to the viewport (content pages and forms stay readable, grids, lists and editors use the space up to a wide limit), the app shell (sidebar, top bar) follows; the 1280 × 800 desktop viewport of the tests stays the reference and must not change; checked at 1280, 1600 and 1920 px wide and on the phone viewport (no horizontal scroll).

The manual prints well to PDF (one H1 per chapter, numbered chapters, page breaks before chapters, figures with captions that never split across pages, no wide tables), and `bun run manual:pdf` prints the print page of the built site (every chapter in one page per language) to `manual.it.pdf` and `manual.en.pdf` with headless Chromium (Playwright, already a dev dependency: no new package); the PDFs are attached to each release, not committed.
  - Method (owner): the documentation is written section by section by coding agents (Sonnet; Haiku for mechanical checks), Italian first. `docs/writing-guide.md` is the brief every writer follows: the readers of each section, the tone of §3.23, the four kinds of page with their outline, the figure markers and the bold labels taken from the dictionaries, the scanner as the normal reader, links to the official pages, no dates or history, every fact checked in the code. For each section the page tree is agreed with the owner first; writers take one group of pages each (at most four at once) and hand over the Italian pages, the figures they need (entries for `docs/shots.ts`; third-party screenshots as a list for the owner) and the facts they could not check, marked [VERIFY]; a fresh agent reviews the pages against the code (commands, paths, labels, steps); the owner reads and corrects them; then the English mirror is written and checked for parity (headings, steps, markers, anchors), by an agent until `bun run site:check` exists. Order: the installation section (8.7), the site's tooling (`site:build`, `docs:shots`, `site:check`; 9.7), the Aiuto section (the glossary first), the manual chapter by chapter, the Amministrazione section (from `docs/admin-guide.*.md`), the Tecnica section (the English documents of `docs/`), the PDF.

- [x] **9.8 Inventory (stocktaking) campaigns** (moved from 10.3 before the 0.9.0 release, owner decision)
  - Context (owner): the inventory is done by scanning **every book's barcode**, with the phone too, and the work spans **several days**; nothing is marked lost during the scanning. Only at the end, when the campaign is closed, the copies that were never scanned are marked as lost, in bulk.
  - Data: `inventory` (`id`, `name`, `status` open|closed, `started_at`, `started_by`, `closed_at`, `closed_by`, `notes`, `summary` JSON written at close) and `inventory_scan` (`inventory_id`, `item_id`, `scanned_at`, `scanned_by`, `shelf` — the optional shelf context typed or picked when a run starts, e.g. `B 4` or `590` —, `misplaced` 0/1, UNIQUE(`inventory_id`, `item_id`)); one migration regenerated (ADR-011). One campaign open at a time; a closed one stays as history with its summary.
  - Tasks: `services/inventory.ts` (`openInventory`, `recordScan` — idempotent per copy, unknown barcodes reported, a copy already `lost` that is scanned is set back to `available` with an audit row "found", copies on loan are recorded but never counted as missing; `misplaced` when a shelf context is set and the copy's `call.head` differs from it —, `progress` (seen / expected per classification shelf and overall; expected = every copy except on loan, withdrawn or `not_for_loan` kept elsewhere), `missingItems` (paginated, exportable CSV/ODS through the export engine of 1.9), `closeInventory` marks the never-seen copies `lost` with reason `inventario <name>` in one transaction — a confirmation shows the count first — and writes the summary, `reopenInventory` within 7 days); remote functions with `requireUser('catalog')`; page `/inventory` (permission `catalog`): the open campaign with its progress bars per shelf, "Nuova campagna", the scan panel built on `BarcodeInput` + `ScannerOverlay` (scanner-first, camera on the phone, continuous mode: scan after scan without touching the screen, the last five results as a list with colour + icon + text: seen, already seen today, misplaced, found again, on loan, unknown), the shelf-context chip, the missing list with filters (shelf, item type) and "Esporta", "Chiudi inventario" with the confirmation and the summary page; mobile-first (full-screen camera flow, 44 px targets, works one-handed, keeps the camera stream open like the census page); a dashboard tile while a campaign is open (progress and "Continua"); desk messages of Appendix I for "lost" copies keep working; `docs/operator-guide.it.md` chapter (with the English mirror) and a paragraph in the admin guide. (The guide chapter and paragraph are written with the manuals in 9.5, after the UI adjustments of the first tests: owner's instruction.)
  - Acceptance: unit tests for every service function (idempotent scans, unknown barcode, found-again path, on-loan handling, misplaced detection, progress per shelf, close marks only the never-seen copies and never those on loan, reopen); Playwright on both projects (desktop with the keyboard-wedge input, mobile with the fake camera fixture): open a campaign, scan across two "days" (two sessions, the second after a reload), see the progress and the missing list, close, verify the lost copies in the catalogue and the audit rows; axe clean; the campaign survives a server restart (data in the database, no session state).
  - Depends on: 3.3, 4.1, 3.10

- [x] **9.9 PWA** (moved from 10.6 before the 0.9.0 release, owner decision)
  - Tasks: web app manifest (name, icons from the logo, `display: standalone`, theme colour from the current colour set, start URL the dashboard) and a service worker (SvelteKit's `service-worker.ts`) that precaches the app shell and the immutable chunks, including the barcode WASM and OpenCV.js so the phone flows (census, inventory, fast-add, cover capture) start fast on the school Wi-Fi and survive short offline moments on the shell; **no offline data**: every remote call needs the server, and the worker never caches API responses or media (photos, covers need a session); "Aggiungi alla schermata Home" hint on phones (Chromium's install prompt); cache versioning tied to the build so an update replaces the old shell on the next load; documented in the setup guide (both languages).
  - Acceptance: Lighthouse PWA installability checks pass on the mobile project; an e2e test verifies the manifest, the registered worker and that `/healthz` and remote calls are never served from the cache; the OpenCV chunk is served from the cache on the second load of the cover page. (Lighthouse 12 has no PWA category, so the e2e test runs Chromium's own installability check through the DevTools protocol; the setup guide paragraph is written with the manuals in 9.5.)
  - Depends on: 3.7, 9.8
