- [ ] **9.13 UI revisions from the owner's reviews** (owner request) — one step that collects the interface changes the owner asks for while trying the app, round by round, before the manuals are written (9.5). Each round is listed here, implemented with its tests, committed as `phase 9.13 (round N): …` and recorded in `docs/STATUS.md`; the step is ticked when the owner closes the reviews. `docs/navigation.md` follows every change of pages or links.
  - Round 1: (a) dashboard — the "Libri senza etichette" tile opens the label printing dialog and the separate labels card goes (the `pendingLabels` dashboard switch with it); the patron tiles read "Utenti senza tessera stampata" and "Utenti senza foto"; (b) the back button — checked against UX guidance first: it returns to the page the operator came from inside the app and says where it goes, falls back to the parent page when a page is opened directly, and is larger (a labelled button with a 44 px target); (c) the sidebar and the top bar stay visible while only the content scrolls; (d) the desk tabs of Prestiti and the controls at the top of the Stampe pages take the look of the dashboard's quick actions (icon buttons, the current one filled); (e) the factoring audit re-run, with a count of the candidates under stricter thresholds, reported to the owner before any refactor.
  - Round 2: the desk tabs and the Stampe filter sit in a card like the quick actions; the factoring audit also counts the static part of class attributes with expressions, and the shared looks it finds become components (`ToggleButton` for the small choice toggles, `Notice` for status lines, `IconTile` for large icon squares, one snippet for the print-list entries), with `Spinner`, a list-count `Hint` tone and a scrolling `DialogFrame` for the useful pairs.
  - Round 3: the patron list works as the catalogue — nothing until a filter is set or the search has 3 letters (a card number at any length), the filter choices loaded on their own; a status column with badges for loans in progress, overdue loans (the same loan icon, in red) and a missing photo; a new setting `photos.required` (on by default, in the Photos section form and the archive): off, nothing reminds that photos are missing (the dashboard tile, the list badge, the print-list filter) while photos can still be taken, uploaded and printed on the cards.
  - Round 4: the class and section filters of the patron list read "Classe: 3" and "Sezione: A"; the page actions of the top bar get a row of their own under the title (next to it only on very wide windows), so they never overlap the search. Print lists stay shared by every account with their permission (personal lists were tried and withdrawn at the owner's request).
  - Round 5 (first tries on the phone through the tailnet): fast add without the "Passa il lettore…" hint and with the material under the code field; the camera opens full screen on phones (`CameraStage` around every `BarcodeInput` camera); the camera keeps scanning past reads that are not a valid code or not the code the page wants (fast add: an ISBN or an EAN), showing a small warning, and stops at the first good one; no "Ferma fotocamera" button where the page has its own close; the census drops the superfluous texts ("Oppure…", "Foto di", "con foto"/"senza foto" under each name) and shows the classes two per row on phones.
  - Round 6: after saving a new book the first choice is "Fotografa la copertina" (the cover camera opens at once, then the download from the providers as before); the cover camera opens full screen on phones with a portrait preview that fills most of the screen (`CoverPhotoPanel`, shared with the record page; `CameraCapture portrait`, used by the patron photos too).
  - Round 7: ISBN lookups that came back empty for older books — OPAC SBN indexes books before 2007 by their ISBN-10 only, so a 978 ISBN is now searched in both forms (`@or`), and the metadata cache key moves to v3 so the empty answers are asked again; a diagnostic mode `LOG_LEVEL=debug` writes every camera read (reported by the browser, card numbers masked) and every metadata lookup to the container log; the first-run wizard asks about the demo data before the passwords, with the demo data every account gets the same easy password (`biblioviva`), and it creates the `report` account (role `reporting`) as well.
  - Round 8: the debug log of the next empty record showed the camera had taken `0101876694291`, a non-ISBN EAN-13 whose check digit happened to be right — a single-frame misread (or a second code on the cover). The camera scanner now counts a code only after reading it the same way in two consecutive detections (`#lib/scanner/confirm.ts`, `ScannerOverlay confirmations`), and fast add takes only ISBNs from the camera: any other valid code gets the amber badge "Non è un ISBN: inquadra il codice del libro" while scanning goes on, and a valid EAN-13 is offered with "Usa comunque `<codice>`" for DVDs and other material (`ScannerOverlay offer`, `BarcodeInput cameraOffer`).
  - Round 9: the next empty record came from the phone still running the code of before the update (the log showed a non-ISBN read `taken`, which the new fast add refuses): the root layout now loads the next page afresh once SvelteKit has noticed a new build (`beforeNavigate` + `updated`), and the scan reports carry the client build so the log flags a stale one (`staleBuild`). "Nuovo libro" opened again always starts from the code: remote forms keep their last result for the whole visit, so the confirmation of the book saved before came back — the page (and the patron, catalogue, loan and photo imports, same pattern) now ignores a result that predates the visit. The fast-add form has "Leggi un altro codice" at the top and says which code was read, so a wrong read is one tap from a new try. Firefox for Android (Fennec) has no installation event: the dashboard hint explains its ⋮ menu instead of an "Installa" button.
  - Round 10: the report account shows its username in the wizard like the others.
  - Round 11: **the cover goes with the form** — in "Nuovo libro" the cover is looked up online while the form is shown (`previewCover`: the cover chain without storing, as it will be stored), shown next to the form with "Sostituisci con una foto" (full-screen `CoverCapture`) and "Togli", or "Fotografa la copertina" first when none is found; it is stored when the record is saved (`saveCoverData`) and dropped otherwise; `covers.autoFetch` now means this lookup and is on by default (the old fetch at save time is gone). The confirmation keeps the cover's state with the summary (thumbnail and "Copertina scaricata da …", never among the buttons) and puts "Aggiungi un altro" first, full width on a phone and focused. **Cover corners**: when the standard edge search finds nothing, two more sensitive passes run (contrast-stretched grey with lower thresholds; each colour channel and the saturation), with a closing to join broken edges and a convex-hull second chance for almost-square contours — a light cover on a light table is found (browser test with 8 grey levels of difference, which the standard pass misses). **New patron**: the same idea for the photo — taken before the save next to the form ("La foto è richiesta" when `photos.required`), stored once the patron exists (`savePhotoData`; `createPatron` returns the id and the page opens the patron).
  - Round 12: **the diagnostic log follows a whole session** (`LOG_LEVEL=debug`): one `http` line per request (account, path with safe query keys, remote function name, status, time, error/redirect/issues outcome; static files and the health check left out), `error` lines from `handleError`, `desk` outcomes (scans with masked cards, checkout/renew/checkin reasons), `covers` per provider, `import`/`backup` summaries (`traced`: numbers, flags and codes of the result only), and `client:*` events from the browser through `reportEvent` (`#lib/debug.ts`: navigation, script errors, camera start/failure, code field entries, cover corners and the pass that found them, photo and cover captures, the fast-add stages and the cover lookup). Toasts and desk messages have an even border instead of the thick left one. The configuration section's "Impostazioni / JSON" and the template designer's "Disegno / Codice" are `ChoiceButtons` in a card, as the Stampe filter.
  - Round 13: **one layout rule for the buttons** (Q86): header = actions on the page, `AreaTabs` under the title = the other pages of the area (circulation desk, Catalogo/Inventario, Utenti/Censimento, Report/Statistiche with the year filter under them, configuration Impostazioni/Cronologia, print templates Modelli/Anteprime, the four imports); `DeskTabs` became `AreaTabs area="circulation"`. **Imports in Administration**: `/admin/imports` (hub) and `/admin/imports/{catalog,patrons,loans,photos}` (moved from `/catalog/import`, `/patrons/import`, `/circulation/import-loans`, `/admin/photos`), one "Importazioni" card on the administration home, the import permissions out of the default `cataloging` and `membership` roles.
  - Round 14: photos and covers are stored as **WebP by default** (`photos.format`, `covers.format`; owner decision after asking which is better: smaller files on the SD card and in the backups, read by every browser; JPEG stays selectable for photos that must go to another program such as Koha). Existing images keep their format.
  - Round 15 (the full `bun run ci` runs only before a commit, owner rule): password fields get the eye button that shows what was typed (`PasswordInput`: login, staff account dialog); the open-loans page loads its grid only with a filter or 3 characters of search, as the catalogue (Q83), with the count of open loans in the start message (`getOpenLoanOptions`); the overdue notices by class keep each class's table on one page (`break-inside: avoid`, header repeated when a class needs more than a page); new documents `docs/database.md` (the database with Mermaid diagrams) and `docs/studies/multi-library.md` (feasibility study: one installation for several libraries).
  - Round 16: the navigation review applied with the owner's answers — the back button returns to the previous page, previous tab included; the template previews are for administrators only (one entry, under Configurazione); the import permissions stay, with a dashboard quick action "Importazioni" for non-admin holders and a hub that shows their imports only; Stampe, the print basket and its quick action need `patrons` or `catalog`; borrowers' names on the record page only for `loans`/`patrons`, links to patron pages only with `patrons`; the census class in the address; the calendar's year paging in the page; no single-pill tab rows; one name per import; "Altro" highlighted on phones and a search button below `lg`; the configuration's first tab is "Sezioni". The code field has no automatic submit any more (Q87). A daily housekeeping job removes expired sessions, old metadata lookups (and those of older cache versions), old "no cover" answers and abandoned temporary files. `docs/navigation.md` updated.
  - Round 17: the first day of the week is a setting, `library.weekStart` (`monday` | `sunday` | `saturday`, in the Biblioteca section of the configuration and in its archive like every setting): the first-run wizard sets it from the chosen language (Monday for Italian, Sunday for English), the calendars (read-only page and the calendar editor, weekly closed days included) show the weeks from that day, and the dashboard's "this week" ends on the day before it (the empty message names that day).
  - Round 18: the service worker no longer downloads the whole shell again at every update: the files named after their content hash (`immutable`: OpenCV.js with its 10 MB, the barcode reader's WASM, every unchanged chunk) are copied from the previous build's cache, so an update fetches only what changed (OpenCV was downloaded again and again during the daily deployments).

Tests: `bun run ci` runs the independent checks side by side and the e2e suite on the build just made (`scripts/ci.ts`, `E2E_PREBUILT`), with cached Prettier/ESLint; the GitHub workflow runs them one by one with one worker (`lint:ci`, `--maxWorkers=1`, Playwright `workers: 1` under `CI`) and skips the font-dependent print baselines there; the print baselines were made again after an operating-system update changed the fonts.

The record page: Modifica (primary), Aggiungi copie, menu Stampa etichette, Aggiungi alla lista, Elimina scheda; the patron page: Modifica (primary), Aggiungi alla lista, menu Esporta i dati (`admin`), Rimuovi; a print list of copies: Stampa etichette (primary), Modifica in blocco, menu Rinomina (a list of patrons keeps its two buttons). `MenuItem` gains `tone="error"` and `disabled`; `AddToPrintList` can be opened from a menu entry (`trigger={false}`, `bind:open`).
  - Round 22: **header actions that fit** — the record page's second action is Aggiungi alla lista; `#lib/ui/PageActions.svelte` takes the actions as data (`PageAction`: label, icon, link or click, primary, danger) and shows them all as buttons when they fit on the header's row with 48 px to spare, else the first ones — two at most, as many as fit beside the menu, one at least — with the rest under "Altre azioni"; it measures a hidden copy of the buttons and of the menu's button at every change of the header's width, and stays invisible until the first measure (record, patron and print-list pages). **The dashboard's top band** is as high as the other pages': the BiblioViva logo at 48 px, and the search and the account stay on the title's row on every page (a long title wraps beside them). **The demo-data badge** shows on the dashboard only, right of the print basket. The development server prints Vite's usual output again (the `logLevel: "warn"` of the dev commit is removed).
  - Round 23: **the title is never squeezed** — the page's actions always take a row of their own under the title, very wide windows included (where they used to sit beside it and left the title a narrow column); from tablets up the title keeps 18rem at least beside the search and the account, which go to the next row, on the right, when there is no room for both.
  - Round 24 (a review on an iPad-sized window): the demo data are announced only by the dashboard's panel, now with the warning icon (the top bar's badge is gone, so the bar no longer wraps); the back button is the arrow alone, its tooltip and accessible name saying where it goes ("Torna a Catalogo"); Stampe is renamed **Liste** (the lists are for bulk edits too), with the icon of the lists (the print basket's) instead of the printer; the statistics page (6.4) is withdrawn, Report is a single page; the built-in reports `loans-by-patron-type`, `items-by-status`, `reading-list-by-class` and `patrons-without-loans` are removed. On the calendar a notable date marks the day's number itself (extra bold, in the tertiary colour, underlined) instead of a coloured line under the cell, the owner's choice among six marks drawn; the legend shows a sample number. **The top bar keeps one height** (owner's choice among the layouts drawn): from tablets up it is one row — back arrow, icon, title cut short with its tooltip (two lines at most on a phone), search (a button below `xl`), print basket, account (its name from `xl`) — and the page's views and actions move to a toolbar under it (`PageHeader views`/`actions`: a card with the switch between views on the first row and the buttons on the second, no line between them; on record and patron pages the actions are in the record card); no breadcrumbs. **Views never look like actions**: `ChoiceButtons` (the area pages of `AreaTabs`, the kind filter of Liste, the form/JSON and design/code switches) and the Skeleton tabs of the records are round pills in one grey track, the current one tinted with a blue border (with a tick in `ChoiceButtons`), on one line that scrolls sideways on a phone with the current one in sight; actions stay buttons, the main one filled. The sidebar is narrower on tablets (16rem, 18rem from `lg`). The print lists of the demo data each print within the public demo's cap of sheets (the teachers' cards and the series' labels were refused): ten teachers, the first 24 books of the series.
  - Round 25: the switches between views take the buttons' corners and a little more room between the pills; the page's actions sit on the right (the toolbar's second row, the bottom right of the record card); the calendar page keeps "Nuova data" and loses "Modifica chiusure" (the closures are edited only in Amministrazione → Configurazione → Calendario); the record and patron pages show the kind of page in the top bar ("Scheda del libro", "Scheda dell'utente": a text that never changes with the record) and the record's title, subtitle and facts in their card, the title being the page's heading; the dashboard opens with the quick actions above the pinned notes, and its tiles with the books loaned and returned today first; the administration home keeps the configuration and the jobs on the data (accounts, activity log, backups, imports, exports, class upgrade), while Aspetto e lingua (where the library's logo is uploaded), Classi and the print templates move into Configurazione, whose overview shows the editors ("Aspetto, classi e modelli": appearance, classes, classification, print templates, reports) apart from the settings of each section; the read-only calendar card goes (the desk's tabs and the dashboard lead there).
  - Round 26 (an upright tablet, 834 px): the record card puts the cover beside the title and the facts only from `lg` (above them on a tablet, where they were left a narrow column); the bar's title is 1.5rem below `lg`, so "Scheda del libro" fits; the page's actions sit on the views' row, on the right, when the buttons fit there, and go to a row of their own otherwise (a narrow window, the desk's six pages).
  - Round 33: the checkout desk in one field and two steps (the card or a part of the name, then the books; a patron at the loan limit gets a dialog in the middle of the page, never a passing toast; the renewal button reads "Rinnova"); the patron searches find a part of the name anywhere, from three characters; code fields open the keyboard with letters (no numeric keyboard, no keyboard button); the overdue notices and the other slips draw their margin on every page, so the browser always prints without margins as for labels; on a phone the window shrinks above the keyboard, the bottom bar steps aside while the keyboard is open and a dialog taller than what is left scrolls; Aspetto e lingua in the order name, logo, language, area style, colour set, dashboard, the colour set in a card with its own button; a colour set tried and not saved (refused in the public demo, or the page left) no longer stays on the page.
  - Round 34: About without description, maintainer, site, the note on the generated list and the "Ruolo" column; the idle timeout of the connections is 120 s.
  - Round 35: the quick actions of the dashboard named as the tab or button they open (Prestiti, Restituzioni…), with the same i18n key.
  - Round 36: the checkout desk starts over from its own tab and the limit notice has its "Fine"; data loading show the spinner with "Caricamento…" instead of a line of text.
  - Round 37: the print lists' area is named **Stampe** again (menu, page, the dashboard's quick action), with the printer icon. The bulk change of copies leaves Stampe for **Catalogo → Liste** (`/catalog/lists`, the tab before Inventario): the same lists of copies, printed only from Stampe and changed only from the catalogue (`ListIndex` and `ListDetail` with a `purpose`). The texts of the interface take a plainer register: instructions in the infinitive ("Leggere la tessera"), states described impersonally, no second or first person, no "Benvenuto" (the owner's choices on a page of fifty texts, the same rule for the rest).
  - Round 38: the top bar loses the catalogue's search box (each area searches in its own page) and gains the **help icon** (9.5b begun: `#lib/ui/help-links.ts`, the manual of the area on `docs.biblioviva.org` in the installation's version and language, `latest` for a build that is not a release). **One wait** in the whole app: `Loading` is the large turning icon with "Caricamento…", centred across and down the page (`md` in a box or a dialog), and the wait overlay shows the same over the page's part of the window only, the menu and the bars left in sight (the whole window on the login). The checkout desk: the field only as wide as a code or a name needs, today's loans and returns in two boxes side by side under it; on a phone no box runs past the right edge any more (a grid without columns of its own has one column as wide as the page, `app.css`; fixed test first). The catalogue is for `CATALOG_READERS` (`loans`, `catalog`, `catalog.import`, `reports`): the cards' account no longer sees it nor the lists of books. Catalogo → Liste is gone, and with it the bulk change of the copies of a list: the lists of Stampe only print (the copies change in bulk from the catalogue's selection), with "Nuova lista utenti" and "Nuova lista libri" by permission, the kind filter only for an account that sees both kinds, no "Duplica", and under the lists the classes, each with "Stampa tessere" (`?group=`); the census offers its patron lists before the classes. The search hints say what to search by, without the minimum of letters or how the search works.

- [x] **9.14 Series, and the most complete record first** (owner request, Q84, Q85)
  - Tasks: (a) **series** — `biblio.series` (text) and `biblio.series_number` (text: `3`, `3.5`, `II`), both optional, in the single regenerated migration (Q82): record form (a "Serie" field with suggestions from the series already in the catalogue, and "Numero"), record page ("Harry Potter · 3", a link that searches the series), catalogue search (FTS column `series`) and a "Serie" column in the catalogue grid, sorting by series then number (numeric when the number is numeric), catalogue CSV export and import (`serie`, `numero_serie`, optional columns), Koha import unchanged (the school's Koha has no series), the data-part archives and restore carry the columns, the print templates get `{{series}}` and `{{seriesNumber}}`. Metadata fills them only from a real series: the UNIMARC `461` set of SBN (`$a` title, `$v` volume: "Il Signore degli anelli ; 2"); the `225`/`410` of SBN hold the publisher's *collana* (e.g. "Il battello a vapore. Serie azzurra ; 45") and are ignored by the owner's choice (Q84), and so are Open Library's edition `series` (mostly the collana too, "Puffin books") and Google Books' `seriesInfo` (an id without a name); the catalogue CSV import splits "Harry Potter ; 3" when there is no number column (`#lib/shared/series.ts`). (b) **record choice** — `z3950.maxRecordsIsbn` 5 → 20 (one ISBN has a record per reprint on SBN: 9788884510495 has 7, 2001–2008); every candidate gets a completeness score (`#lib/server/metadata/score.ts`: title, author, publisher, place, year, physical description, Dewey, subjects, ISBN, with an empty or malformed field costing its points) and the candidates are ordered by it, ties to the most recent year; an **ISBN** lookup opens the form at once with the best one and says "Scheda più completa tra N" with "Altre schede" back to the list; a **title/author** search keeps the list, ordered by completeness (Q85).
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the score, the ordering and the 461 series mapping (and for 225/410 left out); service tests for series in create/update/search/export/import; e2e: fast add with the mock provider opens the form with the most complete of several records, "Altre schede" shows them all; a series typed on one record is suggested on the next and the record page shows it; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.13

Table `wish` (`title`, `author`, `series`, `series_number`, `note`, `patron_id` → the patron who asked, nullable, set to NULL when the patron is removed/anonymized, `requests` ≥ 1, `created_at`/`updated_at`, `created_by`), in the single regenerated migration (Q82), in `docs/database.md`, in the `catalog` data part of backups/restores (with its staff column), audit rows without personal data. Service `#lib/server/services/wishlist.ts` (list with search/sort, create — at least one of title, author, series; an exact duplicate (same normalised title, author, series) adds a request instead of a second row —, update, add a request, delete, fulfil, `similar` = the matching catalogue records (with availability) and wishes). Page `/wishlist` for accounts with `loans` or `catalog`: a tab of the circulation desk area (and of the catalogue area for accounts without `loans`); an add form (title, author, series with the catalogue's suggestions, number, note, "Chi l'ha chiesto" through the desk patron picker — only for accounts that see patrons), the "already in the catalogue / already wished" warning under the form while typing, the grid (title, author, series · n, requests, requester — only with `loans`/`patrons` —, note, date) with search and sort, row click to edit, selection actions "+1 richiesta", "Cataloga" (with `catalog`: opens "Nuovo libro" with title, author, series and number prefilled, the wish removed when the record is saved) and "Elimina", CSV/ODS export without the requester. `docs/navigation.md` updated.

Kept in sync by a test that fails when a page route is missing from the map or the map names a route that no longer exists. - [x] **9.16 A colour for every area in the top bar** (owner request, design chosen from the proposals page: hybrid colours, three styles; carried out **after Phase 12**) — the operator must see at once which area they are in: the top bar takes a colour of its own for each area of the navigation (Circolazione, Catalogo, Utenti, Stampe, Report, Amministrazione; the area of a page is the `NAV_ITEMS` entry its path belongs to, `isCurrent`), clearly distinct from one area to the next and **in tune with the chosen colour set without losing the difference**. **Colours (hybrid, owner choice):** every area has a **fixed hue**, the same in every colour set, so the colour of an area is learnt once — Prestiti indigo (OKLCH hue 270), Catalogo yellow (105, lightness +0.06), Utenti jade green (175, lightness +0.03; both moved towards yellow and green at the owner's request), Stampe raspberry (0, lightness −0.07), Report violet (320, lightness +0.035), Amministrazione anthracite (hue 255, chroma 0.03, lightness −0.14) —, while the **chroma and lightness follow the set**: chroma = the set's primary chroma × 0.9 within 0.10…0.17, plus half of the set's lightness shift (vivid sets give vivid bands, the soft and grey sets gentler ones, never below the floor). The hues and offsets were chosen by maximising the smallest perceptual distance between the areas and from the status colours (red 30, amber 78, green 150): measured on the 15 sets, the closest two areas of a set stay at an OKLab distance of 0.09–0.15 and no area comes near an error, warning or success colour; a unit test keeps both bounds (≥ 0.08 between areas, ≥ 0.07 from the status colours) for every set. The **dashboard stays neutral** (no area colour: the set's own look, its primary only on the page icon), which also avoids it matching Prestiti in the default Oceano set. The area tones are generated with the theme (`#lib/shared/theme-palette.ts`, `bun run themes:generate`) as CSS tokens (`--bv-area-<area>-*`: tint, strong, deep) and applied through a `data-area` attribute on the shell. **Three styles, chosen in the configuration** (`ui.areaStyle`: `tinted` default, `filled`, `accents`, or `none` for the neutral bar of today without area colours; owner: the three styles and the "no colour" option as one setting, replacing the on/off switch planned before): **tinted** — a light tint of the area as the bar background with a strong band of the area's colour along its bottom edge; **filled** — the whole bar in the area's deep shade with white text; **accents** — the bar neutral as today with a band of the area's colour along its left edge. In every style the page icon is filled with the area's colour and the sidebar's current item (and the phone's bottom bar) match it; white on the area colour keeps at least 3:1 (icons) and the white text of the filled bar 4.5:1. Colour is never the only cue (§3.18): the area's icon and name stay in the bar (the proposals page shows two areas can come close for colour-blind viewers). `ui.areaStyle` is edited on the Appearance page with a live preview of the three styles, is in the coverage map and the configuration archive; the colour swatches of the Appearance page and of the wizard preview the area colours of each set.
  - Acceptance: unit tests on the generated tokens for all fifteen sets: text and icons on every area background reach WCAG AA (4.5:1), every pair of areas within a set differs by a clear minimum distance in OKLab (ΔE threshold fixed in the test), every area colour keeps a minimum hue distance from the status tones; the committed theme files match the generator; e2e: each area shows its `data-area` and its band colour, the dashboard none, and each of the three styles chosen on the Appearance page changes the bar, `none` removes the area colours; visual snapshots of the bar in the three styles and in two sets (one vivid, one grey); axe on the main pages of every area.
  - Depends on: Phase 12, 2.3a, 11.13

- [x] **9.17 Public demo behind a Cloudflare Tunnel, reset every day** (owner request, Q111, Q112, Q118, Q119, Q121, ADR-028, ADR-035; not required for 0.9.0, linked from the presentation site 9.7) — a public instance anyone can try with the synthetic demo data, never the school's installation (which stays on the tailnet, Q21, Q111).
  - Context: a tunnel (`cloudflared`) reaches Cloudflare from inside the host, so no port is opened on the router and the browser gets HTTPS (the camera flows work); Cloudflare ends TLS, so it sees the traffic in clear, which is acceptable only because every datum of the demo is synthetic. Every visitor shares the same data and the same accounts, so the demo must not let one visitor break it for the others.
  - Tasks:
    - **Host: an Oracle Cloud Always Free virtual machine** (Q119): one `VM.Standard.A1.Flex` (Arm, within the Always Free allowance of 2 OCPU and 12 GB, a 50 GB boot volume) with Ubuntu Server and Docker, created in the home region of the account; no port opened in the VCN's security list or the host firewall except SSH from the administrator's address (the tunnel connects out). The account is upgraded to pay-as-you-go while staying inside the Always Free resources, with a budget alert at 1 €, so the instance is not reclaimed for being idle (an Always Free tenancy reclaims a machine whose CPU, network and memory stay under 20 % for a week) [VERIFY with Oracle's documentation when the demo is set up]; the multi-arch image of 9.6 runs on it as on a Raspberry Pi. When no A1 capacity is available, the host is a `VM.Standard.E2.1.Micro` (AMD, 1 GB, also Always Free) with a 2 GB swap file. The instance has an ephemeral public IPv4 address (free; without one it would need a NAT gateway, which is not free, to reach `ghcr.io` and Cloudflare). The app keeps the `small` profile and the container is limited to 512 MB and 1 CPU, as on a Raspberry Pi (`DEMO_MEM_LIMIT`, `DEMO_CPUS` in `.env`), so the demo behaves like a school's usual host and fits either shape.
    - `docker/compose.demo.yml`: the app container and a `cloudflared` sidecar (`cloudflare/cloudflared:<exact tag>`, a named tunnel whose token comes from `.env` as `TUNNEL_TOKEN`, ingress to the app's port, `demo.biblioviva.org` on the domain managed by Cloudflare); the app runs with `PROTOCOL_HEADER=x-forwarded-proto`, the client address from `ADDRESS_HEADER=cf-connecting-ip` (otherwise the login limiter of §3.5 sees every visitor as the tunnel and five wrong passwords lock everyone out) and the host from the `Host` header, which cloudflared keeps (`HOST_HEADER=host`); `BODY_SIZE_LIMIT=4M`, `mem_limit`, `cpus`, `restart: unless-stopped` and a health check on `/healthz`; `TUNNEL_TOKEN`, `DEMO_RESET_AT`, `DEMO_NOTES_PER_DAY`, `DEMO_MEM_LIMIT` and `DEMO_CPUS` in `docker/.env.example`; the host holds only this file and its `.env`, never the repository.
    - **Daily reset inside the container** (`DEMO_RESET_AT`, default `04:00` in `library.timezone`, Q112, Q119), so it works the same on any host with no timer of its own: with `DEMO_PUBLIC=1` the server **builds the demo at every start, before it opens the database or reads the configuration** (`#lib/server/demo/build.ts`: both volumes emptied, then the wizard's choices of Q101 with the default preset in Italian, the demo data of 9.12 and 12.8 seeded with the library's today so the loans stay current, the demo class picture of Q109, the generic library logo `demo/library-logo.png`, a copy of `docs/reference/demo/library-logo.png`; a few seconds); a folder that holds files without the demo's marker `.biblioviva-public-demo` is refused and the server stops, so the flag set by mistake on a school's volume never empties it. At the reset time the server exits, so `restart: unless-stopped` starts it again on a fresh demo (a few seconds of "service restarting" for whoever is on the page then). The first-run wizard never shows; the scheduled backups do not run.
    - **Demo mode** (`DEMO_PUBLIC=1` in `src/env.ts`, distinct from `DEMO`, which only pre-ticks the wizard's demo data): no band on the app's pages (the login page says it all, owner review); the usual login card on `/login`, with a box under it (i18n keys `common.demoPublic.*` in both locales): what BiblioViva is, what the demo holds (the counts of the demo data, made-up names and photos, the nightly reset, the configuration not saved), each account with its area's icon and colour and what it is for, and the one password `biblioviva`, typed in the form like anywhere else; `bun run demo` starts it locally on folders of its own (`local/demo/`, port 5174); `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` and a `robots.txt` that disallows everything; every staff session expires at the reset; the menu and Informazioni show as version the time of the build, `yyyy.mm.dd.hhmm` on the library's clock (the demo follows the main branch; archives and `/healthz` keep the release number); Amministrazione does not offer "Svuota dati dimostrativi".
    - **Blocked in demo mode** (one guard, `assertNotDemo(feature)` in `#lib/server/demo/public.ts`, checked by the services; the refusal reaches the browser as a notification in the style of Appendix I that says what is off in the demo and why, answered as `403` (never a `500`; a print sheet or a download opened in a tab of its own gets a small page with the same words and a link back to the app, `#lib/server/demo/refusal.ts`); the lists of Q112 and Q118): changing staff passwords and accounts; importing a configuration archive, restoring a backup and the manual backups (the class upgrade keeps its backup, one copy at a time, so visitors cannot fill the disk); "Svuota dati dimostrativi"; class pictures other than the shipped demo picture (the other pictures are replaced, below) and files to import beyond a size cap; downloading an archive or a backup (Esporta tutto, the chosen parts, a stored backup, the configuration archive: the buttons say so instead); sheets of labels or cards over 3 sheets of paper (`DEMO_PRINT_MAX_SHEETS`, refused before rendering: a sheet gives the turn to other requests while it is rendered, but a sheet of every copy would still take seconds of the demo's one CPU and megabytes of its memory from every visitor); the visitor's address stays in memory for the limits and is never written in the sessions or the audit log; the metadata and cover lookups run on `METADATA_PROVIDER=mock` (no outbound calls, so the demo is never a public proxy to OPAC SBN or the cover providers).
    - **Administration visible, saves blocked** (Q118): every page of Amministrazione opens and can be tried — the configuration sections, the classification editor, the template designer and code view, the report editor with its preview, the classes, the calendar — but every write of `CONFIG_DIR` (a section, the taxonomy, a template folder or asset, a stock, a report definition, the logo, the theme, a language pack) is refused by the guard of the configuration writes (`applyConfigChange`, through which every writer of the history goes, one check) and a "Salva" is answered by the notification that saving is off in the demo. Changes to the library's data (patrons, books, loans, classes of the database, print lists, inventory) stay allowed and are shared by every visitor until the reset (Q118).
    - **Reports in SQL off** (Q118): with no way to interrupt a statement (9.20), an SQL report that never ends would freeze the server for everyone, so in demo mode an SQL definition is never saved, uploaded or previewed; the SQL tab shows the compiled SQL of a guided report read-only, and the guided reports run as usual (their pieces are bounded).
    - **What a visitor sends is replaced by an example** (Q121, ADR-035): every flow can be tried to the end, but the server never keeps the content a visitor sends. One place in the services, on the model of `assertNotDemo`: `demoText(kind, sent, stored)`, `demoPatron`, `demoBiblio`, `demoItem` and `demoPicture()` in `#lib/server/demo/content.ts`, so it holds for a direct call of a remote function too; outside the demo they return what was sent. A value equal to the stored one is not the visitor's and stays (editing a copy of a seeded book keeps its title).
      - Pictures (patron photos, covers taken or uploaded, the photos of the ZIP import, the pictograms of the classification): the bytes received are dropped and the demo's placeholder is stored, with the date and time of the save drawn on it (a bitmap font of the module, no new library). A provider's cover sent by the browser is fetched again on the server; class pictures stay the shipped one only.
      - Staff notes: the text becomes "DEMO – segnalazione del <data e ora>"; colour and "In evidenza" stay as chosen; at most `DEMO_NOTES_PER_DAY` new notes per address and day.
      - Patrons: first name and surname from the generator of fake names of the demo data; email and external id emptied; a note or a desk note "Nota demo del <data e ora>".
      - Records: the title "Libro demo del <data e ora>", the author "AA. VV." and the publisher "s.n." (the special values of `catalog.*`), the other free texts emptied; a value of the mock provider's records (`MOCK_RECORDS`) is kept, so "Nuovo libro" from a lookup stores the provider's data. Copies: the call number generated from the configured format, the volume emptied, a note "Nota demo del …".
      - Classes and teachers groups, print lists, inventories (name, notes, shelf outside the shelves of the catalogue), wishes (title, author "AA. VV.", note) and the reasons of a forced loan, a lost copy or a change of status: "Classe demo del …", "Lista demo del …", "Inventario demo del …", "Desiderata demo del …", "Motivo demo del …"; a section of one or two letters or digits stays (the classes "A", "B" of "1 A"…"5 D").
      - CSV imports of students, teachers and the catalogue accept only the demo's example file, generated with the demo (`DATA_DIR/demo/imports/`, valid against its classes and classification) and recognised by its SHA-256; in the demo the template download of each import gives that file. The loans import, made of card numbers, barcodes and dates only, stays free. A stored upload is named after the example.
      - Free: codes from closed lists (item types, classification, levels, classes), dates, numbers (year, number in the series), barcodes and card numbers validated by the format table, ISBN, ISSN, EAN, Dewey numbers of their shape.
      - The visitor is told: the login box says "Nella demo il testo e le foto vengono sostituiti da un esempio", and a save that replaced something answers with the header `X-BiblioViva-Demo-Replaced`, which the browser turns into the same notification.
    - Limits for a public host: a per-address request limit on writes (60 a minute) and on the heavy downloads (exports, archives, print sheets: 20 a minute; `429` with `Retry-After` over it, `#lib/server/demo/limits.ts`), on top of the login limiter, the row caps unchanged, and the image and `yaz` concurrency of the `small` profile; Cloudflare's own protection in front. `compose.demo.yml` sets `mem_limit` and `cpus` for the app, `restart: unless-stopped` and a health check on `/healthz`, so a stuck server comes back on its own. The e2e run through the tunnel includes a few visitors at once (two phones and a desktop) to check that the Pi stays responsive.
    - `docs/demo.md` (English, technical): creating the Oracle Cloud account and the Always Free instance (Arm, or the 1 GB AMD shape with swap), its public address, the pay-as-you-go upgrade with the budget alert, the security list and SSH (links to Oracle's official pages); Docker on Ubuntu (link to Docker's official page); the domain on Cloudflare and creating the tunnel in Cloudflare's dashboard (links to the official pages); starting the stack; updating the demo to a new release; what to check when Oracle changes the Always Free allowance.
  - Acceptance: unit tests of the guard (every blocked feature refused in the service with `DEMO_PUBLIC=1`, allowed without; every configuration writer refused; an SQL report refused, a guided one previewed; the notes limit per address), of the next reset time across a DST change, of the address taken from `cf-connecting-ip`, of the per-address limits; the demo built in empty folders as the wizard would, built again over a visitor's changes with the same data, a folder without the marker refused untouched; unit tests of the replacement (every kind, a value unchanged kept, the mock provider's values kept, nothing changed outside the demo, the placeholder decoded with its text, every service of the list storing the example), of the example files (generated valid against the demo, any other file refused); e2e with `DEMO_PUBLIC=1`: the reset time and the credentials on `/login`, no band on the app's pages, a patron saved under a fake name with the notification, a note stored as the demo's text, a photo stored as the placeholder, an own CSV refused and the example file imported, a record from the mock lookup kept, `/setup` absent, the administration pages open with "Salva" refused and its notification, an SQL report refused, a note made and seen by a second visitor (another address) and the notes limit per address, `noindex` and `robots.txt`; a server started on a school's folder stops without touching it, and the reset time makes the process exit cleanly (unit test with a fake clock, e2e with a server of its own, then the restart in the compose stack on the host); `compose.demo.yml` validated with `docker compose config` and run once on the Oracle instance through a real tunnel (login, checkout, census with the demo picture from a phone on mobile data, a reset seen happen); `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 8.4, 8.8, 9.12, 12.8, Q109; links: 9.7

- [x] **9.18 Segnalazioni: staff notes like post-its** (owner request, Q113, ADR-029; required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: the staff need a place to leave short messages to one another and to the administrator ("lo scanner del banco non legge", "manca il toner", "copia trovata rovinata sullo scaffale"), and the administrator a place for notices everyone sees on opening the app. A note is a post-it: written once, read by everyone, taken down by the administrator.
  - Tasks:
    - Table `note` (`id`, `body` 1–500 characters with its line breaks, `color` one of `yellow` (default), `pink`, `green`, `blue`, `orange`, `pinned` boolean, `created_by` → `staff_user`, `created_at`, `archived_at` and `archived_by` → `staff_user`, both null while the note is open; a check keeps an archived note unpinned), in the single regenerated migration (Q82) and in `docs/database.md`; a new data part **`notes`** of backups, restores and exports (11.12) with its staff columns; audit rows `note.create`, `note.pin`, `note.unpin`, `note.archive`, `note.restore`, `note.delete` with the note's id only, never its text.
    - Service `#lib/server/services/notes.ts`: the open notes (pinned first, then the newest), the archived ones (the most recently archived first, 48 at a time), create (every account; `pinned` only with `admin`), pin and unpin, archive (unpins) and restore (back among the open notes, not pinned), delete for good (archived notes only), the pinned notes for the home pages and the count of open notes. **Nobody edits a note** (owner, Q113): a wrong one is archived and written again.
    - Remote functions `#lib/remote/notes.remote.ts`: listing and creating need a staff session only (`requireUser()`); pin, unpin, archive, restore and delete need `admin`; a `pinned` note sent by an account without `admin` is refused.
    - Navigation: the area **Segnalazioni** (`/notes`, Lucide `sticky-note`, one icon for the concept in `#lib/ui/icons.ts`) between Stampe and Report, shown to every account (no permission); the seventh area of §3.18; on phones under "Altro". Its top-bar colour (9.16): **petrol blue**, fixed hue 210 with lightness −0.06, chosen with the same search as the other areas (across the 15 sets the nearest area stays at OKLab 0.10 and the nearest status colour at 0.14; white text and icons keep their contrast), covered by the 9.16 unit test and the area colours reference page.
    - Page `/notes`: `PageHeader` with "Nuova segnalazione" (primary); `AreaTabs` **Aperte** / **Archiviate** (the archive is readable by everyone); the notes as **post-it tiles** in a responsive grid (one column on phones, up to four on a wide desktop): the note's colour as background, a folded corner, a slight alternating tilt with no hover effect (owner review), the text with its line breaks (long text clamped, "Mostra tutto" opens it), the account's display name and the date and time at the bottom; pinned notes first with a pin icon and "In evidenza" (icon and text, colour never the only cue, §3.18). Administrator actions on each tile: "Metti in evidenza" / "Togli dall'evidenza", "Archivia"; in the archive, who archived it and when, "Ripristina" and "Elimina" (with a confirmation dialog). The new-note dialog: a text area with a character counter, the five colours as named swatches (a radio group), "In evidenza sulla dashboard" for administrators only, and the hint "Le segnalazioni le leggono tutti gli account: non scrivere dati personali degli alunni." `EmptyState` when there are none. The post-it colours are fixed light tints, the same in every colour set, with text contrast ≥ 4.5:1.
    - Home pages: the open pinned notes appear **at the top of the dashboard** (before the quick actions) as post-its under the heading "Segnalazioni" with the pin icon, without the "In evidenza" mark (every note there is pinned; owner review), always (no dashboard switch: the administrator decides by pinning), and at the top of the checkout desk for the accounts that open on the desk instead of the dashboard (`loans` only, 4.9) as a compact strip. Accounts with `admin` get a dashboard tile "Segnalazioni aperte" (the count, a link to `/notes`).
    - Seed: a few synthetic notes (one pinned) in the demo data (9.12), so the demo and the tests have content; the public demo (9.17) keeps notes writable, the daily reset clears them.
    - i18n `notes.json` in both languages (Italian "Segnalazioni", English "Notes"); `docs/navigation.md` (tree, route index, user paths), `docs/database.md`; the note tile in `/dev/ui`.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the service (empty and too long text, unknown colour, `pinned` refused without `admin`, archive unpins, restore, delete only archived notes, order of the lists, audit rows without the text) and for the data part (round trip alone); the 9.16 colour tests with the new area; e2e: an operator account writes a note with a colour and sees it with no administrator action, and the remote functions refuse them; the administrator pins a note → it is at the top of the dashboard and, for the `prestiti` account, at the top of the desk; unpin and archive → gone from both, listed under Archiviate; restore; delete; on the phone viewport; axe clean; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.16

- [x] **9.19 Imports: English columns, templates with the chosen columns, students and teachers apart** (owner request, Q115; required for 0.9.0, carried out before 9.3 and the manuals of 9.5)
  - Context: the column names of a file are code, and code is English; the operator chooses which columns a file carries, and the template is generated with those only. What is required depends on the kind of import, and a key that is left out is generated or replaced by another way of matching, which the wizard says.
  - Tasks:
    - **Column catalogue** `#lib/shared/import-columns.ts`: for each import (students, teachers, catalogue, loans) the columns with their English header (`snake_case`, the only accepted spelling, compared without case and with spaces or hyphens read as `_`), their group (**required**, **key**, **optional**), the i18n label and hint, and for a key what happens when it is missing. A header cell outside the catalogue is an ignored column, never an alias: the Italian and Koha alias tables go, and the Koha queries in `docs/import/` output the English names. A file without a header is mapped by hand, except the student shapes `first_name;last_name;level;section` (4 columns) and `first_name;last_name;class` (3 columns) and the teacher shape `first_name;last_name` (2 columns), recognised by their count as today.
    - **Students** (`/admin/imports/students`, `patrons.import`): required `first_name`, `last_name` and the class, either **one column** `class` (`3A`, `3 A`, `3ª A`: matched against the labels of the existing classes, built from the `class_label` template of each group and level, ignoring case, spaces and the ordinal mark) or **two columns** `level` + `section` (as today: a level code, label or name, and the name of a group that hosts it); keys `card_number` (missing → generated), `external_id` (missing → students are matched by name and class); optional `email`. Modes "Aggiungi e aggiorna" and "Sincronizza studenti" as today.
    - **Teachers** (`/admin/imports/teachers`, `patrons.import`, a page of its own): required `first_name`, `last_name`; keys as for students; optional `email`, `group` (a teachers group; missing → the default teachers group). Modes "Aggiungi e aggiorna" and "Sincronizza docenti" (missing teachers offered for removal with the `anonymize` permission, as for students). No `patron_type` column: the page decides it.
    - **Catalogue** (`/admin/imports/catalog`): required `title`, `author`, `publisher` (an empty cell is an error unless "usa i valori speciali" is ticked, as today); optional `classification` (a genre or class code; the kind follows from it, so the separate `kind`, `genre_code` and `class_code` columns go, and the configuration check refuses a code that is both a genre and a class; missing or empty → the title goes to the list of books to classify of 9.21, and the preview counts them); keys `biblio_key` (missing → copies grouped by ISBN, else one title per row) and `barcode` (missing → generated in the current format, labels offered for printing); optional the other fields of §4.
    - **Loans** (`/admin/imports/loans`): required `card_number` and `barcode` (they name a patron and a copy that exist, nothing can be generated); optional `issued_on` (missing → today) and `due_on` (missing → computed as at the desk from the loan rules and the calendar). A copy of a book still to classify is accepted with a warning (the loan already exists on the shelf of the old system; only the desk refuses it, 9.21).
    - **Template generator**: on every import page a "Scarica modello" card lists the columns by group, the required ones ticked and locked, every key with its consequence when left out ("le tessere saranno generate"), the class choice for students (one column or two); the choice is remembered by the browser. `…/template.csv?columns=…` (validated against the catalogue) returns the header only (no sample rows that could be imported by mistake), `;`, BOM, `students-template.csv`, `teachers-template.csv`, `catalog-template.csv`, `loans-template.csv`.
    - **Mapping and preview**: `ColumnMapper` shows the columns of the import with their group; the mapping is complete when every required column (or the class alternative) is mapped, and the message names the missing ones. A missing key is announced at the mapping step and counted in the preview ("38 tessere saranno generate", "120 codici copia saranno generati: etichette da stampare", "nessuna chiave titolo: le copie sono raggruppate per ISBN").
    - **Fixes, test first**: an empty `card_number` cell of a matched patron keeps the card (it cleared it); the "mapping incomplete" message is per import; the "file too large" message gives the limit of the import; an unknown class lists the valid classes of that level (or all of them for the one-column form); `docs/import/README.md` describes the classes of Phase 12 (not `maxClassYear` and `library.sections`); the admin guides lead to Amministrazione → Importazioni (not "Utenti → Importa da CSV").
    - **Exports in the formats of the imports** (7.3) follow, so an exported file goes back in unchanged: `students` and `teachers` files instead of one patrons file (`level` + `section` for the students, `group` for the teachers), the catalogue with `classification` instead of `kind`, `genre_code` and `class_code`, the open loans as `card_number;barcode;issued_on;due_on`, and the loan history with English headers too.
    - Docs: `docs/import/README.md` and the samples with English headers, the Koha queries, the admin guides, `docs/navigation.md` (the two patron pages and the hub), Q97.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the catalogue (unknown header ignored, no alias accepted, required sets, generated template headers for every choice, invalid `columns` refused), the one-column class (labels with and without spaces, ordinal mark, pluriclasse, unknown class with the list), teachers (group given, missing, unknown), loans without dates (today, due date from the rules), the empty card cell, the per-import messages, the counted notices; e2e: generate a student template with one class column and import it, a teacher import, a catalogue file without `barcode` and `biblio_key` (notices, labels offered), loans without `due_on`, the template download for each import; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.13, 9.21

- [x] **9.20 Reports: guided editor, pivot tables, built-ins reviewed** (owner request, Q116; required for 0.9.0, carried out after 9.19 and before 9.3 and the manuals of 9.5)
  - Context: an administrator must be able to change a report or define a new one without writing SQL, including a table with rows and columns and their totals, such as loans of the period by class year (rows) and section (columns); the built-in reports follow the principle of the templates: they can be copied and changed, and new ones added.
  - Tasks:
    - **Pivot tables** in the engine: a definition may carry `pivot` (`rows`: one or more column keys, `columns`: one key, `value`: one key, optional `columnOrder` key, `totals`: `none` | `rows` | `columns` | `both`); the SQL returns long rows, the engine builds the columns from the values found (ordered by `columnOrder`, then by value), fills missing cells with 0 and adds the totals; a row without a value for the column dimension (teachers in a class year × section table: they have no section) counts in the row total only. Grid, print view, CSV/ODS export and chart (stacked bars, one series per column value) show the pivoted table.
    - **Guided definitions**: instead of `sql`, a definition may carry `query`: `source` (`loans` from the class snapshots of the loan, `items`, `patrons` from the live data), `filters` (each fixed or asked at run time, which makes it a parameter: period, school year, patron type, stage, level, group, item type, classification kind, reading level, item status), `rows` and an optional `columns` dimension (class year, section, class, stage, patron type, month, weekday, school year, genre or class, reading level, item type, item status, decade of publication, acquisition year), one `measure` (loans, distinct readers, titles, copies, patrons, average loan days), `chart`. Teachers are one row "Docenti" of the class-year dimension and have no section; books still to classify (9.21) are one value "Da classificare" of the classification dimension. The SQL is generated on the server from a fixed catalogue of dimensions and measures (`#lib/server/reports/builder/`) and goes through the same checks and the read-only connection.
    - **Editor** `/admin/config/reports/<id>` (`admin`): tabs **Guidata** and **SQL** over the same file; a live preview runs the draft (200 rows); a guided report shows its generated SQL read-only, and "Modifica come SQL" turns it into an SQL report after a confirmation (never the other way); the SQL tab edits titles in every language, category, parameters, columns, chart, pivot and the SQL, with the validation messages next to the field. Saving validates, then tries the report on the read-only connection — compiled and run with its defaults (a sample for a required parameter without one) — and writes one configuration change (history, archive) only when SQLite accepts it; an error is shown with SQLite's message and nothing is saved, and a JSON part that does not parse blocks the save.
    - **Library** `/admin/config/reports`: on a built-in "Modifica" creates the customised copy with the same `id` ("Torna all'originale" removes it), "Duplica" creates a new report from any report, "Nuovo report" starts a guided one; upload and download stay.
    - **Built-ins reviewed**: removed `active-loans` (the open-loans page), `pending-labels` (the dashboard tile and the print basket), `patrons-without-card-or-photo` (the dashboard tiles and the census), `audit-summary` (the activity log page), `renewals-stats`; `loans-by-class-section` becomes the pivot of class year × section with both totals and the "Docenti" row, `patron-counts-by-class-section` the pivot of class year × section of active patrons, `loans-by-genre` becomes `loans-by-classification` (genres and classes); added `reading-index-by-class` (students, readers, share of readers, loans per student, per class), `loans-by-school-year` (by stage), `classification-by-level` (pivot genre or class × class year of the readers), `collection-age` (copies by decade of publication). Built-ins are written in the guided form wherever the builder can express them. The statistics page (6.4) keeps its panels with the new definitions.
    - Engine fixes: `performance.reportTimeoutMs` goes (`bun:sqlite` cannot interrupt a query, and a Worker stopped at the limit leaves its query running: the row cap and the read-only connection stay the limits; an installation that still has the key loads as before, the key is ignored); §3.11, Appendix B, step 6.3 (`loans-by-reading-level`, the code in `src/lib/server/reports/`) and `docs/reports-authoring.md` describe the format of the code (`level`/`stage`/`group` parameters, `doughnut` not `pie`, no `series`, `pivot`, `query`); `docs/navigation.md`.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the pivot (dynamic columns, order, missing cells, totals, export rows), the builder (every dimension and measure compiles and runs on the seeded primaria and comprensivo, fixed and asked filters, teachers row), the editor service (save, conversion to SQL, refusals), every built-in valid and bilingual on both seeds; e2e: build the report of class year × section with totals in the guided editor, run, print and export it; customise a built-in and return to the original; duplicate; convert to SQL and edit; the statistics page; axe clean; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.19

- [x] **9.21 Books to classify** (owner request, Q117; required for 0.9.0, carried out before 9.19)
  - Context: entering a book and classifying it can happen at two different times (an import without classification, a pile of new books entered at the desk and classified later by the librarian); a book without classification waits in a list until somebody classifies it, and its copies do not circulate meanwhile.
  - Tasks:
    - Data: `biblio.kind` may be null ("da classificare"), with `genre_code`, `class_code` and `reading_level` null too (the check constraint allows the null kind); an index on the unclassified records; `docs/database.md`.
    - Entry: the record form ("Nuovo libro", the edit form) and the fast add offer **"Classifica dopo"**: the record is saved without kind and classification; everything else is validated as usual. The catalogue import leaves a title without `classification` unclassified (9.19).
    - **Da classificare** (`/catalog/to-classify`, permission `catalog`): a third tab of the Catalogo area (Catalogo, Inventario, Da classificare), the unclassified records oldest first (title, author, publisher, ISBN, copies, entered on); **"Inizia a classificare"** opens a flow that proposes them one after another: the record summary (cover, title, author, publisher, series, subjects, notes), the classification choice of the record form (genre or class with their images, the reading level for fiction), the suggested call number, then "Salva e avanti" or "Salta"; at the end, the list again. The record page shows a "Da classificare" badge with a "Classifica" button. Classifying a record (in the flow or the record form) gives its copies without a call number the suggested one.
    - Desk: a copy of an unclassified record is refused at checkout with the new message `item_unclassified` of Appendix I (not forceable), with "Classifica" for an account with `catalog`; check-in, renewal of an existing loan and the inventory work as usual.
    - Printing: the barcode label prints; the spine band and the call number wait for the classification: a pair job prints the barcode label of an unclassified copy with the band's cell left blank, a spine job leaves it out, and the print dialog says how many copies wait for their band; "Segna come stampate" never marks a band that was not printed, and the band of such a copy is not pending until the record is classified, then it is (so the barcode label is not printed twice).
    - Dashboard: a tile "Libri da classificare" (count, opens the tab) in the `tiles` section, shown only when there are some.
    - Catalogue list: a filter "Da classificare"; the classification column reads "Da classificare". Reports and statistics show them as one value "Da classificare" (9.20). i18n in both languages; `docs/navigation.md`.
  - Acceptance: unit tests (save without classification from the form and the fast add, classify later, the null kind refused nowhere else, the checkout refusal, label jobs without the band, the counts); e2e: fast add with "Classifica dopo" → the book in the tab and the dashboard tile → the flow classifies two books and skips one → the desk refuses a copy of the skipped one and lends the others; phone viewport; axe clean; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.13

- [x] **9.22 The patron's email on their page, no phone** (owner request, Q122; required for 0.9.0, carried out before 9.3 and the manuals of 9.5)
  - Context: the email of a student or teacher is the address the school gives them (internal mail, no access from outside); it will carry the notices of 10.2. The phone is not needed.
