- [x] **9.23 Original title** (owner request, Q123; required for 0.9.0, carried out before 9.3 and the manuals of 9.5)
  - Context: the same book translated in different editions often carries different titles; the original title ties them together and lets the operator find the book by either.
  - Tasks:
    - Data: `biblio.original_title` (text, nullable, the length cap of the title) in the single regenerated migration (Q82), §4.1, `docs/database.md`; the FTS table gains the column (§4.1), weighted like the title.
    - Record form and fast add: "Titolo originale" under the title, with the hint "Solo se il libro è una traduzione"; the record page shows it in the details; the catalogue grid shows it beside the title, and the grid export has it as a column; the candidate cards of a lookup show it.
    - Metadata: `BiblioDraft.originalTitle` in every provider. SBN UNIMARC: 500$a only when 101$c is present and differs from 101$a (a translation), with $3 and $9 dropped and the sorting markers cleaned; the other providers fill it only from a field that really names the original work; old cache entries read as `null`. The mock provider gives the translated books an original title ("1984" → "Nineteen eighty-four", "Il piccolo principe" → "Le petit prince"). Appendix D gains the 500/101 row.
    - Import and export: the optional column `original_title` in the catalogue import (`#lib/shared/import-columns.ts`, the length cap of the form) and in the catalogue export, so an exported file goes back unchanged; the Koha query in `docs/import/` reads 500$a from the MARCXML when 101$c differs from 101$a.
    - Everywhere a title is used to find a book: the catalogue search (9.24), the wishlist's similar books (the duplicate check of "Nuovo libro" compares ISBNs only).
    - Demo: `demoBiblio` treats it like the subtitle (Q121). Seed: a few translated titles with their original title, deterministic, no faker call.
    - Docs: §4.1, Appendix D, `docs/import/README.md`, `docs/database.md`.
  - Acceptance: unit tests (mapping on the recorded SBN files: "1984" and "Le mille e una notte" give their original title, the English Harry Potter editions none, the Polish one its English title; the import and export round trip; the FTS finds a record by its original title; the demo replacement); e2e: a lookup fills the original title, edit it by hand, find the book by it; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.19

- [x] **9.24 Search that forgives typing mistakes** (owner request, Q124; required for 0.9.0, carried out before 9.3 and the manuals of 9.5)
  - Context: a title or a name typed with a mistake finds nothing today, because every word must be the prefix of a word of the record. The search must find the closest books and patrons, every word counting, and put first those where every word is found.
  - Tasks:
    - **Survey first** (owner): the existing JavaScript components for approximate search (MiniSearch, uFuzzy, Fuse.js, fuzzysort, FlexSearch, Orama, fastest-levenshtein and any other found) compared with our own code over the FTS vocabulary, on the 10 000-record seed: the books found for a set of misspelt queries, time on the server (and the estimate on the `small` profile), memory, size, maintenance and a licence compatible with GPL-3.0-or-later. A library is adopted only if it does clearly better, with its ADR and §2; the outcome is recorded in an ADR either way. SQLite extensions are excluded (`spellfix1` is not in Bun's SQLite, and a native library per architecture goes against §2.8).
    - **Fields**: a book is searched by title, original title (9.23), author's surname, copy barcode and ISBN, and by the other fields of the FTS as today; a patron by first name, last name and card number. Mistakes are forgiven on title, original title, author's surname and the patrons' names; barcodes, ISBN and card numbers match exactly or as a prefix of digits.
    - **Order**: the exact search runs first (every word a prefix, as today). When it finds **fewer than 3** books or patrons, the words of at least 4 letters are corrected (one mistake up to 6 letters, two from 7; shorter words and articles never) and the approximate results follow: first those that contain every word, corrected or not, then those with fewer words, by the number of words found, then closeness, then relevance. Approximate results are marked "Simile" (icon and text) wherever results are listed.
    - **Books** (`#lib/server/services/catalog-search.ts`): the vocabulary of titles, original titles and authors read from the FTS index (`fts5vocab`) and kept in memory, refreshed when the catalogue changes; a whole copy barcode typed in the catalogue's search box opens its record (5.1). Used by the catalogue grid, the top-bar search and the wishlist's similar books; exports carry the exact results only.
    - **Patrons** (`#lib/server/services/patron-search.ts`): the name condition plus the card number (the patrons grid sends digits that today match nothing: fixed test first); the approximate pass reads the normalised names of the active patrons (≤ 1 000) and scores them in memory. Used by the patrons grid, the desk's picker, the census and the quick search.
    - One shared module for normalisation, the bounded edit distance and the word score (with its unit tests), used by both.
    - Budgets (§3.14): catalogue < 50 ms on 10 000 records and patrons < 20 ms on 1 000 in the tests, the approximate pass included.
    - Docs: steps 5.1 and 5.2, `docs/performance.md`.
  - Acceptance: unit tests (misspelt queries: "piccolo principie", "hary poter", "calvno cosmicomice", an original title with a mistake; the threshold of 3; every word before some; short words never corrected; barcode, ISBN and card number; "Rosii" finds Rossi, "de lcua" finds De Luca; the budgets); e2e: a misspelt title in the catalogue grid shows the book marked "Simile", a misspelt surname at the desk finds the patron, a card number in the patrons grid finds them; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.23

- [x] **9.25 Notable dates on the calendar** (owner request, Q125; required for 0.9.0, carried out before 9.3 and the manuals of 9.5)
  - Context: besides the closures, the staff need to mark dates that matter to the library — a reading in a class, the deadlines of the bookmark contest, a meeting with an author — and to see the next ones on opening the app.
  - Tasks:
    - Table `calendar_event` (`id`, `starts_on`, `ends_on` nullable and not before `starts_on`, `title` 1–120 characters, `notes` up to 500 with line breaks, `created_by` and `updated_by` → `staff_user`, timestamps) in the single regenerated migration (Q82), §4.7, `docs/database.md`; a data part of backups, restores and exports (11.12); audit rows `calendar_event.create`, `.update`, `.delete` with the id only, never the text.
    - Service `#lib/server/services/calendar-events.ts` (the dates of a range, the next ones from today in the library's time zone, create, update, delete); remote functions: reading needs a staff session, writing `requireUser('loans')`.
    - Page `/calendar`, readable by every account: the months of the school year as in the read-only calendar of 4.1, with the closures (listed under each month, days in a row closed for one reason on one line, "da … a …") and the notable dates (a coloured underline on the day, the list under each month: date or period, title, note); "Nuova data", and a date opens its dialog to edit or delete it, for accounts with `loans`. The read-only view `/admin/calendar` becomes this page (the administrator keeps "Modifica chiusure" to the configuration editor; the calendar's words move from the `admin` dictionary, loaded only under `/admin`, to `circulation.calendar`). Reached from the dashboard section, from Amministrazione and from a tab "Calendario" of the Prestiti area (shown to the accounts with `loans`).
    - Dashboard section **"Prossime date"** (`DASHBOARD_SECTIONS`, switchable in 11.15, on by default, coverage map): the dates of the next 30 days, at most five, with "Apri il calendario".
    - Demo: title and note replaced with an example (Q121, `#lib/server/demo/content.ts`). Seed (no faker call, relative to the seed's today): "Lettura in classe: «Il piccolo principe»", "Concorso del segnalibro: consegna dei disegni", "Concorso del segnalibro: premiazione", "Giornata mondiale del libro" (23 April), "Incontro con l'autore", and one period of a few days ("Mostra del libro").
    - i18n in both languages; `docs/navigation.md` (trees, route index, user paths); the date list in `/dev/ui`.
  - Acceptance: unit tests (validation: empty or long title, end before start; the next dates in order across a period; writing refused without `loans`; the data part round trip; audit rows without the text; the demo replacement); e2e: an account with `loans` adds a date and a period, edits and deletes one; the dashboard lists the next dates; an account without `loans` reads the calendar and has no "Nuova data"; phone viewport; axe clean; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 11.15, 4.1

- [x] **9.26 Scan and photo: the two samples of the demo, rectangles turned by any angle** (owner request, Q126; required for 0.9.0, carried out before 9.3 and the manuals of 9.5)
  - Context: on a scan the photos lie flat, so their frames are rectangles that can be turned by any angle but never skewed; on a photo taken at an angle the frames are any quadrilaterals (perspective and rotation). The demo shows the difference with two samples.
  - Tasks:
    - **Scan detection** (`SCAN_DETECTION`, `#lib/vision/frames.ts`): rectangles turned by any angle instead of nearly upright ones only, with the same checks of contrast, fill and size; calibrated on about ten real scans, kept out of the repository (`local/`), with the numbers found recorded in `docs/STATUS.md`; the synthetic scans of the browser tests gain cards turned up to 45°.
    - **Two samples**: the current picture is the sample of a scan (`demo/census/class-cards.jpg`); the sample of a photo (`demo/census/class-cards-photo.jpg`) is made from it by a script (`scripts/make-class-photo.ts`, like `make-cover-fixture.ts`: the picture laid on a table in perspective, with light and shadow), which also writes its ground truth projected from `class-cards.frames.json`; the copies in `docs/reference/demo/` and `tests/fixtures/images/` with their `.frames.json`.
    - **Upload with a kind**: `uploadClassSheet` takes an optional `kind` (default `photo`), so the scan sample opens as "Scansione" and the photo sample as "Foto".
    - **Demo**: "Prova con l'immagine di esempio" becomes two buttons, "Esempio da scanner" and "Esempio da foto", with one line on the difference, on a class or list with a photo missing only; the media route serves either sample; the public demo accepts the bytes of either (two fingerprints).
    - Docs: steps 13.1 and 13.3, Q109, `docs/reference/demo/README.md`, `tests/fixtures/README.md`, `docs/demo.md`.
  - Acceptance: browser tests: the scan sample in scan mode gives 10 of 10 frames, rectangles, each within 2 % of the diagonal of the ground truth; the photo sample in photo mode 10 of 10; the synthetic scans with turned cards ≥ 95 % found and ≤ 2 % false; the real scans measured locally; unit tests: both fingerprints and nothing else accepted, the kind at upload; e2e (demo): each sample opens in its mode with "Foto trovate: 10"; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 13.3; real sample scans for the calibration

- [x] **9.27 Cards printed on both sides, with a calibration** (owner request, Q127; required for 0.9.0, carried out before 9.3 and the manuals of 9.5)
  - Context: a printer without two-sided printing needs the front and back side by side, folded (as today); a printer that prints both sides can put the back on the other side of the sheet, ten cards per A4 instead of four, provided the backs fall exactly behind the fronts. Printers shift the back by a millimetre or two, so the offset is measured once and stored. The template designer stays as it is: front and back are already separate faces.
  - Tasks:
    - Configuration: `cards.layout` (`side-by-side` default, `duplex`), replacing `cards.unit` (an installation that still has the key loads as before); `cards.duplex` with `flip` (`long-edge` default, `short-edge`) and `backOffsetMm` (`x`, `y`, from −10 to +10 mm, default 0); the duplex grid is as many cards as fit inside the margins with the gaps of `cards.sheet` (2 × 5 on A4 with the defaults), checked by the configuration validation; the cards editor (11.x), the coverage map and the configuration archive.
    - Rendering (`#lib/server/services/cards.ts`): every unit or card placed in mm from the corner of the paper (`@page { margin: 0 }`) in both modes, so the offset applies exactly; in duplex mode the fronts on the odd pages and the backs on the even pages at the mirrored places (long edge: x′ = paper width − x − card width; short edge: y′ = paper height − y − card height), plus `backOffsetMm`; the faces never mirrored or turned, so the photo and the images keep their place on each face; cut lines and crop marks on the fronts only; the last sheet always has its back page; `bleedMm` implemented: in duplex mode the face's background (its root element, copied empty behind it) runs `bleedMm` past the edge, and the designer shows the safe area 2 mm inside the edge as a guide.
    - **Print dialog for cards** (none today): side by side or both sides (default `cards.layout`), the template, and what to choose in the browser's print window ("Fronte-retro, capovolgi sul lato lungo", scale 100 % or "Dimensioni effettive", no margins); every entry point (patrons grid, patron page, print lists, import wizard, single card) opens it.
    - **Calibration** (`/admin/cards/calibration`, `admin`, linked from the cards settings and the dialog): a sample card of 150 × 100 mm centred on A4, printed on both sides with the configured flip: side 1 a large "1", the outline to cut along and "Ritaglia lungo la linea"; side 2 a large "2" and two rulers in mm (−10 … +10, a tick every millimetre, longer and numbered every 5, signs shown), a horizontal one across the place of the left edge and a vertical one across the place of the top edge. The page explains the steps (print both sides at 100 %, cut along the line on side 1, read on side 2 where the cut edge crosses each ruler), takes the two values with a drawing of their meaning, saves `backOffsetMm` (one configuration change, with history), and offers to print the sample again to check that the edges fall on 0.
    - Docs: §3.12, `docs/templates-authoring.md` (the two modes, bleed and safe area), `docs/config-reference.md`, `docs/navigation.md`; the admin guide (calibration steps, Italian and English, for non-technical readers) in the 9.5 sweep.
  - Acceptance: unit tests (geometry of both modes; the backs mirrored for both flips; the offset on the backs only; the bleed; the fit check; the calibration sheet: outline size, rulers at the mirrored places; the configuration with the old `cards.unit`); visual snapshots of a duplex front page and back page and of both calibration pages; the PDF alternates fronts and backs with an even page count; e2e: the dialog prints side by side and on both sides, the calibration saves the offset and the next print moves the backs; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 11.16

- [x] **9.28 Hardware profiles that work, and a `micro` one** (owner request; required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: the profile chose only the three SQLite settings; `imageConcurrency`, `yazConcurrency`, the profile's `argon2MemoryMb`, `backupCompress` and `smol` were declared and never read. The owner wants a fourth, smaller host: one slow core and 512 MB of RAM.
  - Tasks:
    - **`micro`** profile (one slow core, 512 MB, e.g. a Raspberry Pi Zero 2 W or the smallest VPS): `sqliteCacheMb` 8, `sqliteMmapMb` 0, `walAutocheckpointPages` 500, `imageConcurrency` 1, `yazConcurrency` 1, `argon2MemoryMb` 32, `smol` true. Values small | medium | large unchanged.
    - **Every value is read**: one resolver (`resolvePerformance`) gives the profile's values with the explicit keys over them, used by SQLite, the password hashing (accounts, wizard, demo), a limiter of the image work (patron photos, covers, class pictures, the logo: `Bun.Image` jobs beyond `imageConcurrency` wait their turn) and of the `yaz-client` processes (`yazConcurrency`); `smol` starts the server with `bun --smol` through a launcher (`cli/start.ts`, the container's entry point) that reads the profile from `CONFIG_DIR` before the server starts.
    - **`backupCompress` goes**: backups and archives are always compressed; an installation that still has the key loads as before (ignored).
    - **Container memory**: the `mem_limit` of each profile doubles — micro 512m, small 1g, medium 2g, large 4g (`docker/compose.yml`, `compose.lan.yml`, Appendix E, the setup guide); the public demo runs `small` with 1g.
    - **Verification** (resolves the `[VERIFY]` of §3.14): `bun --smol ./build` serves the app; with the demo data (5 000 copies) under `micro`, the server's peak memory measured through a session (login, catalogue search, checkout and return, a sheet of cards, a sheet of labels, a backup, a patron photo) stays under the micro container limit with room to spare; the measure recorded in `docs/STATUS.md`.
    - Configuration UI: the profile choice shows the four profiles with their hosts (both languages); `docs/config-reference.md` regenerated; §3.14, Appendix A, Q22.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the resolver (each profile, overrides), the limiter (never more than N at once, order kept, errors released), the launcher's choice of `--smol`, the password memory from the profile; the measurement above; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.27

- [x] **9.29 Memory for the smallest host** (owner request after the load test of 9.28; required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: under load, with the largest library the system is planned for (10 000 copies, 1 000 students, 100 teachers, 400 open loans), the server's memory grew with the host's RAM (JavaScriptCore sizes its heap from the RAM it sees, not from the container's limit) and peaked on the sheets of cards, which carried the back's picture and the logo once per card (6.4 MB for a class); `--smol` changed nothing measurable.
  - Tasks:
    - **The engine told the container's memory**: the launcher `scripts/start.ts` reads the container's limit (cgroup v2 `memory.max`, v1 `memory.limit_in_bytes`) and starts the server with `BUN_JSC_forceRAMSize` set to three quarters of it (unless already set); it now always replaces itself with the server (`execve`), so the variable is read when the engine starts.
    - **Each picture once per sheet**: a picture that the template's styles use as a background (`url('data:…')`) is written once per printed document, as a CSS custom property, and every face refers to it (`var(--bv-asset-N)`); cards, label sheets and the previews; what is printed does not change (the visual baselines stay).
    - **Password hashes of 16 MB** on every profile (`argon2MemoryMb` 16, still overridable): the system is never exposed (owner); an existing hash keeps its cost until its password changes.
    - **Container limits halved**: micro 256m, small 512m, medium 1g, large 2g; the public demo `small` with 512m.
    - **The load test again** (one core, the largest library, 8 reading users and 2 desk users for 60 s, micro and small), results in `docs/STATUS.md` and `docs/performance.md`.
    - **Exports downloaded at the same time** (found by the load test): a streamed export iterates a statement of its own (`prepare`), never the shared cached one, which a second download ran again and broke.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the cgroup limit read (v2, v1, unlimited), the launcher's environment, a sheet with one copy of each background; the print baselines unchanged; the load test within the halved limits; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.28

- [x] **9.30 Architecture review: defects, duplicated rules, dead code** (owner request; required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: a read-only review of the whole code base found the same rule or pipeline written three to five times with small differences, some of which have already become defects, and code that only the tests read. Nothing in the product changes on purpose: what a user sees stays the same except where it was wrong.
  - Tasks:
    - **Defects, each fixed test first**: the backup of chosen parts copies the faces of removed pupils (the painting over erased frames moves into the backup and export services, one `backupNow` shared by the scheduled and the manual backup, every removal of a patron covers the faces once its transaction has committed); a restore of patrons ignores the class tables (`class_group`, `class_group_level` travel with the patrons; a test checks that every table belongs to a part or to the list of tables that never travel); a print job of cards whose patron types use templates of different sizes takes the sheet of the last one (each template's cards on sheets of their own geometry, the audit names the templates used); `list=<id>` of the print routes accepts a list of the other kind; the full archive is unpacked without limits (one safe archive reader with the limits of the configuration archive, used by both archives and the language packs); the Z39.50 host is checked only by the form (the rule moves into the configuration schema); the language and colour set are written into `config.json` even when a `ui.json` fragment holds them; dates of "Da classificare" in the host's time zone; the loans search treats `%` and `_` as wildcards and drops the matches of the patron search; the card test sheet lost its fold and crop marks; the card print dialog still speaks of a red cut line; slip templates checked with a placeholder schema; camera pictures never released; a batch on copies and the clearing of its print list in two transactions.
    - **One implementation per rule**: the CSV imports (one pipeline for upload, mapping, validation and commit; one import wizard on the client); the configuration writes (one commit path with every guard, one check of a folder used by the import, `config:check` and the overview, one table of the configuration folder's files from which kinds, parts and defaults are derived); covers and patron photos (one stored-image module), one atomic file write, one file-name stamp; the print document and paper sizes, the label stock geometry in `#lib/shared/label-stock.ts`, one template loader, one sheet-fit check of a card size; service errors mapped to HTTP in one helper; patron names, dates, class order, ISBN recognition, overridable refusals, lendable statuses and label status from one shared helper each; repeated UI pieces (file upload through a remote form, camera capture, list filters, query states, confirmations that track their own promise, overlapping primitives) folded into one component or helper.
    - **Remote functions thin**: setup, classification, inventory, loans count, appearance and backups keep their logic and their audit in services.
    - **Large modules split at their seams** (catalogue search and copies, patron search and census, the erasure of class pictures, label geometry), pages split into components where a page holds several independent flows.
    - **Dead code**: exports that only the tests or nobody read are removed (or move to the test helpers), with the i18n keys that no screen uses.
  - Acceptance: a failing test first for each defect, then green; every moved rule keeps its unit tests (coverage gate unchanged); the print baselines unchanged; `docs/navigation.md` and `docs/database.md` unchanged unless a page or table changed; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.29

- [x] **9.31 One archive format; sheet marks from the configuration only** (owner decisions after the review of 9.30; required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: the configuration archive (11.3) and the full archive (export, backups, restore, 11.12) are two zip formats that do not read each other: a backup given to "Importa configurazione" is refused, a configuration archive given to "Ripristina" restores nothing. A card template could override the sheet's marks (`foldMark`, `cutMarks`, `cutLines` in `template.json`), a setting the designer does not show and the print dialog does not follow. Until 0.9.0 every archive and configuration is a trial: no old format is kept readable.
  - Tasks:
    - **One archive format**: every archive BiblioViva writes (Esporta configurazione, Esporta tutto, the archive of chosen parts, the backups) has the layout and manifest of the full archive (`config/`, `db/`, `uploads/`), and holds the parts it was asked for; "Esporta configurazione" is the archive of the configuration parts. "Importa configurazione" takes any archive and applies its configuration parts (an archive without them is refused with a readable message); "Ripristina" takes any archive and offers the parts it holds. Both check before writing with the same checks; the command-line tools (`config:export`, `config:import`, `backup:restore`) follow the same rules. The reader of the former configuration format goes.
    - **Sheet marks from the configuration only**: `foldMark`, `cutMarks` and `cutLines` leave `template.json` (like any key a template does not know, they are ignored); the marks and the outline come from `cards.sheet`, and the print dialog's cutting step follows them.
    - **The intermittent end-to-end failure of the record page** (`catalog.spec.ts`, the actions row on a desktop window resized several times): found and fixed so that it passes every run.
    - Docs: §3.6, 11.3 and 11.12 texts, the admin guides (it/en), `docs/deploy.md`, `docs/demo.md`, `docs/templates-authoring.md`, the cards reference.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for exporting the configuration parts in the archive format, importing the configuration from a full backup, restoring from a configuration-only archive, refusing an archive without configuration parts; e2e of the configuration archive round trip and of the restore updated; the record page spec green in repeated runs; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.30

- [x] **9.32 Heavy jobs one at a time on the smallest host** (owner request after a load test on a Raspberry Pi 4; required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: the load test of 9.29 again, on a Raspberry Pi 4 limited to half a core and 256 MB (`micro`), with twice the largest library planned (20 000 copies on 16 000 titles, 2 000 students, 200 teachers, 1 191 loans in progress, 1 050 photos): three users at once are served (searches under a second, memory stable around 110–210 MB), but a checkout waits up to 17 s while the cards of a class render, a sheet of cards or labels that takes more than 10 s before its first byte is cut by the server's idle timeout, and ten users at once for three minutes grow the memory until the container is stopped at 256 MB.
  - Tasks:
    - **Idle timeout of 120 s**: the server and the basic local-network front keep a connection that has sent nothing for 120 s (`CONNECTION_IDLE_TIMEOUT`, the image's default, overridable), so a slow print sheet or a long check is not cut.
    - **Heavy jobs wait their turn**: the print sheets (cards, labels, notices, receipts, the print view of a report) and the exports (CSV and ODS, the archives and backups) run through a limiter of their own, at most `performance.heavyConcurrency` at once (micro 1, small 1, medium 2, large 4; editable in Configurazione → Prestazioni with the other values of the profile), the others waiting in the order they came; never nested with the image limiter.
    - **The load test again** on the same Raspberry Pi with the same data and load (2 reading users and 1 desk user, 8 and 2, three minutes each): the desk's waits, the cut sheets and the memory compared with the first run; results in `docs/performance.md` and `docs/STATUS.md`.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the limiter of heavy jobs (one at a time on micro, order kept, errors released, no nesting with the image limiter) and for the idle timeout passed to both servers; the configuration reference and the coverage map updated; the load test recorded; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.31

- [x] **9.33 A backup at start when the scheduled one was missed** (owner request, Q129; required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: BiblioViva may run on the desk computer itself (one PC with Docker and the browser, Linux or Windows, 9.34), switched off at the end of the day. The scheduled backup of 1.9 (`Bun.cron(backup.cron)`, by default at night) then never runs, and nothing tells the operator.
  - Tasks:
    - **Catch-up at start**: when the server starts (`hooks.server.ts` `init`, beside the scheduler of 1.9), the backup service works out the last time `backup.cron` should have fired, in `library.timezone`, and compares it with the newest backup of the backups folder (`BACKUP_DIR`, 9.35); when that run is missing it runs **one** backup (never one per missed day), a minute after the start so the first pages are not slowed, through the same `backupNow` as the scheduled and the manual backup and through the limiter of heavy jobs of 9.32.
    - **Setting**: `backup.catchUp` (default on), editable in Configurazione → Backup beside the schedule, in the coverage map of 11.11 and in the configuration archive of 11.3.
    - **Shown to the operator**: the backups page marks a backup made at start ("recuperato all'avvio"); the first-run checklist of 8.7 counts it like any backup.
    - **Docs**: the admin guide and the installation guide say that a computer switched off at night makes its backup at the next start, and that the schedule can also be moved into the opening hours.
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the missed-run calculation (daily and weekly expressions, the time zone and a change of daylight saving time, a host off for several days makes one backup, no backup when the newest one is after the last expected run, the setting off), for the delay and for the shared `backupNow`; the configuration reference and the coverage map updated; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 1.9, 9.32

- [ ] **9.34 Windows: BiblioViva in Docker Desktop** (owner request, Q130, ADR-038; required for 0.9.0, carried out before the setup guide of 8.7 and the manuals of 9.5)
  - Context: in many schools the desk computer runs Windows, and BiblioViva must run there too, possibly on that same PC. Docker Desktop with the WSL 2 back end runs the same `linux/amd64` image as a Linux host, so the app does not change; the work is in the deployment files, the setup script and the guide.
  - Tasks:
    - **Compose files unchanged**: `docker/compose.yml` (Tailscale sidecar, userspace networking) and `docker/compose.lan.yml` run in Docker Desktop as they are, checked on a real host. `/config` and `/data` are **named volumes**, kept inside the WSL 2 virtual machine, never bind mounts of Windows folders: SQLite's locking is not reliable on the Windows file share and the owner of the files (uid 1000) is not kept there [VERIFY on the host]. The backups (`/backups`, 9.35), which hold no live database, are the folder `backups` beside the compose file, a folder of the PC [VERIFY: written by uid 1000 through Docker Desktop]; the other Windows folder involved is where the operator saves the archives downloaded from the app.
    - **`setup.ps1`** (PowerShell, beside the `setup.sh` of 8.7, idempotent like it): checks Windows 10 or 11 64-bit, virtualization and WSL 2; checks that Docker Desktop is installed and running, and otherwise links to Docker's official download and installation page (no silent installation); creates `%USERPROFILE%\biblioviva\` with the compose file, `.env` and `serve.json`; asks the same questions as `setup.sh` (network mode, hardware profile, Tailscale auth key); starts the stack and prints the address.
    - **Running on a desk PC**: Docker Desktop set to start at sign-in, the services with `restart: unless-stopped`, so BiblioViva comes back after a restart of Windows; BiblioViva runs only while a user is signed in (Docker Desktop is not a Windows service), which the guide states; the backup at start of 9.33 covers the evenings the PC is off.
    - **Hardware profile**: chosen from the PC as on Linux (`small` or `medium`); the memory given to the WSL 2 virtual machine (`.wslconfig`) is mentioned only if the profile needs more than the default [VERIFY].
    - **Licence**: Docker Desktop's terms for a school [VERIFY]; the alternatives for a school that cannot use it (the Linux path of 8.7, or Podman Desktop [VERIFY]) are named in one line.
    - **Docs**: §3.21 lists Windows; `docs/deploy.md` gains a Windows section (named volumes, sign-in, restore with the archive); the setup guide of 8.7 and the installation section of the site (9.7) gain the Windows path, with the hand-taken screenshots of Docker Desktop kept to a minimum.
  - Acceptance: on a Windows 11 PC (an owner's check, recorded in `docs/STATUS.md` like 8.5): `setup.ps1` on a fresh Docker Desktop brings the stack up in both network modes; the login page opens in the browser of the same PC and, with Tailscale, from another device; after a restart of Windows and a sign-in BiblioViva is back with its data; a backup, a download of the full archive and a restore work; a second run of `setup.ps1` is harmless; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 8.3, 8.8, 9.33

- [x] **9.35 Backups in a volume of their own** (owner request; required for 0.9.0, carried out after 9.33 and before 9.34)
  - Context: the backups live in `DATA_DIR/backups`, inside the same volume as the database and the pictures they protect: a volume removed or damaged takes its backups with it, and an operator cannot copy them, or keep them on another disk, without knowing Docker. Until 0.9.0 every installation is a trial: nothing is moved for an existing one.
  - Tasks:
    - **Three volumes**: `/config` (`CONFIG_DIR`), `/data` (`DATA_DIR`: database, pictures, imports, temporary files) and `/backups` (`BACKUP_DIR`, new, declared in `src/env.ts`): the scheduled and manual backups, their copy of the pictures, the archives the helpers write, the state of the off-site copy (`offsite.json`), and what the backups page lists, downloads and restores. In development `./local/backups`.
    - **Compose files**: `/backups` is the folder `./backups` beside the compose file (a plain folder the operator can see, copy, or mount from a second disk or a USB drive), `/data` stays a named volume (the database's locking, 9.34); the `offsite` service syncs `/backups`. On Windows (9.34) the same folder is a folder of the PC [VERIFY: written by uid 1000 through Docker Desktop], the database staying in its named volume.
    - **Docs**: §1, §3.20, 8.1, 8.2, Appendix E, CLAUDE.md ("three volumes"), `docs/deploy.md` (volumes, backups on a second disk, restore from `/backups`), the admin guides (it/en: where the backups are and how to keep a copy elsewhere), the demo (`docs/demo.md`) and the helpers' examples (`/backups/<file>`).
  - Acceptance: unit tests for the folder of the backups (scheduled, manual, archive, pictures, off-site state, list and download, restore of a stored backup, the catch-up of 9.33) taken from `BACKUP_DIR`; the e2e of the backups page green; the container started with the three volumes on Linux makes, lists and restores a backup in `./backups`; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.33

- [ ] **9.36 The public repository, from a snapshot** (owner decision; Q133, ADR-048; required for 0.9.0, carried out once the first sections of the documentation are written, the installation section first (8.7, 9.5), and before the documentation site of 9.7 goes online, since GitHub Pages publishes only from a public repository; the publication **starts the public beta**: the remaining steps of 0.9.0 are developed in public, the READMEs carry the status "public beta" with what BiblioViva does and does not do, and the home page ends with a short public-beta notice)
  - Context: the history of the private repository is not published (commit messages, working hours, old pictures); rewriting it was judged too much work. A fresh public repository starts from one commit of the current tree, and development goes on there only. A force-push of a snapshot onto the existing repository is not enough: its Dependabot pull requests, Activity log, Actions runs and commits reachable by their hash would stay visible.
  - Tasks:
    - **Audit**: before the snapshot, the tracked files that will be public are checked for times of day that reveal working hours (in the text, in file and folder names such as the migration folder's timestamp, in the metadata of pictures and archives) and for personal data (names, addresses, local paths, device names); dates by day in `docs/STATUS.md` and in the titles of the ADRs stay, times that are data of the product (the demo's reset hour, sample calendars, the clocks of the tests) stay. The findings are fixed by commits on the archive.
    - **Files left out**: what is not published (`docs/reviews/navigation.md`, a review already applied, and the pictures nothing uses) is removed by a commit on the archive, with the references to it, so the snapshot is the archive's `main` as it is.
    - **Archive**: the existing repository is renamed `itotm/biblioviva-archivio` and stays private with the whole history; it is never pushed to the public repository.
    - **Snapshot**: a new `itotm/biblioviva`, created empty, receives one commit of the tree, made by the rehearsed script (outside the repository): it checks that `main` is clean and pushed to the archive, keeps the archive as a second remote, makes one orphan commit of the tracked tree (author the GitHub noreply address, the date optionally fixed) and pushes it; the repository is then made public. No Dependabot pull requests, Actions runs or old commits come along.
    - **Settings**: the GHCR package `ghcr.io/itotm/biblioviva` given to the new repository (Actions access with role Write before the first push, so `publish.yml` can publish; the package's source link follows the image's label); GitHub Pages from Actions for `site/` with the custom domain `docs.biblioviva.org` (the domain verified in the account's Pages settings, the `docs` CNAME in Cloudflare "DNS only", HTTPS enforced, [deploy.md](docs/deploy.md)); a ruleset on `main` that refuses force-pushes and deletion (no required pull requests: one maintainer); Dependabot security alerts on and no version updates (exact pins, upgrades are deliberate commits); the description, the homepage `biblioviva.org` and the topics; `ONLINE.repo` on in `scripts/home-build.ts` for the home page's GitHub links (the home page built and uploaded again; its Cloudflare Pages project is not tied to the repository). The archive has no secrets, variables, webhooks or deploy keys to move.
    - **Docs**: the READMEs' links, the demo's build (it pulls the public image) and `docs/demo.md` checked against the new repository.
  - Acceptance: the public repository has one commit and the same tree as the archive's `main`; CI green on it; the image published from it; the home page built from it and the documentation site published from it.
  - Depends on: the first sections of the documentation, the installation section first (8.7, 9.5)

- [ ] **9.37 Updates with database migrations from 1.0.0** (owner decision; Q82, Q133; required for 1.0.0, carried out during 9.10)
  - Context: until 1.0.0 the database has one regenerated migration and a schema change is carried by export and restore (Q82, ADR-011). From 1.0.0 the school's data are real and every update must carry them forward by itself.
  - Tasks:
    - **Baseline**: when 1.0.0 is tagged, the single migration becomes the frozen baseline; from then on every schema change is a new migration generated by `bun run db:generate` and committed with the change; a released migration is never edited or deleted (CLAUDE.md, the migrations rule).
    - **At start**: pending migrations are applied when the server starts (as today), after a backup taken just before them (`pre-migration`, kept like a manual one), in one transaction; a failed migration leaves the database as it was and the server refuses to start with a readable message; the backups page shows the backup taken before an update.
    - **Restore across versions**: a backup of an older version is restored by migrating it after the restore; a backup of a newer version than the running one is refused with a readable message.
    - **Tests**: an upgrade test from the database of every released version (a small fixture database per release, kept in `tests/fixtures/releases/`) migrated to the current schema, the data checked; the migrations run on a copy of the demo data in CI.
    - **Docs**: `docs/deploy.md` and the admin guide (it/en): how an update works, the backup before it, what to do when it fails.
  - Acceptance: the upgrade test green from 1.0.0 onwards; a failed migration rolls back and the server says so; a restore of an older backup migrates; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 9.6

- [x] **9.38 Patron photos and book covers can be switched off** (owner request, Q135; required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: some schools do not want photos of their pupils, others do not care about covers; each must be able to leave the whole feature out, not only hide a reminder (`photos.required` keeps its meaning while photos are on).
  - Tasks:
    - **Configuration**: `photos.enabled` and `covers.enabled`, both true by default, edited in the generated settings pages of Foto degli utenti and Copertine (the first key of each) and asked in the wizard's Biblioteca step (two switches, on); in the configuration archive like every key. The root layout hands both flags to the pages (`#lib/ui/features.ts`); `requireFeature()` (`#lib/server/features.ts`) answers 404 in every page and remote function of a feature that is off.
    - **Photos off**: no photo taken, uploaded, imported (the Koha import of 3.9b), shown or asked for — the census and the class pictures of Phase 13 leave the patrons' tabs and answer 404, the patron page and the new-patron form have no photo, the lists, the desk and the pickers show no thumbnail, `photos.required` reminders are off whatever its value; the cards print without the photo: the shipped card templates come in a version without it (`config/cards/templates-no-photo/`, the same names, recorded as `cards-no-photo/<name>` by `templates:shipped`), and a copy in CONFIG_DIR never edited follows the switch at every reload; a template with a photo box keeps its placeholder; the demo data have no photos.
    - **Covers off**: no lookup at the providers (automatic or asked), no cover taken or uploaded, none shown (record page, catalogue, search, desk, classification flow, fast add); the providers block of the integrations says covers are off.
    - **Data kept**: switching off deletes nothing; the stored photos and covers stay in the data and in the backups, and come back when the feature is switched on again.
    - **Docs**: `docs/navigation.md` (the pages that disappear), the admin guide (it/en) when 9.5 writes it.
  - Acceptance: unit tests of the guard, of the template variant and of the reload that swaps the untouched copies; e2e with each feature switched off and on again: the pages and controls gone and back, the remote functions refused, a card printed without the photo; the wizard's two switches; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 11.2, 12.8

- [ ] **9.39 Language packs to download with each release** (owner idea, Q136; not required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: the image ships Italian and English only; more languages come as the packs of 1.14, JSON files that would weigh on every installation for nothing. Each release publishes them apart, and an administrator installs the one wanted from the configuration.
  - Tasks:
    - **Packs**: one folder per language in the repository (`i18n-packs/<code>/`, the same files as `src/lib/i18n/en/` plus `pack.json` with `appVersion`), checked by `bun run i18n:check` in CI; the release workflow (9.6) compresses each into `biblioviva-i18n-<code>-<version>.zip` and attaches them to the GitHub release, with an index `biblioviva-i18n-<version>.json` (code, native name, completeness, size). Languages (Q136): French, Spanish, Portuguese of Portugal and of Brazil, German, Dutch, Catalan, Romanian, Hungarian, Czech, Slovak, Polish, Slovenian, Croatian, Greek, Swedish, Norwegian, Danish, Finnish, Turkish, Russian, Ukrainian, Simplified Chinese, Japanese, Korean; Arabic and Hebrew wait for a right-to-left layout.
    - **Install from the app**: Amministrazione → Aspetto e lingua lists the packs of the release that matches the installed version (the index read from the release; a server-side download, refused in the public demo), installs the chosen one into `CONFIG_DIR/i18n/<code>/` after the validation of 1.14, and offers to switch to it; a zip downloaded by hand can be uploaded instead, for an installation without internet. A pack of another version is accepted when it validates, missing keys in English, with a warning; installed packs travel in the configuration archive (the `languages` part) and can be removed.
    - **What a language needs besides the words**: **plural forms** are done (ADR-051): every message that counts things writes `{{count}} {{count:plural; one:…; other:…;}}` (the parser picks by the locale's rules; the old `1:`/`default:` selectors were converted), a pack gives every form its language uses and `i18n:status` refuses one that lacks a form; dates and numbers come from `Intl` for any code (nothing to add); the scanner keyboard layouts stay the six there are (the wizard proposes the closest one and its hint says to choose the nearest, changeable later; more layouts for Czech, Slovak, Hungarian, Turkish and the Nordic keyboards wait for a school that needs one); fonts stay as they are (Inter and JetBrains Mono cover Latin, Cyrillic and Greek, the system's fonts do the rest, Chinese, Japanese and Korean included); school presets for the countries that lack one (9.41).
    - **Translations**: complete, kept in step with every change of the English dictionaries (a pack lagging behind a release is published with its completeness), reviewed by a native speaker where possible.
  - Acceptance: CI validates every pack against English; the release has a zip per language and the index; installing from the app (mocked release in the tests) and by upload works, the language appears in the list and switching to it shows its texts, dates and plurals; a pack of another version is accepted with its warning; e2e on Aspetto e lingua.
  - Depends on: 1.14, 9.6

- [x] **9.40 A beep when the phone's camera reads a code** (owner request, Q137; required for 0.9.0)
  - Context: with the phone the operator looks at the book, not at the screen; a short sound says the code was taken (a card, a copy's barcode or an ISBN). The USB scanner beeps by itself, so the sound belongs to the camera only.
  - Tasks:
    - **Sounds**: generated in the browser with Web Audio (`#lib/scanner/sound.ts`, no audio files): four sounds from the owner's choice on the mock — `till` (a sine at 2400 Hz, 90 ms), `scanner` (a square wave at 1800 Hz through a 3.5 kHz low-pass, 70 ms), `confirm` (two triangle notes rising a fifth, 660 → 990 Hz, 2 × 70 ms), `arpeggio` (three triangle notes, C6 E6 G6, 230 ms) — each drawn once into a buffer at the same peak level. The audio context is opened by the tap that opens the camera (browsers play sound only after a gesture); the sound follows the phone's media volume (an iPhone on silent stays silent).
    - **Where**: `ScannerOverlay` plays it when a read is taken (with the green flash and the vibration), in every camera flow: the desk, the check-in, fast add, the patron and copy fields, the inventory; never for a bad or refused read, never for the USB scanner.
    - **Configuration**: `barcodes.cameraSound` (`off`, `till`, `scanner`, `confirm`, `arpeggio`; default `confirm`), edited in Amministrazione → Codici a barre with a button that plays the chosen sound; in the configuration archive like every key.
    - **The rule of Q40**: the test that kept the application silent allows Web Audio in `#lib/scanner/sound.ts` only.
  - Acceptance: browser unit tests of the sound definitions (each renders to a buffer of its length at the common peak, sounding from the first millisecond) and of `off` opening no audio; the guard of Q40 with its one exception; e2e: a camera read of the fake video beeps once, and on Codici a barre a sound is chosen, played, saved and put back; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 1.6, 11.2

- [x] **9.41 Class names and school years for the countries of the language packs** (owner request, Q138; not required for 0.9.0; carried out before 9.39 publishes the packs and before 1.0.0, so its schema change still regenerates the single migration)
  - Context: the school structure of Phase 12 (stages, levels with a `next`, permanent groups, one label template with literal text) already writes the classes of almost every country of the packs (`6e2`, `2º ESO B`, `7.º B`, turma `601`, `a V-a B`, `5.a`, `IIIa`, `Β2`, `7A`, `0.b`, `5-A`, `5А`, `初一(3)班`, `1年2組`, `1학년 2반`); seven gaps remain, three of which show Italian or a wrong order to a school of another language.
  - Tasks:
    - **A label template per stage**: `school.stages[].classLabel`, optional, overrides `school.classLabel` for the levels of its stage and may leave out `{group}` (the label is then the level alone: the German Oberstufe `Q1`, with no classes, beside `7b` in the same Gymnasium; one class per year is enough for the upgrade). The mirror gives each level its stage's template (`school_level.class_label`) and the view `patron_class` reads it; `classLabel()`, `groupLabel()` and `findClass()` take the stage's template. The uniqueness rule of `structureProblems` becomes "two levels of the same template may not print alike" (labels may repeat across stages with different templates: `中{level}年` and `高{level}年` around `1`). Edited on the Scuola settings page; `docs/database.md`.
    - **Paths that split** (the Netherlands after the brugklas into vmbo, havo and vwo, which end at different years; Spain after 4º ESO; Portugal after 9.º): `next` of a level may be a **list of choices** (level codes, `null` for "leaves the school"), besides one code, `null` or absent. At the class upgrade every pupil of such a level is asked which (the first choice preselected); the class advances to the first choice, and a pupil who chooses another level is placed in a class hosting it, or left without one by the promotion rules; `null` makes a leaver. `structureProblems` checks every choice and the circle test follows every branch; the mirror's `next_code` holds the first choice [VERIFY that no report reads it otherwise]; the editor of the levels edits the list.
    - **School year**: the presets propose `library.schoolYearStart` (Brazil in February, Korea in March, Japan in April [VERIFY each]) and a new `library.schoolYearLabel` (`span` → `2026/2027`, `start` → `2027`, the name by the starting year of Brazil, Japan's 年度 and Korea's 학년도); the label is what the cards, the reports and the screens show, while the loans keep storing the school year as now. Edited with the other library settings, asked in the wizard with the school preset.
    - **The card's class line and the fixed Italian words of the shipped templates**: the line printed on the card becomes the template `school.cardClass` with `{word}` (`school.groupWord`, which stays the name of the group field in the forms and filters), `{group}`, `{level}` and `{class}` (the class label); default `{word} {group}` → `Sezione B`, `{group}組`, `{class}` → `Klasse 7b`. The shipped card templates (both versions, `config/cards/templates/` and `templates-no-photo/`) print it and take the teacher's word (`Docente`) from the installation's dictionary; the shipped slips (`checkout-receipt`, `overdue-patron`, `overdue-by-class`) take their fixed words from the dictionary too (a print namespace handed to the context, so a school that edits a copy can still write its own words); `bun run templates:shipped`.
    - **Order in the installation's language**: the collators of `#lib/shared/school.ts` and `#lib/server/reports/execute.ts` follow `ui.locale` instead of Italian (Å Ä Ö after Z in Swedish, Æ Ø Å in Danish and Norwegian, `ch` after `h` in Czech, Turkish `ç`, `ı`), the locale handed in by the callers.
    - **Classes recognised in the register files**: the matching of `findLevel()` and `findClass()` normalises with NFKC (the full-width digits of Japanese, Chinese and Korean registers, `１年２組`) and ignores more marks (`«»`, quotes, the Greek keraia `΄ʹ`, `-` and `/` between year and group: `5-A`, `9/B`), still refusing a text that two classes would match; a level gains optional `aliases` (`V` for `a V-a`, `三` for `3`, `一年级`), matched like its code, label and name, edited with the level.
    - **Presets**: `name` becomes a record by language code with English as the fallback (`setup.ts` and `school.remote.ts` pick the installation's language); the list puts first the presets of the language's countries (`pt-BR` → Brazil, `de` → Germany, Austria and Switzerland), then the others; the generic preset's stage and level names come from the dictionary. New presets for the countries of the packs that lack one: Portugal, Brazil, the Netherlands, Belgium, Catalonia, Austria, Switzerland, Romania, Hungary, Czechia, Slovakia, Slovenia, Croatia, Greece, Sweden, Norway, Denmark, Finland, Turkey, Russia, Ukraine, China, Japan, Korea; each one [VERIFY] with a teacher of the country before it is advertised, as the existing foreign presets.
  - Acceptance: unit tests of the per-stage template (Oberstufe without a group, labels repeated across stages, the uniqueness rule), of the branching `next` (structure problems, circles across branches, the upgrade plan with a pupil taking each choice and one leaving), of the school year label, of the card line's tokens, of the collation per locale, of the register matching (full-width digits, marks, aliases, ambiguity still refused) and of every preset loading valid; e2e: the class upgrade of a school with a branch (each pupil asked, placed in the chosen level's class), a card and a slip printed in English with no Italian word, the Scuola page editing a stage template and a level's choices and aliases; `bun run ci` green.
  - Depends on: 7.1, 12.3, 12.5, 12.7, 12.8, 12.9

### Phase 11 — Configuration from the admin UI (required for 0.9.0)

**Required for the 0.9.0 release** (owner decision): carried out after Phase 8 and before Phase 9 (§0 rule 2), and the classification taxonomy editor is the **first** step. The configuration folder (§3.6) stays the single source of truth; the UI reads it, validates edits with the same Valibot schema, writes each section back to the file it comes from atomically (temp file + rename, previous version kept in `CONFIG_DIR/history/`), reloads the configuration and records the change in the audit log. Editing the files by hand keeps working.

**Completeness rule (owner):** at the end of the phase **every setting is editable from the admin UI**; no key of the configuration schema, no template, stock, image, report definition or language pack may remain reachable only by editing files. Secrets are not configuration: they stay in environment variables (`src/env.ts`) and are only shown as present/missing. The coverage map and its test (11.2, closed in 11.11) enforce the rule. The whole configuration also travels as **one archive** (export and import, 11.3), because it is many JSON files in a folder tree plus images (card backgrounds, pictograms for the spine bands, the library logo, template assets).

- [x] **11.1 Classification taxonomy** (first step, owner decision) — genres and the two-level non-fiction tree with drag-and-drop ordering, colour picker, pictogram upload (stored in `CONFIG_DIR/classification/images/`), Dewey map editor, preview of the spine band; reclassification impact shown before a code is removed. Being first, it builds the write-back plumbing every later editor reuses: `services/config-edit.ts` learns to write a section to the file it was read from (`classification.json` here), keeps the previous versions in `CONFIG_DIR/history/`, reloads and audits `config.<section>`; Administration gains the "Configurazione" entry with the taxonomy as its first page.
- [x] **11.2 Settings framework** — "Configurazione" area for `admin` showing every section with its current values, validation status and **the file it comes from** (§3.6); JSON view with schema-based errors; "Ricarica" and "Ripristina versione precedente" (the history of 11.1); per-section edit forms generated from the schema where possible; edits are written back to the fragment or folder that defines the section, never merged into `config.json`. Introduces the **coverage map** `#lib/admin/config-coverage.ts` (every leaf key of the schema and every kind of configuration file → the editor that handles it) and its unit test, with a list of what is still pending that the following steps empty.
- [x] **11.3 Configuration archive: export and import** (owner request) — "Esporta configurazione" downloads the archive of the configuration parts in the one archive format (§3.6: the files under `config/`, with the manifest): `config.json`, the section fragments, `calendar.*.json`, `labels/stocks/`, every template folder with its assets (cards, labels, slips), `classification/images/`, `branding/` (the library logo, 4.10), `reports/`, `i18n/` language packs; never `history/` and never secrets. "Importa configurazione" takes any archive of that format (a backup or "Esporta tutto" too: only its configuration parts are taken, an archive without them is refused) and **validates first** (§3.22): unpacked into a temporary folder with safe paths only (no absolute paths, `..`, links; size and file-count limits), loaded and checked exactly like the live folder (schema, fragments, stocks, templates, pictograms, reports, language packs), then a summary of the differences (files added, changed, removed; sections changed) and of the problems; "Importa" is enabled only with zero errors; the current folder is saved as a history snapshot, the new one swapped in atomically, the configuration reloaded, the audit written; the database is untouched. `fflate` (already pinned) builds and reads the zip; CLI twins `bun run config:export [file]` and `bun run config:import <zip> [--yes]`. Admin guide section in both languages.
  - Acceptance: unit tests (round trip of a full folder byte for byte, zip slip and link refused, oversize refused, invalid config or template reported with the file, history snapshot written, nothing changed when validation fails); e2e: export, change a value, import the exported archive, the value is back.
- [x] **11.4 Library, item types, sections, school year, special values, barcodes** — forms with inline validation; renaming a code shows how many items/patrons use it; the barcode settings (formats, counters, Code 39 option) with a preview.
- [x] **11.5 Circulation rules and calendar** — rule matrix editor (patron type × item type overrides), due-date mode, hard due date, overdue notices (`circulation.overdueNotice`), receipts; calendar editor with a month grid: click to toggle closed days, ranges with reason, holiday generator per year.
- [x] **11.6 Cards, labels, slips and stocks** — template selection per patron type, sheet geometry for cards, label stock editor with a live drawing of the sheet and the calibration print, default stock per label kind, slip template choice; template folders can be duplicated, renamed, deleted and their assets uploaded from the UI.
- [x] **11.7 Roles, permissions and staff accounts** — role editor over the fixed permission list; the shared staff accounts of `security.users` (create, rename, deactivate, **set a new password**, written as a hash); guard against removing `admin` from the last admin.
- [x] **11.8 Integrations, privacy, backup, security, appearance, performance, languages** — Z39.50 targets (with a "Prova connessione" button), metadata and cover providers with their flags, photo and cover formats and sizes, privacy, backup schedule and retention (with "next run" preview), session and login limits, theme, language and checkout idle time, the hardware profile with its overridable keys, language packs (upload a pack folder, validation report, remove).
- [x] **11.9 Reports library** — list of report definitions with validation, upload of new JSON definitions, "Prova" run with parameters.
- [x] **11.10 Visual designer for labels, spine bands and cards** (Q56, owner decision): drag-and-drop placement of fields, pictogram, barcode and images on the real stock or card geometry with live preview, plus direct HTML/CSS editing for advanced users (slips included); saves ordinary template folders in `CONFIG_DIR/{cards,labels,slips}/templates/`, so hand-written templates and designed ones are interchangeable; builds on the stock editor of 11.6.
- [x] **11.12 Granular backup, restore, export and import** (owner request) — every backup, restore, export and import offers **checkboxes to choose what it covers**, all granular: the library's data (patrons, catalogue with its copies, loans and their history, print lists, patron photos, covers, audit log) and every part of the configuration (the settings of each section, the classification with its pictograms, reports, card templates, label templates by kind — pair, barcode label, spine band —, slip templates, adhesive stocks, calendars, language packs, the logo). Applies to the configuration archive (11.3: export a selection; import only the selected parts of an archive, the others left as they are), the full export "Esporta tutto" and its restore (1.9b: a selection of data and configuration; the restore validates first, §3.22, and refuses a selection that would break references — e.g. loans without their patrons or copies — explaining what to add), and the backups page (a manual backup of a selection; restoring a backup chooses what to take from it). The selection is recorded in the archive manifest; every archive has the one format of §3.6, so the restore and the configuration import read any of them; the CLI helpers take the same choices (`--only`/`--except`). Acceptance: unit tests per part (round trip of each part alone, refusal of an inconsistent data selection, the untouched parts byte for byte unchanged); e2e: export only the templates, change a template and a setting, import the archive with only templates selected, the template is back and the setting is not.
- [x] **11.13 Appearance: library name and more colour sets** (owner request; one style, colour sets only: no separate accent colour, owner decision) — the library's name, set in the first-run wizard, is edited on the Appearance page next to the logo (it stays in the `library` section form too); six more colour sets beside the nine of 2.3a (fifteen), generated the same way (colours only, contrast-checked). Every setting is in the coverage map, editable from the UI and part of the configuration archive. Acceptance: unit tests (palette, contrast of every set, manifest), e2e (rename the library and see it in the shell, on the dashboard and in the manifest; the archive round trip changes it too).
  - Depends on: 11.2, 2.3a

- [x] **11.14 Generated pictograms for the spine bands** (owner request) — the pictograms the library uses today were given as an example only and must not be in the repository: `docs/reference/classification/koha-pictograms.png` is removed and the reference printout `docs/reference/labels/koha-etichette.png` keeps its layout with the symbols masked. New pictograms are generated for every genre and every non-fiction hundred by `bun run pictograms:generate` from Lucide icons (ISC, already a dependency): a disc in the node's colour, a dark outline and a white or dark symbol chosen by contrast, legible in black and white; written as SVG into the shipped `config/classification/images/` and referenced by `classification.json`. They are ordinary files of the configuration: replaced from the classification editor, exported and imported with the classification part. Acceptance: generator test (one file per genre and hundred, valid SVG, no script), the label and spine band previews show them, visual snapshot of the pair sheet updated.

A section switched off is not computed on the server either. Permissions still decide what each account sees inside a section. The setting is in the coverage map, editable from the UI and part of the configuration archive (settings of the `ui` section). Acceptance: unit tests (default, sections not computed when off), e2e (switch sections off and on, quick actions first, round trip through the archive).

- [x] **11.16 Drawing and code always in step** (owner request) — every card and label template, the built-in ones included, opens both in "Disegno" and in "Codice", and a change made in one is what the other shows: nothing is ever lost or dropped by switching. **The HTML files of the faces are the only source**; there is no separate layout file. An *element* is a direct child of the face's root element (or of the file, when the file has no single root); it carries its place and size in millimetres and its look in its own `style` attribute (`position: absolute; left; top; width; height` and font, size, bold, italic, capitals, alignment, colour, border, background), so the file reads the same way the panel does. The designer reads the files into elements — field, fixed text, barcode (`class="bv-barcode"`, sized by a base rule the template engine adds to every template), photo, pictogram, image of `assets/`, box, and **free HTML** for anything else (its content edited as code from the panel, the box still moved, resized and styled on the canvas) — and writes back only the elements that changed, keeping every other byte of the files: attributes, comments, Mustache sections, style declarations it does not know, `style.css`, `template.json`. Elements without a place in their `style` (placed by `style.css` or by the normal flow) are drawn where the preview shows them, measured in the preview frame; the first move on such a face fixes all of them where they are, writing the measured places into the HTML, so nothing jumps. Moves and sizes snap to a grid chosen from a drop-down above the canvas (none, 0.5, 1, 2, 2.5, 5, 10 mm, 2 mm by default; drawn over the face line by line, where the moves land, the arrow keys move by one step). Label **variants** by cell height are faces of their own (the full and the compact layout, each previewed on a stock of the template that selects it), so a save never drops `variants`. The built-in card `default` places each element in its own `style` and prints as before (visual baselines unchanged); the built-in labels `pair-default`, `spine-default` and `barcode-default` keep their flow layout, which adapts to the width of every stock, and are drawn from the preview's measures until their first move (the compact spine sticker keeps its Mustache sections inside its elements). A `design.json` in a template folder is not read, and the next save removes it. `docs/templates-authoring.md` describes the form; CLAUDE.md gains the rule.
  - Tests: reading and writing back every built-in template unchanged byte for byte, and hand-written samples with sections, comments and unknown declarations; the kind of each element; an edit touching only its element; fixing the measured places (designer state in the browser project); e2e: a copy of the built-in card moved and restyled in the drawing, changed in the code, the change seen in the drawing, saved and reopened; a compact label face fixed by a move without losing the variants.
  - Depends on: 11.10

The card designer offers them for a face's background ("Scegli uno sfondo": a dialog with those of the face's orientation, shown cut as on the card) and copies the chosen one into the template's `assets/` as `background-<name>.webp`, written with `background-size: calc(100% + 6mm) calc(100% + 6mm)`; "Carica un'immagine" uploads a picture of one's own into `assets/` (written with `cover`). Three card templates are always shipped: `default` (the library's card as adjusted by the owner, background D4 on the back), `fronte-retro` (horizontal, text on both sides, background C8) and `verticale` (a portrait card, `cardMm` 53.98 × 85.6, background H5); the previous back picture goes. A sheet of portrait cards takes the rows that fit the page. **The size of each card is its own**: the designer's "Formato della tessera" (width, height, rotate, back to the default) writes `cardMm` in the template's `template.json` (the rest of the file kept), and the faces are drawn at that size; without it a card takes `cards.cardMm` of the configuration. **Crop marks** in place of the red outline: every cut line of the cards is extended into the paper's margins and the gaps between the cards by a short black line 1 mm away from the card (on the page of the fronts when printed on both sides), the fold of a side-by-side unit the same way, dashed; `cards.sheet.cutMarks` and `foldMarks` on by default, `cutLines` null by default; every template follows the configuration. D4 is made 8 % smaller, a little low, with a frame of its edges' colours, so the feather no longer touches the top of the card.

### Phase 12 — Configurable barcodes and school structure (required for 0.9.0)

**New part of the project** (owner decisions, Q90–Q102), designed in `docs/studies/barcodes-and-school-classes.md` (the study keeps the survey of practices around the world, the alternatives and the estimates; this phase is what was chosen). It replaces the fixed formats of §3.7 and the fixed school structure of Q12 (`library.schoolType`, `library.maxClassYear`, `library.sections`, `patron.class_year`/`section`, the reading class `1…maxClassYear`) with configuration chosen in the first-run wizard and editable later, following the practice of Italian and foreign schools and libraries. Required for 0.9.0 and carried out **after Phase 11 and before the remaining steps of Phase 9** (Q98), so the hardening, documentation and release cover it. Before 1.0 there is no compatibility layer (Q95): the single migration is regenerated (Q82), the built-in reports, seed and tests are rewritten, nothing keeps the old column names alive. The Phase 11 completeness rule applies to every new key (editor, coverage map, configuration archive).

**Barcode rules (Q90–Q93):**

- A format is a **pattern of fixed-width tokens**, never a free regex: `YYYY`/`YY` (calendar year of creation in `library.timezone`), `N…N` (zero-padded counter, restarting every year when the pattern has a year), `C` (the check character, anywhere in the pattern, computed over the digits of the payload in order), `{lib}` (the optional library code `barcodes.libraryCode`: supported, not used by this library), digits and `-` as literals, upper-case letters as quoted literals (`'T'`), never `I`, `O`, `Q`.
- Check characters: **`luhn`** (digit), **`damm`** (digit; catches every single error and every adjacent transposition), **`mod23`** (a **letter**: the payload's value mod 23 mapped onto the 23 letters without `I O Q`, the scheme of the Spanish DNI letter on a safer alphabet; catches every single-digit error, every adjacent transposition and every jump transposition). Others considered in the study (Verhoeff, EAN, ISO 7064 variants, the codice fiscale letter) are not shipped.
- **Never confused (owner rule, Q90):** a pair of formats is accepted only when (1) no string is valid for both kinds, also after the scanner repairs (dash lost or replaced, layout table) and also against the other kind's legacy and retired formats and ISBN/ISSN/EAN; the decision never relies on the check character's value; and (2) a person can tell them apart by at least one visible sign: a **length difference of at least 2 characters**, a **letter (check or literal) in a different position**, or a **different separator pattern**. The editor, `config:check` and the startup validation refuse a pair that breaks the rule and show two example codes that would be confused.
- New default formats (Q91): copies **`YYYY-CNNNN`** (year, dash, check character, 4-digit counter: `2026-E0267` with the letter, `2026-70267` with a digit), cards **`CNNNNNN`** (check character and 6-digit counter: `U012345`); the check algorithm is a choice for both, the **mod-23 letter** by default (Q92), so the defaults read `2026-E0267` and `U012345`. Legacy codes (plain counters, `YYYY-NNNN`, 10-digit zero-padded cards) and any format generated earlier keep working everywhere (recognised, looked up, validated); only new copies and cards get the new format; numbers are never reused.
- Scanner repair tables per keyboard layout (`barcodes.keyboardLayout`: `it`, `es`, `de`, `fr`, `uk`, `us`), Q93.

**School structure rules (Q94–Q97, Q99, Q100, Q102):**

- **Levels** (configuration, `school.levels`): an ordered list, each with a stable `code`, a short `label` (`1ª`, `CM1`, `6e`, `Year 7`, `III`), a full `name`, a `stage` (infanzia, primaria, secondaria-I…) and a `next` level (default: the following one; `null`: the pupil leaves the school). Infanzia is a stage like the others; an **istituto comprensivo** is one list with three stages, and the step from the last primaria level to the first secondaria level is a promotion, not a departure.
- **Groups** (data, not configuration — Q94): a class or section is **its own object in the database** (`class_group`), no longer two attributes of the patron. A group has a name (`B`, `Coccinelle`, `Oak`) and the level or levels it hosts (more than one = a **pluriclasse**/composite class); a student belongs to one group and has their own level (one of the group's levels). Groups are **persistent cohorts** (Q100): at the class upgrade the group advances with its pupils (`3 B` becomes `4 B`), and a group whose pupils leave is archived, never deleted. Groups are created, renamed, re-levelled and archived **only by `admin`** (Q99); whoever manages patrons only picks an existing group.
- **Teacher groups** (Q100, Q102): teachers belong to a group too, of a special kind (`class_group.kind = teachers`): it hosts no level, the class upgrade and the student synchronisation never touch it, and its name is its label (`Docenti`). The wizard creates one (`Docenti`); `admin` may add others (e.g. `Docenti primaria`, `Docenti secondaria` in a comprensivo, with an optional stage for the filters). A student is always in a student group and a teacher in a teacher group.
- **Class label** from a configurable template (`school.classLabel`, default `{level} {group}` → `3 B`, Q94); one shared function builds it everywhere (screens, cards, slips, notices, exports, reports).
- **History is a photo** (Q95): loans store a snapshot of the class as it was (level code and position, stage, group name, label) that **never changes**, not even when a group or level is renamed; reports on live data (current patrons and groups) follow the renames.
- **Reading levels** of books (Q96) are an **independent scale** (`classification.readingLevels`: code, label, shelf mark printed in the call number), not the school levels: the presets propose one level per class for primaria (1–5) and none for infanzia and secondaria, where several levels make little sense; the scale stays editable.
- **Presets** (Q94): Italian by default (`it-infanzia`, `it-primaria` (default), `it-secondaria-I`, `it-secondaria-II`, `it-comprensivo`) and ready presets for other countries (France, Germany, Spain, United Kingdom, USA, Poland, generic), each one an ordinary editable configuration once chosen. The facts of every foreign preset are checked against an official source before it ships (`[VERIFY]` items of the study).
- Student import (Q97, Q115): the class is one column `class` matched against the labels of the existing classes, or two columns `level` + `section` resolved to a level and a group (9.19).

**Steps:**

- [x] **12.1 Barcode format engine** — `#lib/shared/barcodes/` replaces `barcodes.ts`/`luhn.ts`: pattern parser and generator, the three check algorithms with published test vectors, `compareFormats(a, b)` (structural disjointness and the visible-sign rule, returning the colliding examples), the keyboard repair tables; `#lib/shared/scan.ts` becomes table-driven: `normalizeScan` and `recognise` take a **format table** compiled from the configuration (current, retired and legacy formats of each kind, plus ISBN/ISSN/EAN), which the client receives with the layout data so `BarcodeInput` and the camera scanner keep recognising locally; a separator is restored only when exactly one format matches.
  - Acceptance: unit tests per algorithm (vectors; every single error and adjacent transposition detected by `damm` and `mod23`), per token, for `compareFormats` (accepted and refused pairs, each refusal with its example), and a generated test that produces 10 000 codes of each kind for every preset and checks each is recognised as its own kind only, with and without the mishaps of every layout; desk, inventory, catalogue search and camera keep their behaviour on legacy codes.
  - Depends on: 11.4

- [x] **12.2 Barcode configuration, generation and editor** — `barcodes` section reshaped: `libraryCode`, `keyboardLayout`, `item` and `patron` each with `format` (`pattern`, `check`), `retired[]`, `legacy[]` (declared legacy shapes with their lengths, proposed from what the database holds), `symbology`, and `counterStart` for cards; the literal keys (`totalDigits`, `checkDigit`, `pattern`, `yearSource`, `acceptLegacy`) go. Counters named per format (`sequence`), "continue after the highest existing code" for any pattern; a **cross-table guard** (a new card number may not be an existing copy barcode and vice versa: creation, import, manual edit); catalogue, patron and loan imports validate codes against the format table (a code of the other kind is an error with its explanation). Administration → Codici a barre: preset choice (the new defaults, today's `YYYY-NNNNC` + 10 digits, letter prefix, Codabar 14 as in the study), the pattern editor behind "Avanzate", ten example codes per kind, the verdict of the rule ("copie e tessere non si possono confondere" or the two colliding codes), a "Prova un codice" box running the recognition table, the next code of each kind, the retired and legacy formats with their counts; changing a format moves the old one to `retired` and is audited. Coverage map and configuration archive updated; §3.7, §4.2–4.3, Appendix A, `config-reference.md` and the CLAUDE.md barcode rule rewritten.
