### Phase 13 — Photo census from class pictures (required for 0.9.0)

**New part of the project** (owner request, Q103–Q109). Patron photos arrive in four ways: imported one file per patron (3.9b), taken with the camera or uploaded from the patron's page (2.3), photographed one after the other in the census (2.4), and — new with this phase — **imported in bulk for a class or a list from a scanned or photographed picture with many faces**: the sheet of portraits the school photographer delivers, the class's cards laid on a table, a page of ID photos (Q104). Group photos without frames are out of scope: there is no face detection (Q104, Q107). This phase lets the operator upload such a picture, finds the photo frames in it and assigns each frame to a patron with one drop-down per photo. It builds on the class as an object of Phase 12 (`class_group`) and on the patron print lists (2.8), and runs **after Phase 12 and before the remaining steps of Phase 9** (Q103), so the operator guide and its illustrations (9.5) cover it. The demonstration uses `docs/reference/demo/class-cards.jpg` (ten cards of synthetic cartoon faces, four without a name) on the seeded class without photos (step 1.2: class 2 C; the group generated in its place by 12.8).

**Workflow (owner):** Censimento foto → choose the class **or the patron list** → "Da un'immagine" → upload (or take) the picture → automatic processing → **list of the photos found, each with a drop-down to pick the patron** → "Salva le foto". One patron per photo and one photo per patron.

**Rules:**

- **Census targets: classes and lists** (Q108, owner): the census (camera and picture alike) lets the operator choose among the classes (teacher groups included) **and the patron print lists** (2.8), and offers **only those with at least one patron without a photo**. The census and the print lists share one notion of "a set of patrons" (a class or a list → its active members), factored out of the two (`services/patron-sets.ts`), so the progress counter, the "senza foto" filter and the drop-down candidates are computed once.
- A **class picture** (`class_photo_sheet`) belongs to one class (`class_group`) **or** one patron list (`print_list` of kind `patrons`), exactly one of the two; each can have **several** pictures (a sheet per half of the class, a second sheet for latecomers). The original file is kept **until the class upgrade** (Q106): the upgrade (12.5) deletes every class picture, after the backup it already requires; a picture of a list also goes when its list is deleted. Until then the assignment can be finished later, by another operator or on another device.
- **Detection** finds the rectangular photo frames in the picture: portrait-shaped quadrilaterals (height/width between `photos.sheet.minAspect` and `maxAspect`, default 1.0 and 1.6, Q105), not nested in one another, of plausible size relative to the picture and to each other, slightly rotated or in perspective (cards photographed on a table). It runs **in the browser with OpenCV.js** (already vendored and lazy-loaded for the covers, ADR-022; no new dependency), reusing the candidate scoring of `#lib/vision/dewarp.ts` (side contrast, refinement onto the edges) generalised from "the best quad" to "every good quad". Frames are listed in reading order (rows top to bottom, then left to right). The browser sends only the corners: the **server** straightens each frame (a perspective transform in plain TypeScript, `#lib/server/images/warp.ts`) into a photo of exactly the configured size and keeps it next to the picture, so the crop the operator sees is the photo the patron gets. Face detection, OCR of the printed names and any server-side image analysis are **not** part of this phase (Q107; listed as possible future improvements in 10.11).
- **Correction before assignment:** the picture is shown with the frames drawn and numbered; the operator can drag a corner, delete a false frame or draw a missing one (two taps or a drag), and the crop updates. On a phone the same screen works in full-screen with the 44 px handles of the cover capture.
- **Assignment:** next to each crop a drop-down lists **only the patrons of the chosen class or list** (Q108; alphabetical; those without a photo first, marked; those with a photo show a thumbnail and "ha già una foto"); a patron outside it is reached by adding them to a list, not from the drop-down. A patron already chosen for another frame of the same class or list (in any of its pictures) is shown disabled with that frame's number; "Nessuno" leaves the frame unassigned and "Ignora" hides it. Choices are saved as **drafts** as they are made (the list survives a reload) and nothing touches the patrons until "Salva le foto".
- **Saving** follows §3.22: a summary first (N photos to save, M of them replace an existing photo, listed with the old and new thumbnails, a confirmation for the replacements), then one transaction that stores each crop as the patron's photo through the same service as the camera census (`savePatronPhoto`: configured size and format, metadata dropped, audit `patron.photo_upload` with the source `class_sheet`, no names in the log); the frames become "saved" and keep their patron. The progress counter of the class or list ("12 di 14 con foto") updates like in the camera census.
- **Privacy** (§3.13): the class picture is personal data of minors, often with names printed. It lives under `DATA_DIR/uploads/class-sheets/<sheet>/` with the photos of its frames (re-encoded, EXIF/GPS removed, longest side capped by `photos.sheet.maxPixels`, Q105), is served only through an authorised media route, travels with the "patron photos" part of backups, exports and restores (11.12), and is deleted at the class upgrade (Q106). **Anonymizing a patron** (2.9) during the year **paints over every frame assigned to them** in the stored pictures (a solid grey a little beyond the frame, irreversible) and erases the frame's photo, so the face no longer exists anywhere while the picture stays with its class or list (Q106; the frame becomes `erased`). The photo file goes inside the removal's transaction; the painting, which decodes and encodes the picture, runs right after it, and the picture route, the backups and the exports run it too before they read a picture. The confirmation of the removal says how many frames are covered. "Elimina immagine" removes the picture and its frames at any time; photos already saved stay on the patrons.
- Permission **`patrons`** (like the census).
- **Limits are configuration** (Q105, owner: configurable values): `photos.sheet` — `maxUploadBytes` (default 32 MB, a picture of the whole class is larger than a portrait), `maxPixels` (longest side stored, default 4 000 px), `maxFrames` (default 60), `minAspect` and `maxAspect` (1.0 and 1.6: the photos of the demo cards measure 1.10–1.18, too close to the 1.1 first proposed) — validated by the schema, edited in the photos editor (11.8), in the coverage map and the configuration archive (Phase 11 completeness rule).
- **Labels match the page they open** (owner): the patrons area tab says "Censimento foto", like the page, the dashboard tile and the quick action. Survey of the same mismatch elsewhere, fixed with 13.3: the area tabs "In prestito" (page "Libri in prestito") and "Desideri" (page "Lista desideri", in the desk and catalogue areas), and "Modelli" in the print templates area where every page and the administration card say "Template di stampa" (two words for one thing). The tabs inside the Importazioni and Configurazione areas ("Catalogo" for "Importa il catalogo", "Cronologia" for "Cronologia della configurazione") shorten a title the area already explains and stay as they are.

**Steps:**

- [x] **13.1 Multi-frame detection** (three clear sides are enough — a white shirt on white paper leaves no bottom edge —, and a frame whose clear side goes on past a corner is one cut short) — `#lib/vision/frames.ts`: `detectFrames(cv, image, options)` → frames `{ quad, score }[]` in reading order, built on the passes and the `sideContrasts`/`refineQuad` scoring of `dewarp.ts` (shared helpers moved, not copied): every convex quad with portrait proportions and all four sides above the contrast threshold, nested and overlapping duplicates merged (the inner photo of a card wins over the card, the tile over the grid cell), outliers by size dropped (a frame much smaller or larger than the median of the others), a frame lying almost wholly inside one at most 2.5 times its size dropped as a detail of that photo; on a scan (`SCAN_DETECTION`) only rectangles drawn by long straight edges, upright or turned by any angle up to 45° (9.26, Q126: the picture is looked at as it is and turned by the angles of its long lines, `scanAngles`, at most five, the rectangles found there turned back; a turn adds only the photos the upright look did not outline; on a photo the same rectangles are looked for when the quadrilaterals are fewer than five, and replace them when they find more photos, some not outlined by any quadrilateral, so a scan sent as a photo gives the frames of a scan) (`rectanglesFromRuns`: colour edges at low thresholds, opened with long thin kernels across and down; every side drawn for 80 % by a line that does not run on past both corners, or by the line's own runs when the photo is glued against the edge of its card or sleeve; full along every side inside, lighter or darker than the paper's grey or coloured, or, for a portrait on a white background, full in the middle and along the bottom and half full on both sides, `photoFill`; at least half a percent of the picture, so the rectangles inside a stamp's rings are left out; of two about the same size the one with sharper sides), for pale photos on cards crossed by stamps, touched by ruled lines, in plastic sleeves; a photo with a white background on a white card can stay unfound and is drawn by hand; `readingOrder(frames)` (rows by vertical overlap, then x); the crop is made on the server (13.2). Fixtures: `tests/fixtures/images/class-cards.jpg` (the demo picture) and `portraits-sheet.jpg` (the 30-face sheet of step 1.2) with hand-measured ground truth (`*.frames.json`: corners of every frame), plus synthetic sheets generated like the cover benchmark (grids of 2–40 portraits, rotation up to 10°, perspective, white, coloured and textured backgrounds, shadows, a phone photo of a printed sheet) and synthetic scans of a page of school cards (eight cards a little askew, ID photos on pale backgrounds, ruled lines with handwriting, printed lines, a stamp across each photo's corner).

`services/class-sheets.ts` (`uploadSheet` re-encodes and stores the picture, `setFrames` stores the frames the browser found (once) and `addFrame` / `moveFrame` / `deleteFrame` the operator's corrections, each frame straightened and stored as a photo-sized crop, `assignFrame` / `ignoreFrame` (drafts, uniqueness checked with a readable error naming the other frame), `planSave` (summary of §3.22: new, replacing, unassigned), `saveSheet` writes the crops as the patrons' photos (new files first, then one transaction, then the replaced files removed), `deleteSheet`, `deleteSheetsAtUpgrade` called by the class upgrade (12.5), `eraseFramesOfPatron` used by the anonymization and `paintErasedFrames` right after it); `photos.sheet` (`maxUploadBytes`, `maxPixels`, `maxFrames`, `minAspect`, `maxAspect`) in the schema, the photos editor (11.8), the coverage map and the configuration archive; `docs/database.md` updated.

## 7. Testing strategy

Everything is tested. **Playwright** is the backbone for anything a user can see; **Vitest** covers the logic underneath. One CI job gates every step (§0 rule 3).

| Layer | Tool | Scope | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unit | Vitest 5, `server` project | pure modules (`#lib/shared/*`, `#lib/server/**`): ISBN, Luhn, calendar, rules engine, UNIMARC mapping, CSV mapping, report safety, anonymization, config schema, card rendering; services against an in-memory migrated SQLite | coverage via `@vitest/coverage-v8`: **95 % lines** (CI gate, Q49) on `#lib/server/services`, `#lib/shared`, `#lib/server/{auth,barcodes,marc,z3950,config,covers}`; every rules-engine reason and calendar mode has a test |
| Component | Vitest 5 browser mode (`@vitest/browser`, Playwright provider, Chromium) | `BarcodeInput`, `CameraCapture` error states, `FormErrors`, `DataGrid` wrapper, `PhotoCapture` crop math | real Chromium, no jsdom |
| End-to-end | Playwright Test 1.64 | every user-facing flow named in the §5 acceptance criteria: login/logout/roles, patron CRUD, census with fake camera, print lists (build, scan-to-add, print, mark printed), card preview and print, cataloging with the mocked metadata provider, cover capture with a fixture image, checkout/check-in/renew with calendar cases, overdues and slips, reports and CSV export, class upgrade, anonymization, imports, first-run wizard, setup checklist, labels, admin pages | projects `desktop-chromium` (1280 × 800) and `mobile-chromium` (viewport 412 × 915 CSS px, `deviceScaleFactor` 2, touch); Chromium in every everyday run (`BV_BROWSER=firefox|webkit` and `bun run test:browsers` for the other engines, Q110), mobile is tested by viewport size only, no device emulation and no real devices (Q77); trace and video on first retry; `webServer` boots the app on a temporary seeded `DATA_DIR` |
| Visual regression | Playwright `toHaveScreenshot` | card front/back/sheet, labels, slips, dashboard, checkout desk (light theme) | baselines in `tests/snapshots/`, generated on Linux Chromium inside the CI container image to avoid font drift |
| Accessibility | `@axe-core/playwright` inside the e2e suite | login, dashboard, checkout, check-in, patrons, catalog, reports, census | no `serious` or `critical` violations |
| Print output | Playwright with `page.emulateMedia({ media: 'print' })` | card sheet: unit size, fold-line position, units per page, page size read from computed styles (mm → px at 96 dpi); `page.pdf()` saved as a CI artifact for manual inspection | assertions on computed geometry |
| Integration (opt-in) | Vitest with `Z3950_LIVE=1` | live SBN lookup, Open Library lookup | not in CI by default (network, courtesy to SBN) |
| Documentation screenshots | `bun run docs:shots` (Italian in `bun run ci`, every language in the site build), `bun run site:check` and `bun run site:build` | every picture of the shot list taken (a failed step or a missing element fails the run); figure markers, bold UI labels against the dictionaries, anchors and step numbers matching between the languages, the anchors of the help links (9.5b) | build or check fails |
| Container | shell script in CI | image builds for both platforms; `/healthz` OK; `yaz-client -V`; runs as uid 1000; read-only root FS | on `main` and tags |

Conventions:
- Unit and component tests sit next to the code (`foo.test.ts`); e2e specs live in `tests/e2e/<area>/<flow>.spec.ts`, one flow per file, using page objects in `tests/e2e/pages/`.
- Fixtures in `tests/fixtures/{marc/*.mrc, csv/*.csv, images/*.jpg, video/*.y4m}`, captured once, committed, no personal data. The seed script (1.2) uses a fixed faker seed so specs can rely on known names and barcodes.
- Camera flows run on Chromium with `--use-fake-device-for-media-stream --use-file-for-fake-video-capture=<fixture.y4m>` (fixture video showing a real EAN-13 and a Code 128).
- External providers (SBN, Open Library) are replaced in e2e by `METADATA_PROVIDER=mock`, which swaps the provider chain for fixture-backed responses.
- Playwright runs under Node (Q37); everything else under Bun. `bun run test` = unit + component; `bun run test:e2e` = Playwright chromium projects; `bun run ci` = lint + check + unit (with the 95 % coverage gate) + component + e2e + i18n key parity; visual snapshots and slow suites run nightly and on tags. Chromium is the browser of `bun run ci` (Q49). **`bun run test:browsers`** (Q110) runs the component tests and the whole Playwright suite on Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, one engine after the other, each on a fresh server and database (projects `desktop-<engine>`, `mobile-<engine>`; Firefox has no mobile mode (`isMobile`), so it runs the desktop project only and the phones are covered by Chromium and WebKit; no visual comparison outside Chromium, whose fonts the baselines are; WebKit through Playwright's container image when the host lacks Ubuntu's libraries). It is opt-in and required before 1.0 (9.10).
- Reports (ADR-021): every run writes HTML reports under `test-reports/` (gitignored) — Vitest as one self-contained file with the coverage in its Coverage tab (`vitest/index.html`, `vitest/coverage/`), Playwright's own report (`playwright/index.html`) — and `bun run ci` ends by writing `test-reports/index.html` (`scripts/test-report.ts`, also `bun run test:report`): counts per project, failures, the coverage and the files under the 95 % line target, with links. GitHub's CI uploads the folder as the `test-reports` artifact.
- A step that adds a screen without a Playwright spec, or a service without unit tests, is not done.
- A reported bug is fixed test first (simplified TDD): a test that reproduces it (unit or Playwright, whichever reaches the fault) is written and seen to fail, then the code is changed and the same test is seen to pass; the test stays in the suite.

## 8. Definition of done (per step and for v1)

Per step: acceptance criteria met → tests added → `bun run ci` green → docs touched if behaviour changed → checkbox ticked here → STATUS.md line → commit.

For v0.9.0: all Phase 0–9, 11, 12 and 13 steps ticked (except 9.17, the public demo, and 9.10, which follows the release); every §11 question either answered by the owner or resolved by its default with an ADR; device matrix executed; a fresh deployment performed from `docs/deploy.md` alone.

## 9. Risks and mitigations

| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| Remote functions API changes (experimental in SvelteKit 3) | rework | Pin exact versions; upgrade only at phase boundaries; thin remote layer over services (§3.4) so churn stays local. |
| Skeleton 5 with SvelteKit 3 (documented minimum is SvelteKit 2) | build or runtime issues | Verified first thing in 0.3; Skeleton is plain Svelte 5 components plus CSS, so an incompatibility would be superficial; fallback shadcn-svelte. |
| `yaz-client` wrapping fragile (output parsing, process handling) | metadata lookups fail | Parse only the ISO 2709 dump file (never human-readable stdout except hit count); timeouts; fixtures; manual cataloguing always possible; opt-in Open Library provider. |
| SBN policy / availability changes | lookups fail | Provider chain + cache; user can always catalogue manually. |
| Bun under QEMU crashes in multi-arch CI | no arm64 image | Build stage on `$BUILDPLATFORM`; native arm64 runners. |
| OpenCV.js payload on slow phones | poor UX | Lazy + immutable caching; PWA precache (9.9); manual-corner fallback (Q6). |
| HID scanner keyboard-layout mangling | wrong barcodes | Normalisation in `BarcodeInput` (§3.7); document scanner configuration. |
| Camera requires HTTPS | census/cataloging unusable on LAN | Tailscale Serve certificates; `localhost` for desktop dev; documented. |
| Personal data of minors | legal | §3.13 minimisation, protected media routes, anonymization, backups restricted. |
| Single SQLite file corruption (SD card on a Pi) | data loss | WAL + nightly `VACUUM INTO` + volume backups; recommend SSD. |
| sveltekit-i18n 3 is a young major version (runes core) | bugs, API churn | Exact version pin; thin wrapper in `#lib/i18n`; the JSON dictionaries are library-agnostic, so a 60-line custom `t()` is the fallback. |

## 11. Open questions and doubts (numbered — to be discussed with the owner)

Each item: **question — why it matters — default used until decided (and recommendation).** A ticked box `[x]` means the owner has decided; the decision is written in the item. Unticked items apply their default until discussed.

### Stack and tooling

- [x] **Q1 — UI kit (resolved, confirmed by the owner): Skeleton v5.** The owner likes Skeleton and daisyUI and asked for the more complete one. daisyUI 5 is CSS-only (no behaviour or accessibility logic beyond native elements); Skeleton 5 combines a Tailwind 4 design system (themes, presets, typography, forms) with functional Svelte 5 components built on **Zag.js** state machines (Dialog, Toast, Combobox, Listbox, Menu, Popover, Tooltip, Tabs, Pagination, Date picker, File upload, …), which is exactly what the circulation and patron screens need (autocomplete, dialogs, toasts) and is the more robust option. Flowbite Svelte and shadcn-svelte are dropped; daisyUI is not mixed in. Remaining doubt: only the SvelteKit 3 RC compatibility check in step 0.3 (Skeleton documents SvelteKit 2 as its minimum).
- [x] **Q2 — Chart library (resolved):** Chart.js 4.5.1 (simple, well known) vs LayerChart 2.5 (Svelte-native, d3). — *Decision: Chart.js, registering only the bar, line and doughnut controllers, lazy-loaded on report pages; it is the simplest option that still gives tooltips, legends and responsive resizing without custom code. No sophisticated charts are needed.*
- [x] **Q3 — SvelteKit 3 RC and TypeScript 6 (resolved):** keep the latest versions that work together without compatibility tricks: SvelteKit 3 with TypeScript 6.x; migrate to TypeScript 7 when 7.1 (with the programmatic API) lands and the Svelte tooling supports it. Exact pins, updated deliberately at phase boundaries.
- [x] **Q4 — Drizzle version (resolved):** Drizzle ORM and Kit **1.0.0-rc.4** (the `rc` tag the official docs default to), pinned exactly, moved to 1.0.0 when GA.
- [x] **Q5 — Camera scanning library (resolved):** `barcode-detector` (zxing-wasm).
- [x] **Q6 — OpenCV.js payload (resolved):** automatic detection with OpenCV.js lazy-loaded (about 10.5 MiB, cached after the first download) with a determinate progress indicator; corners adjustable after detection; no manual-only mode.
- [x] **Q7 — Item barcode format (resolved):** new items get `YYYY-NNNNC` (year, per-year counter, Luhn check digit over the eight digits); the rule applies only to barcodes generated for new books. Existing codes are mixed (plain counters from the past, `YYYY-NNNN` more recently) and must keep working unchanged (§3.7). `YYYY` is the **calendar year of cataloguing** (current date when the item is created, in the configured time zone), not the school year, which spans two calendar years (resolved).
- [x] **Q8 — Patron card number (resolved):** new cards are 10 digits, the last one a Luhn check digit (9-digit counter); legacy cards were plain counters and stay valid; new numbers are generated when new names are imported or created. Remaining detail: the counter's starting value (default: continues after the highest legacy number so the two ranges never overlap). Legacy detail seen on the current cards: numbers are zero-padded to 10 digits (`0000001240`), the same length as the new format, so `counterStart: afterLegacyMax` is what keeps the ranges apart and recognition never relies on the check digit alone (§3.7).
- [x] **Q9 — Dash and scanner input (resolved):** the dash stays; input is normalised (`'`, space or nothing accepted in place of `-`).
- [x] **Q10 — Roles (resolved):** a small fixed set of permissions (`loans`, `patrons`, `patrons.import`, `catalog`, `catalog.import`, `reports`, `anonymize`, `admin`) and roles defined in `config.json` as bundles of permissions (§3.5), with the sensible defaults of Appendix A; editable from the UI in Phase 11.

### Domain rules

- [x] **Q11 — Patron types (resolved):** only `student` and `teacher`; library operators are not patrons but staff accounts defined in `config.json` with roles and permissions (§3.5, Q20). Personal fields stay minimal: an optional email, no phone, no birth date (Q122).
- [x] **Q12 — School structure (resolved; superseded by Phase 12, Q94: configurable stages and years, classes as groups):** `library.maxClassYear` is configurable, at most 5 (primaria, secondaria II) and 3 for secondaria I; v1 targets primaria and secondaria I. Sections are single letters in Italy; the field accepts up to 8 uppercase letters/digits so other systems fit, validated against `library.sections`.
- [x] **Q13 — Loan rules (resolved):** students 15 days, 1 loan at a time, 5 renewals; teachers 15 days, 4 loans, 5 renewals (renewal length 15 days). Secondary details keep their defaults: renew from today, no block when overdue, no hard due date, `consultazione` not for loan; DVD rules are Q68.
- [x] **Q14 — Calendar maintenance (resolved):** national holidays computed automatically; school closures typed into JSON by the admin, checked on the read-only calendar page, reload without restart.
- [x] **Q15 — Fines (resolved):** none, ever; overdue days are shown only.
- [x] **Q16 — Holds/reservations (resolved):** not planned at all; the `hold` table and Phase 10 step are removed.
- [x] **Q17 — Overdue notifications (resolved):** print only in v1, reproducing the current "solleciti" (§3.12, Appendix H): blocks one after another, page break per class as an option, `minDays` 5 by default (a book is listed only when at least 5 days overdue, configurable). Sending the same notices by email is planned (Phase 10.2) and the slip rendering is designed to be reused as the email body.
- [x] **Q18 — Public interface (resolved): none.** The product is staff-only: no OPAC, no self-checkout, no patron self-service, no Funnel; every route except `/login` and `/healthz` requires a staff session. Class reading lists (10.4) are produced by staff as printouts or exports.
- [x] **Q19 — Localization (resolved): sveltekit-i18n 3.0.0**, with Italian and English both complete from the first screen (per-user switching dropped by Q52: one language per installation). Chosen over svelte-i18n because its documented per-request server instance makes per-user locales safe under SSR, it is Svelte-5-native (runes core) and freshly released, and it stays simple (`i18n.t('ns.key')`, JSON files, per-route lazy loading). Remaining doubt: the language of printed material and exports (Q52).
- [x] **Q20 — Authentication (resolved):** simple custom password sessions, no Tailscale identity login (step 8.6 dropped). Accounts are defined in `config.json` (`security.users`) and are normally shared per role rather than personal; the general case with personal accounts managed from the admin UI is foreseen as Phase 10.1 (`security.auth.mode = 'database'`). No operator-name tracking (Q69).

### Deployment and operations

- [x] **Q21 — Network topology (resolved, revised by Q89):** every client is on the tailnet and no other access exists (no plain-HTTP LAN mode — since Q89 a basic local-network mode exists as well, with limits). Deployment is the Tailscale **sidecar** only (a second container from the official image sharing the app's network namespace and running `tailscale serve`); no all-in-one image. Hostname `biblioviva`.
- [x] **Q22 — Target host (resolved):** four profiles in the `performance` section, each key overridable (§3.14, Appendix A): `micro` = one slow core and 512 MB (step 9.28); `small` (default) = Raspberry Pi 4 with 2 GB and an SD card, the only profile that assumes an SD card (64-bit OS required); `medium` = an old PC with a spinning hard disk; `large` = a PC with plenty of RAM and an SSD.
- [x] **Q23 — Code hosting and CI (resolved):** GitHub public repository, GitHub Actions with native arm64 runners, images on GHCR.
- [x] **Q24 — License (resolved):** `LICENSE` now contains the GPL-3.0 text and the project is **GPL-3.0-or-later** (`package.json` `"license": "GPL-3.0-or-later"`). Chosen over GPL-2 because Apache-2.0 dependencies (OpenCV.js and others) are compatible only with GPL-3, and over AGPL because the app is self-hosted by schools, where AGPL's network clause adds obligations without benefit. Same family as Koha (GPL-3). The agent adds SPDX headers in step 0.1. Remaining doubt: none, unless you prefer AGPL-3.0-or-later.
- [x] **Q25 — Z39.50 implementation (resolved):** the `yaz-client` wrapper is the permanent solution; a pure-TypeScript client will never be needed (Phase 10.7 dropped).
- [x] **Q26 — Metadata providers (resolved):** only the public OPAC SBN Z39.50 service by default (`metadata.providers: ["sbn"]`). Clarification: the Z39.50 access is public and documented (Appendix F); what is *not* public is the JSON gateway used by the SBN mobile app (`opacmobilegw`), which stays available as an opt-in provider without any guarantee. Open Library and Google Books remain opt-in metadata providers.
- [x] **Q27 — Covers (resolved, confirmed by the owner; Koha itself uses Amazon covers):** cover providers are configurable (`covers.providers`), default order Google Books (Italian market, `country=IT`) → Amazon (`amazon.it` catalogue through the undocumented image URL by ISBN-10; no API and no terms, so it may stop working and the chain falls through silently) → Open Library; on demand via a button, `autoFetch` optional; stored at 600 px (§3.9, step 3.7b).
- [x] **Q28 — Photo policy (resolved):** consent handled by the school; photos visible to every staff role with patron read access, printed on cards, deleted at anonymization.
- [x] **Q29 — Printing details (resolved):** cards on A4 as front/back units, 4 per sheet, 6 mm margins, thin red cut lines, no fold marks, colour printer. Labels are Q56.
- [x] **Q30 — Catalog codes (resolved):** the *segnatura* is the first three letters of the author's surname, used for alphabetical order; labels show `B 4 DEL` when reading classes are enabled, `B DEL` otherwise, and non-fiction always `NNN SSS` (`590 ROS`). Shelving in this library: with classes a shelf per class, grouped by genre, alphabetical within; without classes by genre or, for non-fiction, by subject; the patterns stay configurable for other libraries. Locations, item types, place and price: defaults of Appendix A (optional fields).
- [x] **Q31 — Import sources (resolved):** students come as a simple CSV `nome;cognome;classe;sezione` (2.6). The catalogue comes from the school's Koha: no direct export in the needed shape exists, so the repo ships SQL queries to run on the Koha database that produce the CSV files for books and items, patrons with their card numbers, and open loans (3.9). UNIMARC file import is future development (10.10).
- [x] **Q32 — Class upgrade and removal (resolved):** no date-driven rollover. An admin function "Avanza di classe" adds one to every student's class, lists for confirmation the students beyond the last class and anonymizes the confirmed ones immediately; exceptions (repeating pupils, section changes) are handled in the same wizard. A student removed at any time (manually or by the synchronisation) is anonymized immediately (2.9, 7.1).
- [x] **Q33 — Anonymization scope (resolved):** immediate; erases every personal datum (see Q71) and keeps class, section, type and the loan history for statistics.
- [x] **Q34 — Reports (resolved):** SQL in JSON is acceptable; built-in list as in 6.3; exports CSV and ODS only. **No proprietary format, ever** (no XLSX, no DOCX): open formats only, in both directions.
- [x] **Q35 — Backups (resolved, extended):** in-app daily backup with configurable retention (`backup.keepDays`, default 30); the owner asked that photos and covers be part of the backup and that an admin can download **one archive with everything** (database, images, configuration with templates and their images) — step 1.9b. A second copy of the backups with **rclone** is integrated as an optional third compose service (`offsite`, official `rclone/rclone` image, `./backups` mounted at `/backups`, runs `rclone sync` every hour and writes `offsite.json` that the backups page shows): by default into a folder of another disk (`OFFSITE_DIR` mounted at `/offsite`: a second disk, a USB drive, a folder of the school's server), or to any destination rclone supports, made once with `rclone config` (its `rclone.conf` in `./rclone/`), preferably with an account the school already has, never a particular provider (ADR-049); documented in `docs/deploy.md` with links to rclone's documentation. Running rclone on the host instead stays possible and is described too.
- [x] **Q36 — PWA (resolved, moved):** step 9.9, before the 0.9.0 release.
- [x] **Q37 — Playwright runtime (resolved):** Playwright runs under Node (installed for it only); Chromium is the sole target browser, desktop and mobile projects differ by viewport only.
- [x] **Q38 — Seed/demo data and first run (resolved):** synthetic Italian data for development and demos; the **first-run wizard** (1.11) shown at the first start of the container asks for the language, the theme colour, the library name and the admin password, and offers "Carica dati dimostrativi" (the seed, clearly marked as demo in the UI) so a demo installation is ready in a minute.
- [x] **Q39 — Audit log retention (resolved):** 24 months.
- [x] **Q40 — Circulation UX (resolved):** separate checkout and check-in pages; every inconsistency is detected and explained with a clear, unambiguous message that says what happened and what to do, with the suggested action as a button (catalogue in Appendix I, maintained as the single source for these texts). No sounds at the desk: the only sound of the application is the beep of a camera read (Q137, 9.40). Receipt printing stays optional.
- [x] **Q41 — Single site (resolved):** one library per installation, no branches.
- [x] **Q42 — Item statuses (resolved):** `available / lost / damaged / withdrawn / in_repair`; lost items are just marked, no charge.
- [x] **Q43 — Development host (partly resolved):** Bun 1.4.2 is installed; `yaz` still has to be installed locally (`sudo dnf install yaz`) in step 0.1. OK? — *Default: yes.*
- [x] **Q44 — Grids on phones (resolved):** SVAR grid for list pages (responsive, horizontal scrolling on phones); custom lists for the mobile-first flows (census, checkout).
- [x] **Q45 — Loan history retention (resolved):** keep the full history forever (statistics); anonymization is the only pruning.
- [x] **Q46 — Search behaviour (resolved):** accent-insensitive everywhere; SBN queries without diacritics.
- [x] **Q47 — School year boundary and time zone (resolved):** time zone `library.timezone` (default `Europe/Rome`); `library.schoolYearStart` kept at 1 September but used only to label loans and pre-fill report date ranges; nothing happens automatically on a date.
- [x] **Q48 — Card content (resolved):** the default reproduces the current cards (§3.12, Appendix C). The template engine must allow **completely different cards**: a template owns the whole HTML and CSS of both faces (layout, fonts as `.woff2` assets, background images, portrait or landscape content, with or without photo or barcode), and may override `cardMm`; the renderer only fixes the face size, the unit and the sheet. Logo and back illustration to be supplied into `CONFIG_DIR` (never into the repo).
- [x] **Q49 — Test depth (resolved):** Chromium only, everywhere (no cross-browser matrix); coverage gate raised to **95 %** lines on services and shared code; a Playwright spec per flow and visual snapshots for print layouts as in §7.
- [x] **Q50 — Text discipline for i18n (resolved):** all UI text in the `it/` and `en/` dictionaries from the first screen; the key-parity test is a hard CI **error** when the two key sets differ, not a warning.
- [x] **Q51 — Theme and colours (resolved):** a Skeleton preset with a saturated primary colour, light only; a custom theme with the school's colours can be swapped in later without code changes; dark mode never.
- [x] **Q52 — Language (resolved):** one language for the whole installation, `ui.locale`, chosen in the first-run wizard and editable in the configuration; it applies to the UI, printed material and exports. No per-user setting.

### Classification and labels

- [x] **Q53 — Fiction reading class (resolved; superseded by Q96: an independent scale of reading levels, step 12.6):** the recommended class, primary schools only, a single value 1…`maxClassYear`, enabled by `classification.fictionByClass`.
- [x] **Q54 — Taxonomies and images (resolved):** the structure of Appendix A is right; its labels and placeholder pictograms serve as test and demo data; the school's real pictograms and names go into `CONFIG_DIR` at installation (`classification:import-images`).
- [x] **Q55 — One genre per book (resolved):** one genre; free keywords stay in `subjects`.
- [x] **Q56 — Label stock (resolved):** default `a4-70x48` confirmed. Half-height labels (`a4-70x24`) and black-and-white printers must also work: the template adapts through variants chosen by the cell height and never relies on colour alone (§3.12). A visual designer for labels, spine bands and cards is step 11.10, part of Phase 11 and therefore of 0.9.0 (owner decision). Drawing and code edit the same files, in both directions (11.16).
- [x] **Q57 — Spine band content (resolved):** exactly as the current printout by default; anything else through the template.
- [x] **Q58 — Kinds and materials (resolved):** two classification kinds (fiction, non-fiction) apply to every material. DVDs exist and are classified like books: they are an item type (`dvd` in `library.itemTypes`, which is generic and extensible), identified by their EAN in `biblio.ean` (§4.1, step 3.6). Loan rules for DVDs are Q68.
- [x] **Q59 — Taxonomy editing (resolved):** JSON at first; the taxonomy editor is the first configuration screen to build (11.1); Phase 11 is required for the release (0.9.0) and starts with it.
- [x] **Q60 — XLSX import (resolved):** CSV only, also after v1; an Excel file from the school register is converted by hand before importing. No spreadsheet reader will be added.
- [x] **Q61 — Print lists (resolved):** shared, kept until deleted, "svuota dopo la stampa" as an option, no size limit.
- [x] **Q62 — Icons (resolved):** Lucide through its official Svelte package `@lucide/svelte`; Iconify dropped.
- [x] **Q63 — Platforms (resolved):** only `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64` images, on Linux hosts and on Windows through Docker Desktop (Q130). No 32-bit support (Bun has no 32-bit builds, §3.21). The small runtime facade `#lib/server/runtime/` is kept as good hygiene, not as a commitment to a Node variant.
- [x] **Q64 — Configuration UI scope (resolved, revised):** Phase 11 as listed, required for the release (0.9.0), complete (every setting editable from the UI) and with a single-archive export and import of the configuration.
- [x] **Q65 — Barcode symbology (resolved):** **Code 128** for every new card and label (`barcodes.patron.symbology`, `barcodes.item.symbology`, template default); the existing Code 39 cards and labels keep working because scanning is symbology-agnostic (scanner and camera decode both).
- [x] **Q66 — Genre letters (resolved):** the full list comes from Koha's CCODE authorized values (`docs/reference/classification/koha-ccode.csv`): A Romanzi e Racconti, B Romanzi Storici - Educazione Civica, C Stampato Maiuscolo, D Avventura, E Fantasy, F Miti e Leggende, G Favole e Fiabe, H Fantascienza e Scienza, I Gialli e Horror, L Racconti Umoristici, M Fumetti, N Libri Game, P Lingua Straniera, R Poesia e Teatro; MMM Milano is a non-fiction node. The letters are not initials. The library's pictogram sheet was an example only and is not kept in the repository (step 11.14): BiblioViva ships generated pictograms.
- [x] **Q67 — Single labels (resolved):** all three kinds are needed: the pair (default) plus barcode-only and spine-only print jobs (replacement barcode, new spine band after a reclassification).
- [x] **Q68 — DVD loan rules (resolved):** 5 days and 1 renewal (`circulation.itemTypeOverrides.dvd`), configurable like everything else; the number of DVDs at once follows the patron's `maxLoans`; same label pair.
- [x] **Q69 — Shared accounts and the audit trail (resolved):** the audit log records the account only; no operator-name prompt, not even optional.
- [x] **Q70 — Tailscale account model (resolved):** one school Tailscale account logged in on every staff device (within the free Personal plan, limits to be checked when the guide is written); personal accounts only mentioned in a side note of the setup guide.
- [x] **Q71 — What is erased at anonymization (resolved):** all personal data: first and last name, photo, email, notes, circulation note, block reason, external id and card number. Everything that allows statistics stays: class, section, patron type, loan history with its snapshots.
- [x] **Q72 — Class per copy (resolved):** copies of the same title may sit in different class shelves (wrong in principle, practical for this library). `classification.classPerItem`, default `false`; when on, `item.reading_class` replaces `biblio.reading_class` (only one of the two is ever filled). The catalogue import detects the situation during validation and asks whether to enable the flag (§3.17, §3.22).
- [x] **Q73 — Data model trimmed to the Koha fast framework (resolved):** dropped `contributors`, `edition`, `language`, `summary`, `pages`, item `location`, item `price`, second item note; `subjects` kept; `physical_description` replaces pages/illustrations/size; `author` and `publisher` mandatory with configurable special values (`Anonimo`, `AA. VV.`, `s.n.`, `s.l.`); Dewey numeric part only; `in_repair` stays as an item status.
- [x] **Q74 — Imports validate first (resolved):** every import validates the whole file, asks its questions, and commits in one transaction only when there are zero errors and the operator confirms (§3.22). No partial imports.
- [x] **Q75 — Periodicals (resolved):** minimal support: `biblio.issn`, item type `rivista`, `item.enumeration` for the issue; no subscription management.
- [x] **Q76 — Review follow-ups (resolved):** removing students during the synchronisation needs `anonymize`, which only `admin` holds by default (fine as is); `cards.validityMonths`, `cards.showPhoto` and the `valid_until` field stay for other schools; the external id is not needed by this library but stays supported through a second CSV format with `matricola` as first column, detected automatically or chosen by the operator (2.6).
- [x] **Q77 — What "mobile" means for tests (resolved):** Chromium with a phone-sized window is enough; no Android device or emulator. The mobile viewport is 412 × 915 CSS pixels with device scale factor 2, the size a recent high-resolution phone reports to the browser, so pages are not designed for tiny screens.
- [x] **Q78 — One configuration file or several (resolved):** several. The owner wants templates for cards and labels (and similar bulky, repetitive sections) as separate files that can be copied and edited to make new ones. `config.json` keeps the core; `<section>.json` fragments, `labels/stocks/*.json` and one folder per template are merged or discovered at load time (§3.6, step 2.4a). Appendix A stays a single document for readability.
- [x] **Q79 — Manual blocking of patrons (resolved):** none. The owner does not need a "blocca utente" feature: the block/unblock action, the `blocked` status, the `block_reason` column and the `patron_blocked` desk message are removed. Circulation restrictions come only from the rules (loan limit, overdue policy, `blockIfOverdue`); the circulation note remains the way to leave a message for the desk.
- [x] **Q80 — Importing patron photos from Koha (resolved):** yes, in the Koha migration chapter. Koha keeps the pictures in the `patronimage` table (`borrowernumber`, `mimetype`, `imagefile` BLOB) [VERIFY on the school's Koha version]; the repo ships a documented `mysql` export that writes one file per patron **named after `borrowers.cardnumber`** (`<cardnumber>.jpg`, they are expected to be JPEG) into a folder, and BiblioViva imports that folder with a simple procedure (step 3.9b): match by card number, run each file through the census photo pipeline (WebP 480 × 640, EXIF dropped), report the unmatched ones. Photos and covers are always **files** under `DATA_DIR/uploads` (§3.10); the database stores only their paths — no binary blobs in SQLite.
- [x] **Q81 — Language packs from the configuration folder (resolved):** yes. English stays built in as the base; the Italian dictionaries **stay in the repository** (`src/lib/i18n/it/`, tested with the parity rule) and are copied into `CONFIG_DIR/i18n/it/` at first run as the example to clone; any other language is a folder in `CONFIG_DIR/i18n/<code>/` validated before loading (same files, keys and placeholders as English, a `pack.json` with name and target version), rejected with a readable warning when invalid and merged over English key by key when valid (gaps logged, never a crash). The build-time "missing key fails the build" rule keeps applying to the built-in languages; for packs it becomes a runtime validation with fallback. Step 1.14.
- [x] **Q82 — Database migrations until 1.0 (resolved):** no incremental migrations before the real 1.0, field tests included. The single migration of ADR-011 is regenerated from the schema at every change through 0.9.x as well; a database in use at the school during the field tests is carried across a schema change by exporting the data ("Esporta tutto" or the granular export of 11.12) and restoring it into a fresh database, never by a migration. Incremental migrations start at 1.0.0 (9.37: a backup before them, an upgrade test from every release).
- [x] **Q83 — What the catalogue grid loads (resolved):** nothing until a filter is set: without filters there is no reason to see the whole catalogue. The grid loads when any filter is chosen or when the text search has at least 3 characters (a scanned barcode or ISBN still opens its record at once); the page shows a short hint in place of the grid otherwise. Implemented in 9.2.
- [x] **Q84 — Series (resolved):** the owner wants the series and the number in the series (Harry Potter, 3), reversing that part of Q73. SBN records carry the publisher's *collana* in UNIMARC 225/410, not the narrative series (no Salani Harry Potter record has one), so the series is typed by hand with suggestions from the catalogue, and a lookup fills it only from a real series (MARC 21 490/800/830); the collana is ignored. Step 9.14.
- [x] **Q85 — Which record a lookup uses (resolved):** up to 20 records (one ISBN can have one per reprint); an ISBN lookup opens the form with the most complete record, with "Altre schede" to see and pick the others; a title/author search, where the candidates are different editions, shows the list ordered by completeness. This revises the "never pick for the operator" rule of 3.5–3.6 for ISBN lookups only. Step 9.14.
- [x] **Q86 — Where the imports live (resolved):** the four imports (catalogue, patrons, open loans, patron photos) are gathered in Administration → Importazioni (`/admin/imports/…`), used when moving from Koha and, for the class lists, once a year by whoever runs the class upgrade; the Catalogo and Utenti pages no longer show an import button, and the default `cataloging` and `membership` roles lose `catalog.import` and `patrons.import`. Also settled with it, the layout rule of the page headers: the header holds the actions on the page (create, edit, print; one primary at most), the other pages of an area go in a row of choice buttons under the title (`AreaTabs`), as the circulation desk already did. 9.13 round 13.
- [x] **Q87 — Automatic submit of scanned codes (resolved):** none. The code field sends on Enter only; a handheld scanner is configured to send Enter after the code (its usual factory setting), and a person typing can check the code before sending. The field opens the phone keyboard with letters, since codes carry letters (no numeric keyboard, no keyboard button). 9.13 round 16 and 33.
- [x] **Q88 — Wishlist (resolved):** a list of desired books for the desk and the cataloguers (`loans` or `catalog`), fields title, author, series, number (at least one of title, author, series), plus a free note, the patron who asked (optional, only visible with patron access, erased with the patron) and a request counter (the same book asked again adds a request); "Cataloga" opens "Nuovo libro" prefilled and removes the wish once the record is saved; while typing, the catalogue and the list are checked for the same book (a warning, saving stays possible). Step 9.15.
- [x] **Q89 — Running without Tailscale (resolved):** a basic mode on the local network over plain HTTP is offered next to the Tailscale deployment, with limited features (no camera, no app installation, no remote access); to be documented in the installation guide. Step 8.8.
- [x] **Q90 — Configurable barcodes (resolved):** the formats of copy barcodes and card numbers become configuration, for other schools and for this library too, which will move its new books to a new format; legacy codes keep working, only new copies and cards change. Presets by default, plus a pattern editor for full configurability (fixed-width tokens, no free regex). Binding rule: the formats chosen for copies and cards must never be confusable — structurally disjoint (after the scanner repairs and against the other kind's legacy formats too) and visibly different by at least one sign: a length difference of 2 or more, a letter (check or literal) in a different position, or a different separator pattern. Phase 12, study `docs/studies/barcodes-and-school-classes.md`.
- [x] **Q91 — New formats and check characters (resolved):** copies `YYYY-CNNNN` (year, check character, zero-padded counter), cards `CNNNNNN`; the check character is a choice among Luhn, Damm and a check **letter** (mod 23 on the 23 letters without `I O Q`; the study lists the algorithms considered).
- [x] **Q92 — Default check character (resolved):** the mod-23 letter for both copies and cards — it is the strongest of the three, makes a new code impossible to confuse with a legacy all-digit code or an ISBN even structurally, and makes the card visibly different at a glance; Luhn and Damm stay available.*
- [x] **Q93 — Library code and keyboard layouts (resolved):** the `{lib}` library-code token is supported but not used by this library; scanner repair tables for the Italian, Spanish, German, French, UK and US layouts are included.
- [x] **Q94 — School structure (resolved):** class and section are no longer attributes of the patron: the **group** becomes its own object in the database (`class_group`), with the level or levels it hosts (pluriclassi are groups with several levels), and each student has their own level. Levels are an ordered configurable list with stages; infanzia is managed like the other schools; the istituto comprensivo (infanzia + primaria + secondaria I, promotion across stages) is one of the choices. Italian presets by default and ready presets for other countries. Class label `3 B` by default, configurable; the section is kept at promotion by default.
- [x] **Q95 — History and live data (resolved):** the loan history is a photo of the situation at the time and never changes (snapshots are not rewritten by renames); reports on live data follow the current names. No compatibility with the old columns while the project is in development (before 1.0).
- [x] **Q96 — Reading levels (resolved):** an independent scale (`classification.readingLevels`), not the school levels; the default proposes one level per class for primaria and none for infanzia and secondaria, editable.
- [x] **Q97 — Class in the student import (resolved, see Q115):** the class is given in one column `class` (matched against the labels of the existing classes) or in two columns `level` + `section` (resolved to a level and a group), as the operator chooses in the template (9.19).
- [x] **Q98 — When Phase 12 runs (resolved):** required for 0.9.0, carried out after Phase 11 and before the remaining steps of Phase 9 (a new phase because it is a new part of the project, not a refinement).
- [x] **Q99 — Who manages the groups (resolved):** `admin` only: creating, renaming, re-levelling and archiving groups (Amministrazione → Classi); whoever holds `patrons` only assigns patrons to existing groups. The class upgrade stays `admin`.
- [x] **Q100 — Groups as cohorts (resolved):** permanent groups: a group advances with its pupils at the class upgrade (`3 B` becomes `4 B`, the same object, which also suits the per-class notices to teachers of 10.2) and is archived when its pupils leave. Teachers need a kind of class configured in a special way: the **teacher group** (no level, never promoted, untouched by the synchronisation; details in Q102).
- [x] **Q101 — Wizard and demo data (resolved):** the school and code choices are steps of the first-run wizard, before the demo-data question; the demo data are generated from those choices (classes, reading levels, barcode formats), not copied.
- [x] **Q102 — Teacher groups in detail (resolved, the default accepted):** a group kind `teachers`; the wizard creates one named `Docenti` (from the installation's language), which new teachers get automatically; `admin` may add others (e.g. one per stage in a comprensivo, with an optional stage for the filters); every teacher belongs to exactly one teacher group; the default card keeps printing `Docente` for teachers, and the overdue notices group teachers by their teacher group after the classes.
- [x] **Q103 — When Phase 13 runs (resolved):** required for 0.9.0, after Phase 12 (it needs the class as an object) and before the remaining steps of Phase 9, so the operator guide and its illustrations cover it.
- [x] **Q104 — Where the photos come from (resolved):** four ways: single photos imported one file per patron, taken with the camera or uploaded from the patron's page, the camera census, and the bulk import for a class or list from a scanned or photographed picture with portrait frames (the photographer's sheet, cards on a table, a page of ID photos). No face detection: group photos without frames are out of scope. Owner note: the "Censimento" tab must say "Censimento foto" like its page; the survey of similar mismatches is in Phase 13 and fixed with 13.3.
- [x] **Q105 — Limits (resolved):** the defaults stand (frames with height/width between 1.1 and 1.6; pictures up to 32 MB, stored with the longest side at most 4 000 px; up to 60 frames) but every value is configurable (`photos.sheet`, editable from the photos editor). Note from 13.1: the minimum default went to 1.0, because the photos of the demo cards measure 1.10–1.18 and 1.1 lost some of them.
- [x] **Q106 — How long a class picture is kept (resolved, revised):** until the class upgrade, which deletes every class picture (or earlier, by hand; a list's pictures also go with the list); anonymizing a patron during the year paints over their frame in the picture (irreversible) and erases its photo, and the picture stays with the class (owner decision).
- [x] **Q107 — Face detection and name reading (resolved):** not now; listed among the possible future improvements (10.11).
- [x] **Q108 — Which patrons the drop-down offers (resolved):** only the patrons of the chosen class. Factored with the patron lists: the census chooses among classes **and** patron print lists, showing only those with at least one patron without a photo; the drop-down offers the members of the chosen class or list.
- [x] **Q109 — The demo picture (resolved):** `docs/reference/demo/class-cards.jpg` is shipped as `demo/census/class-cards.jpg` (the sample of a scan, with a sample of a photo made from it, Q126) and offered by "Esempio da scanner" (the photo by "Esempio da foto") only when demo data are loaded, on a class or list with a photo missing (the seeded class 2 C has none); it is also the e2e fixture and the source of the guide's illustrations.
- [x] **Q110 — Testing on several browsers (resolved):** besides the everyday Chromium runs, an opt-in mode runs the tests on Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`bun run test:browsers`); the default stays Chromium only, and the three-engine run is required before 1.0 (9.10). Q49's "Chromium only" now describes the everyday runs.
- [x] **Q111 — Cloudflare Tunnel for the school's installation (resolved, the default accepted):** could the school reach BiblioViva through a Cloudflare Tunnel instead of Tailscale? It matters because a public tunnel turns "only devices on the tailnet see the login page" (Q18, Q21) into "anyone on the internet sees it", with shared role accounts as the only defence, and because Cloudflare ends TLS and sees in clear the names, classes and photos of minors (one more data processor for the school under the GDPR; Tailscale encrypts end to end and keeps the certificate on the library's machine). Cloudflare Access (an e-mail code in front of the site, free for a few dozen users) would close the first gap but not the second. — *Decision: not offered for the school's installation; Tailscale stays the way in and the local-network mode (Q89) the way without it. Recommendation: keep the default; if a school ever needs it, document it as an alternative with Access required, never a bare public tunnel.*
- [x] **Q112 — The public demo (resolved, the defaults accepted; step 9.17, ADR-028):** a public instance with the synthetic demo data behind a Cloudflare Tunnel, reset every day. To decide: (1) when it resets; (2) what is blocked so that one visitor cannot spoil it for the others; (3) whether the metadata and cover lookups are real; (4) where it runs; (5) whether it is required for 0.9.0. — *Decision: (1) every day at 04:00 in `library.timezone`, from a golden copy of both volumes, said on the login page; (2) blocked: staff passwords and accounts, configuration import, backup restore, picture uploads (patron photos, covers, class pictures; the shipped demo picture stays) and import files over a size cap; (3) mock provider, no outbound calls; (4) an Oracle Cloud Always Free virtual machine with the `small` profile (Q119), on a domain managed by Cloudflare (`demo.biblioviva.org` when 9.7 has its domain); (5) not required for 0.9.0, done when the presentation site (9.7) can link it.*
- [x] **Q113 — Segnalazioni (resolved; step 9.18, ADR-029):** the owner asked for staff notes like post-its: anyone writes them, only the administrator archives them, and the administrator can put some at the top of the dashboard, in a section before the reports shown as tiles. To decide: (1) who may change a note once written; (2) how long a note stays on the dashboard; (3) the colour of the post-its; (4) the details left to the defaults. — *Decision (owner): (1) nobody, not even the administrator: a note is written once, a wrong one is archived and written again; (2) until the administrator takes it off the dashboard or archives it, no expiry date; (3) chosen by whoever writes it among five post-it colours (yellow by default, pink, green, blue, orange), decoration only. Defaults: every account writes and reads notes, no new permission; the archive is readable by everyone, the administrator restores and deletes archived notes; at most 500 characters, a hint against writing pupils' personal data; the pinned notes also open the checkout desk of the accounts that never see the dashboard; an administrator's dashboard has a tile with the open notes; the area has its own top-bar colour (petrol blue, 9.16).*
- [x] **Q114 — Screenshots of the app in the documentation; a static documentation site (resolved, owner; §3.23):** how the manual, the guides and the presentation site show BiblioViva's screens, and where the documentation is published. — *Decision (owner): screenshots generated from the running app with the demo data by a Playwright run (`bun run docs:shots`), never taken by hand, in lossless WebP, in every language of the site, not committed; the documentation site is static and never served by BiblioViva. Defaults applied: (1) a shot list in `docs/shots.ts` (page, window, steps, crop); (2) the three windows of Q132 (computer, tablet, phone); (3) the documentation is built by a Bun script into `site/dist/` (ADR-043), published on GitHub Pages as `docs.biblioviva.org` (Q131) (the owner: the site has a presentation and a technical part besides the user manual; the app's help icons link to it); (4) the pages of other programs as mocks drawn in the repository, never screenshots taken by hand (ADR-046); (5) the Markdown stays the source of the prose, the PDF is printed from the site; (6) one documentation, the current release's, without versions or selector (owner, ADR-042).*
- [x] **Q115 — Import columns (resolved, owner request; step 9.19):** the columns of every import file have English names, the only ones accepted (no Italian or Koha aliases; the Koha queries output the English names); the operator generates the template with the columns they choose, the required ones always included; a key left out is allowed and the wizard says what happens (cards and copy barcodes generated, copies grouped by ISBN, students matched by name and class). Students and teachers have two separate imports. Required: students `first_name`, `last_name` and the class (`class`, or `level` + `section`); teachers `first_name`, `last_name`; catalogue `title`, `author`, `publisher` (the classification may come later, Q117); loans `card_number`, `barcode` (dates optional: today and the due date of the loan rules). The template carries the header only.
- [x] **Q116 — Report editor (resolved, owner request; step 9.20):** reports are edited in the application with a guided editor (source, filters fixed or asked, rows, optional columns, measure, chart; no SQL) and an SQL view for advanced reports over the same file; a guided report can be turned into an SQL report, never the other way. The engine shows pivot tables with dynamic columns and totals (the owner's example: loans of a period by class year and section, teachers as one row counted in the total only; the empty cells of the Koha example come from wrong data in Koha and cannot happen here). Built-ins are customised (same `id`, back to the original) or duplicated like the print templates, and new ones are added. The review of the built-ins is accepted as proposed: five removed (`active-loans`, `pending-labels`, `patrons-without-card-or-photo`, `audit-summary`, `renewals-stats`), three reshaped (class × section pivots, loans by classification), four added (reading index by class, loans by school year, classification by class year, age of the collection).
- [x] **Q117 — Classification later (resolved, owner request; step 9.21):** a book may be saved without classification everywhere (import, record form, fast add) and waits in the tab "Da classificare" of the Catalogo area, with a flow that proposes the books one after another and a dashboard tile; its copies are not lent until it is classified (the desk refuses them, not forceable); the barcode label prints, the spine band and the call number wait for the classification.
- [x] **Q118 — What a visitor of the public demo may change (resolved, owner review; step 9.17, ADR-028):** the demo must show every feature without letting one visitor spoil it for the others or freeze the host. — *Decision: (1) the administration is visible and can be tried, but every write of the configuration is refused ("Salva" says why); (2) the library's data (patrons, books, loans, classes, lists, inventory) are shared by every visitor and changed freely until the nightly reset — no copy per visitor; (3) the staff notes stay writable with a daily limit per address and the length cap — what a visitor writes is replaced by an example (Q121); (4) reports written in SQL are off (no way to stop a statement that never ends), the guided reports work.*
- [x] **Q119 — Where the public demo runs (resolved, owner request; step 9.17, ADR-028):** a free host that keeps the demo online. Checked: Hugging Face Spaces (Docker Spaces need a paid plan), Fly.io (no free tier), Koyeb (free tier only for Postgres), Railway (trial credit), Google Cloud Run (card, US regions, scale to zero), Render (free, but 512 MB, 0.1 CPU, asleep after 15 minutes with a one-minute wake), Northflank (sandbox with unstated compute). — *Decision: an Oracle Cloud Always Free Arm virtual machine (2 OCPU, 12 GB), or the 1 GB AMD `VM.Standard.E2.1.Micro` with a 2 GB swap file when no Arm capacity is available, with an ephemeral public IPv4 address (free; a NAT gateway is not) and the container limited to 512 MB and 1 CPU so it fits either shape, the account on pay-as-you-go inside the free resources so the idle demo is not reclaimed, behind the Cloudflare Tunnel; the daily reset moves inside the container (the demo built afresh at every start, exit at the reset time, Docker restarts it), so the same image works on any host.*
- [x] **Q120 — The language chosen by the browser (resolved, owner decision; steps 9.17, §3.16):** the language of a request taken from a cookie (`bv_lang`, a technical cookie, no consent needed), then from the browser's preferred language (`Accept-Language`) among the available ones, then from `ui.locale`; a switch "Italiano · English" writes the cookie. The server already resolves the language per request (`resolveLocale` in `#lib/i18n/server.ts`, the `?lang=` override of the tests); the messages built on the server (the demo's refusals, exports, slips) would take the request's language instead of the installation's. Only the interface changes: the data (classes and stages, item types, titles, notes, the school preset) keep the language they were written in. In the public demo it would show the interface in the visitor's language; in a school's installation it would be a language per device, against the rule of one language per installation. — *Decision: in a school's installation the language stays the one configured in the settings, one per installation, chosen in the wizard and in Amministrazione → Aspetto e lingua, with no switch, no cookie and no browser language. The **public demo only** (`DEMO_PUBLIC=1`) follows its visitor: the cookie `bv_lang`, then the browser's preferred language among the available ones, then `ui.locale`; a switch with a globe and the codes (IT · EN, the same as on the home page and the documentation; a drop-down once there are more languages) sits on the login page and in the top bar and writes the cookie, shared on `biblioviva.org` so that the home page, the documentation and the demo keep one choice (ADR-047).*
- [x] **Q121 — What the public demo keeps of what a visitor sends (resolved, owner decision; step 9.17, ADR-035):** text and pictures written by one visitor are read by every other visitor until the nightly reset, the patrons and the photos look like a school's, and no word filter can guarantee that nothing offensive or malicious is shown; a private copy of the data per visitor would keep it away from the others. — *Decision: no private copy per visitor. Every flow can be tried to the end, but the server never keeps the content a visitor sends: it is replaced by an example, in one place in the services (`#lib/server/demo/content.ts`), so it holds for direct calls of the remote functions too. Pictures (patron photos, covers, the photos of the ZIP import, pictograms): the bytes are dropped and the demo's placeholder with the date and time drawn on it is stored. Staff notes: "DEMO – segnalazione del <data e ora>", colour and "In evidenza" as chosen. Patrons: names from the fake-name generator, email and external id emptied, notes "Nota demo del …". Records: "Libro demo del …" by "AA. VV.", the mock provider's values kept. Classes, print lists, inventories, wishes and reasons: "… demo del <data e ora>". CSV imports: only the example file of the demo, recognised by its fingerprint. Free: codes from closed lists, dates, numbers, barcodes and card numbers validated by the format table, ISBN; the call number generated from the configured format. The visitor is told in the login box and by a notification after a save that replaced something. Any visitor may archive and delete the notes of the others (they share the administrator's account), and the demo stores no visitor's address.*
- [x] **Q122 — The patron's contacts (resolved, owner decision; step 9.22):** the email of a student or teacher is the address the school gives them, for internal mail with no access from outside; it carries the notices of 10.2 and may later serve as an internal user name. — *Decision: the email is shown on the patron's page and edited in the patron form; the phone goes (database, form, exports, demo).*
- [x] **Q123 — Original title (resolved, owner decision; step 9.23, reversing that part of Q73):** the same book translated in different editions often has different titles. — *Decision: `biblio.original_title`, filled only when the book is not in its original language: from SBN 500$a when 101$c differs from 101$a, by hand otherwise; shown, edited, imported, exported and searched everywhere a title is used to find a book.*
- [x] **Q124 — Search with typing mistakes (resolved, owner decision; step 9.24):** a mistyped title or name must still find the book or the patron. — *Decision: books are searched by title, original title, author's surname, barcode and ISBN (the other fields as today), patrons by first name, last name and card number; mistakes are forgiven on titles, original titles, surnames and names, never on codes. Every word counts: when the exact search finds fewer than 3 results, the approximate ones follow, those with every word first, marked "Simile". The existing JavaScript components are surveyed first; without a clearly better one, our own code over the FTS vocabulary; no SQLite extension.*
- [x] **Q125 — Notable dates (resolved, owner decision; step 9.25):** the staff want to mark dates such as the readings in a class or the deadlines of the bookmark contest. — *Decision: a table of the database, not configuration; a title and a note in free text, one day or a period; no link to classes and no types (configurable types are a possible later development, 10.12, not for 1.0); every account reads them, accounts with `loans` write them; a dashboard section "Prossime date"; examples in the demo data.*
- [x] **Q126 — Scan and photo in the census (resolved, owner decision; step 9.26):** on a scan the frames are rectangles that may be turned by any angle but are never skewed; on a photo they are quadrilaterals deformed by the perspective as well as turned. — *Decision: the scan detection finds rectangles turned by any angle, calibrated on about ten real scans; a scan sent as a photo gives the same frames as a scan (the photo detection falls back on the scan's rectangles when its quadrilaterals find few photos); the demo offers two samples, the current picture as a scan and a photo made from it with a perspective.*
- [x] **Q127 — Cards on both sides of the sheet (resolved, owner decision; step 9.27):** the template designer stays as it is (front and back are separate faces). — *Decision: at every print the operator chooses side by side (as today, for printers without two-sided printing) or both sides with the printer's two-sided printing; the backs go at the mirrored places, moved by an offset measured with a calibration (a 150 × 100 mm sample card: cut along the outline on side 1, read the two rulers on side 2, type the two values).*
- [x] **Q128 — Margins of the label stocks (resolved, owner decision):** the label sheets sold give the size of the labels exactly; between makers only the top margin changes (usually 3–4 mm). — *Decision: the stocks stay as they are, with the top and left margins; the bottom margin is what is left, no field for it.*
- [x] **Q129 — A computer switched off at night (resolved, owner decision; step 9.33):** BiblioViva may run on the desk computer itself, switched off at the end of the day, so the nightly backup would never run. — *Decision: at start BiblioViva makes one backup when the last scheduled one was missed (on by default, `backup.catchUp`).*
- [x] **Q130 — Windows (resolved, owner decision; step 9.34, ADR-038):** must BiblioViva run on a Windows PC? — *Decision: yes, in Docker Desktop with the WSL 2 back end, the same image as on Linux, with named volumes; a PowerShell setup script beside `setup.sh`, and the Windows path in the setup guide.*
- [x] **Q131 — Where the home page and the documentation live (resolved, owner decision; §3.23, 9.7, ADR-039):** one site or two, and on which host? — *Decision: two. The home page on `biblioviva.org`, uploaded by hand to Cloudflare Pages from `home/` (not versioned, Italian and English, one short script for the scroll effects); the documentation on `docs.biblioviva.org`, published by GitHub Pages from `site/` (versioned, sections Manuale, Amministrazione, Installazione, Aiuto, Tecnica); the public demo stays on `demo.biblioviva.org` (9.17). GitHub Pages serves one domain per repository, so the home page goes to the host the demo already uses.*
- [x] **Q132 — Windows of the screenshots (resolved, owner decision; §3.23):** which sizes do the generated screenshots come in? — *Decision: three — computer 1280 × 800 at scale 1, tablet 834 × 1194 at scale 2 (an upright 11-inch tablet), phone 412 × 915 at scale 2; a figure with more than one has a Computer / Tablet / Telefono switch.*
- [x] **Q133 — The way to 1.0.0 (resolved, owner decision; 9.6, 9.10, 9.36, 9.37):** how is BiblioViva published and when does it become 1.0.0? — *Decision: BiblioViva is published from a new public repository that starts from one snapshot commit (the private repository with the history becomes `biblioviva-archivio`), and the **public beta** starts with that publication (ADR-048); 0.9.0, the complete version, is its first tagged release, for the school year 2026/27, until June 2027; 1.0.0 comes for the start of the school year 2027/28 (September 2027), when the school uses it for real; from 1.0.0 every update carries the data forward with migrations (9.37).*
- [x] **Q134 — Search in the documentation (resolved, owner decision; §3.23, 9.7):** a static site has no server to search. — *Decision: Pagefind, a static index built with the site, one per version and language, its script loaded only when the search opens, the A–Z index without JavaScript; an ADR when 9.7 adds the dependency.*
- [x] **Q135 — Photos and covers switched off (resolved, owner decision; 9.38):** may a school do without patron photos or book covers? — *Decision: yes, each with its own switch (`photos.enabled`, `covers.enabled`), on by default and asked in the wizard; off, everything about the feature disappears — pages, buttons, thumbnails, lookups — and the shipped card templates are the versions without the photo; the stored files are kept.*
- [x] **Q136 — Which language packs (resolved, owner decision; step 9.39):** which languages are published as packs? — *Decision: French, Spanish, Portuguese of Portugal (`pt-PT`) and of Brazil (`pt-BR`), German, Dutch, Catalan, Romanian, Hungarian, Czech, Slovak, Polish, Slovenian, Croatian, Greek, Swedish, Norwegian Bokmål (`nb`), Danish, Finnish, Turkish, Russian, Ukrainian, Simplified Chinese, Japanese, Korean (25 packs besides the built-in Italian and English); Arabic and Hebrew wait until the interface can be laid out right to left.*
- [x] **Q137 — A beep on a camera read (resolved, owner decision; step 9.40):** should the phone make a sound when its camera reads a code, against the silence of Q40? — *Decision: yes, a short beep generated in the browser (no audio file), only for the camera and only when a read is taken; four sounds to choose from in Amministrazione → Codici a barre (Cassa, Lettore, Conferma, Arpeggio) or none, Conferma by default. The desk itself stays silent.*
- [x] **Q138 — Class names for the countries of the language packs (resolved, owner decision; step 9.41):** can the school structure write the classes and school years of every country of the packs (Q136)? — *Decision: yes, with seven changes, all accepted: a label template per stage that may leave out the group; a `next` of a level that can be a list of choices (or leaving) for paths that split; the start and the name of the school year proposed by the preset; the card's class line as a template and the fixed Italian words of the shipped cards and slips from the dictionaries; the order of classes in the installation's language; register matching with NFKC, more ignored marks and aliases per level; preset names in every language, the language's countries first, and presets for the countries that lack one.*

---

## Appendix A — `config.example.json`

> Shown as one document for readability. On disk the configuration is a folder (§3.6, Q78): the `classification` section lives in `classification.json`, each label stock in `labels/stocks/<name>.json`, templates in `{cards,labels,slips}/templates/<name>/`; `config.json` keeps the rest.

```json
{
  "library": {
    "name": "Biblioteca dell'Istituto",
    "contactEmail": "biblioteca@scuola.example",
    "schoolYearStart": "09-01",
    "schoolYearLabel": "span",
    "timezone": "Europe/Rome", "weekStart": "monday",
    "itemTypes": [
      { "code": "libro", "label": "Libro" },
      { "code": "consultazione", "label": "Consultazione", "notForLoan": true },
      { "code": "dvd", "label": "DVD" },
      { "code": "testo", "label": "Testo scolastico" },
      { "code": "rivista", "label": "Rivista" }
    ]
  },
  "school": {
    "_comment": "Phase 12 (Q94): the levels of the school in order, grouped in stages, from the preset it-primaria (config/school-presets/). The classes themselves (1 A, 3 B, the teachers group) are data, created in Amministrazione → Classi.",
    "preset": "it-primaria",
    "stages": [{ "code": "primaria", "name": "Primaria" }],
    "levels": [
      { "code": "1", "label": "1", "name": "Prima primaria", "stage": "primaria" },
      { "code": "2", "label": "2", "name": "Seconda primaria", "stage": "primaria" },
      { "code": "3", "label": "3", "name": "Terza primaria", "stage": "primaria" },
      { "code": "4", "label": "4", "name": "Quarta primaria", "stage": "primaria" },
      { "code": "5", "label": "5", "name": "Quinta primaria", "stage": "primaria" }
    ],
    "classLabel": "{level} {group}",
    "groupWord": "Sezione",
    "cardClass": "{word} {group}",
    "promotionWithinStage": "keep",
    "promotionAcrossStages": "ask"
  },
  "catalog": {
    "_comment": "Q73: author and publisher are mandatory; these special values are offered as quick picks when there is no personal author or the publisher/place is unknown.",
    "specialAuthors": ["Anonimo", "AA. VV."],
    "unknownPublisher": "s.n.",
    "unknownPlace": "s.l."
  },
  "barcodes": {
    "_comment": "Phase 12 (Q90-Q93): new copies YYYY-CNNNN and new cards CNNNNNN with a mod-23 check letter (2026-E0267, U012345). The codes of the old system keep working: copies YYYY-NNNN, cards of 10 digits, plain numbers up to plainMaxLength digits [VERIFY at the Koha migration].",

      "symbology": "code128",
      "counterStart": "afterLegacyMax"
    }
  },
  "circulation": {
    "_commentRules": "Q13 resolved: students 15 days / 1 loan / 5 renewals, teachers 15 days / 4 loans / 5 renewals. Q68: DVDs 5 days and 1 renewal, whoever borrows them.",
    "rules": {
      "student": { "loanDays": 15, "maxLoans": 1, "maxRenewals": 5, "renewalDays": 15 },

    },

  },

      { "from": "2027-06-10", "to": "2027-09-13", "reason": "Chiusura estiva" }
    ]
  },
  "z3950": {
    "defaultTarget": "sbn",
    "timeoutMs": 15000,

  },
```
